- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:24; gpu_presolve/src/rules_dual_fixing.cpp:38-41, 49-55. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:254-262; gpu_presolve/src/hash.cpp:102-105 (the one place that reads `tol.dual`, a hash).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (`options_t::tol.dual` exists and has no reader; the designed implied equation will read it, AS-103).
- **Alternatives:** Treat an absolute cost of at most the dual tolerance as zero.
- **May change:** A column with a cost of 1e-12 and an infinite bound in the direction of the cost gives the status "unbounded or infeasible" instead of being left alone; a tiny cost decides the bound at which a column is fixed.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of the columns with a cost above 0 and at most 1e-7 in absolute value; a cluster pair only if there are any.
- **State:** in the engine (since commit `fa8430e24` also in the free singleton column on an inequality).

### AS-074 A term above 1e20 is infinite in an activity; a bound only as IEEE infinity
- **Assumption:** A product of coefficient and bound above 1e20 in absolute value is counted as an infinite term of a row activity. A bound or a side itself is infinite only when it is an IEEE infinity, so a bound of 1e20 or 1e30 that a model uses for "no bound" is a real bound for the engine. The adapter copies bounds as they are and refuses a model with NaN, a lower bound of plus infinity, an upper bound of minus infinity, non-finite coefficients or unset bound arrays.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md section 2.4: "A term with |a * bound| above 1e20 is counted as infinite"); the IEEE convention is part of the model contract.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:28, 105-108; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/state.hpp:18-22; gpu_presolve/src/state_build.cpp:15-45; cpp/src/presolve/gprs_presolve.cpp:71-82, 106-109, 121-124.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.infinite_term` (default 1e20); no switch yet for the convention on bounds.
- **Alternatives:** A lower threshold for the term (1e10 or 1e15); a threshold on the bound itself above which the adapter takes it as infinite.
- **May change:** Whether rows with huge terms give bounds, and whether a column is fixed at a huge finite bound (AS-260). The 31 have no finite bound of 1e8 or more (DESIGN.md:849-850), so only the random campaign exercises this; no such count exists for the 240.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of terms between 1e10 and 1e20 and of bounds at or above 1e20; the campaign with huge bounds through the adapter. A cluster pair `infinite_term` 1e20 against 1e10 only if reduced models differ.
- **State:** in the engine and the adapter.

### AS-075 An activity is summed again, and a candidate dropped, at a tenth of the tolerance
- **Assumption:** A stored row activity is summed from scratch when its error bound exceeds `activity_error_share` = 0.1 of the feasibility tolerance (and the side has had at least twice as many updates as the row has entries). For a column with coefficient a, no bound is derived from a row whose error bound, after one fresh summation, exceeds 0.1 * 1e-6 * |a|. The row rules and row tightening also sum again before a test when the error bound is above 1e-15. The plan says the guard itself is not measured.
- **Set by:** Design for the share (DESIGN.md sections 2.4 and 6, from the review of cuOpt pull request 2372, HISTORY Entry 5); code for the factor 2 and the threshold 1e-15.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 2.4 (lines 412-415, 452-454) and section 6 (lines 595-596); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:29-31, 121-126; gpu_presolve/src/state_ops.cpp:72-85; rules_bounds.cpp:60-74; gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:107-111; rules_propagation.cpp:162-185.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.activity_error_share` (default 0.1); the factor 2 and the product of zero and feasibility tolerance have no switch.
- **Alternatives:** Another share; sum again after every change; no guard; a guard on a proven bound (AS-261).
- **May change:** The last bits of stored activities and the tests they decide; columns with small coefficients in rows with large terms get no bound from those rows; presolve time.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run with the share at 0.01, 0.1 and 1 (reduced sizes, hashes, the correctness checks) and a count of dropped candidates, for which no counter exists. A cluster pair only if the reduced models differ.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-076 Sides, constants and fixing values above 1e8 are left alone
- **Assumption:** The limit feasibility / side_error = 1e-6 / 1e-14 = 1e8 is used in every family: no bound is propagated from a side above it, row tightening does not round a side or strengthen a coefficient when the value it would write exceeds it, two parallel rows are not merged when the constant terms of the kept row exceed it, a near-parallel subtraction is refused. The designed column rules and the five further rules take the same guard for sides, merged bounds and fixing values. Each family carries its own copy of the guard; the lesson that this "deserves one rule in the state" was not carried out. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the singleton family has its own copy of the guard (`free_side_readable`): for the pivot side and the side less the fixed terms of the free singleton column [R2, R5]; for the sides less the fixed terms and each new finite side of the zero-cost singleton rule [R3], which also leaves out a single column whose term at a finite bound is beyond that size instead of dropping the visit (a deviation of the M1 author from the design); and for the finite side of each row of a forcing column [R4], with that side less the fixed terms (an addition of the M1 author that the design does not list; AS-325 follows from it). None of these guards refuses anything on the 240, and with the guards scaled by 2 or switched off inside the family the hashes of the reduced models are identical on all 240 (a scratch build of the worth reviewer). The three wrong engines without one of the guards (fs_guard, zs_guard, fc_guard) fail unit tests only (AS-330). Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): the guards of the singleton family have a factor, `side_guard_scale`. The factors 0.01, 2 and 8 change 0 of 240 reduced models in default mode, and 0.01 and 8 also 0 in primal-only mode; the guards refuse nothing there (the counters `refused_side`, `refused_free_side`, `zero_cost_term`, `zero_cost_side` and `forcing_side` are all 0). In the campaign with a value pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models each, 0.01 changes 429 results and 8 changes 33, and none fails. The figure above for the guards "switched off" is from the scratch build and cannot be repeated: off is not an option value, because it is not valid. What the two ends of the range assume is AS-343.
- **Set by:** Design for propagation (DESIGN.md section 6: "no bound is derived when |b| * 1e-14 > feastol"); extended to the other families in repairs (HISTORY Entry 18) and in the rule designs (v2_answers.txt items 2 and 10). HISTORY Entry 54 has the side guard in its table for the owner's decisions, with the words that no reduced model changes in size; the owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:35-36; gpu_presolve/src/rules_propagation.cpp:158-160; rules_tightening.cpp:74-80, 184-189, :297; rules_parallel_rows.cpp:307-320, 438-446; state_storage.cpp:152, :183; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:27, :34, :41, :62, :69, :83; lowworth_v2_design.txt:24, :45, :52, :59. At commit `fa8430e24`, for the singleton-column rules: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:131-136, 263-265, 320-325, 345-351 and 491-507; gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:64-70 (`side_readable`). At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:295-313 (the option and its range) and 325-331 (`choices_in_range`); gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:154-159 (`free_side_readable`) and 764-768 (the family works on a copy of the tolerances); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:109-112; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 10).
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.side_error` (default 1e-14) together with `options_t::tol.feasibility` (default 1e-6); neither has a tool flag. For the singleton-column rules alone, since commit `f05d48e31`: `options_t::side_guard_scale` (default 1; a factor on the size of a readable side, from 1e-3 to 11 with the default tolerances by `choices_in_range`; tool flag `--side-guard-scale <x>`). The guards off: no switch, not built, because it is not valid (a side that holds an absorbed term of 1e20 would be read as if it still held its own value).
- **Alternatives:** A higher limit (1e10 with `side_error` 1e-16); a limit relative to the row; sides kept in double-double. For the singleton-column rules a factor on the limit: built, `side_guard_scale` (a factor below 1 only refuses reductions, a factor above 1 loosens the guard in proportion, AS-343). The guards off: not valid.
- **May change:** On models with constants above 1e8 (large big-M sides) rows give no bounds and are not tightened or merged, and columns are not fixed at such values; those rows and columns stay where HiGHS removes them.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of rows with a side above 1e8 and of reductions the limit blocks, plus the campaign with huge bounds. Then a standard cluster comparison: `side_error` 1e-14 against 1e-16, only if the count is not zero. Since commit `f05d48e31`, for the factor of the singleton-column rules (`side_guard_scale` = 1 against 0.01, 2 or 8): the hashes of the reduced models are equal on 240 of 240 as local dumps on the Mac, and the same on the node closes that pair for the benchmark set, as long as the guards refuse nothing there.
- **State:** in the engine for the Stage 1 rules and for the singleton-column rules (commit `f05d48e31`: the guards of the singleton-column rules have a factor, 1 by default); designed for the other column rules.

### AS-077 An implied bound of a continuous column counts only when it improves by over 1e-3
- **Assumption:** For a continuous column, a bound that a row implies is recorded, and later written as explicit bound, only if it improves on what is there by more than `implied_bound_step` = 1000 times the larger of the feasibility tolerance and 1e-14 times the bound, which is at least 1e-3. Integer columns have no such step.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md section 6: "when they improve the record by more than 1000 * feastol").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:32-34; gpu_presolve/src/rules_propagation.cpp:50-58, 100-120, :248, :262.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.implied_bound_step` (default 1000).
- **Alternatives:** 1 or 10 (any improvement beyond the tolerance); a step relative to the range of the column.
- **May change:** Bounds of continuous columns in the reduced model are up to 1e-3 looser than the rows imply; columns whose range is below 1e-3 are never tightened; fewer rounds.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison: `implied_bound_step` 1000 against 10.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-078 Derived bounds are moved outward by their rounding and by the activity error
- **Assumption:** A bound derived for a continuous column is moved outward by 4 rounding units of its size plus the activity error over the coefficient, unless the residual and the division are exact. An integer bound, from propagation or from a singleton row, is moved outward by 4 rounding units before it is rounded to an integer.
- **Set by:** Code (a choice made with the implementation; the reason is in the source comment: a bound that is a rounding step too tight cuts a point off). No HISTORY entry that decided it was found.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:23-30, 163-181; gpu_presolve/src/rules_propagation.cpp:98-99, 142-146; gpu_presolve/src/rules_rows.cpp:30-33, 101-106.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constants `candidate_rounding_units` = 4 and `bound_rounding_units` = 4).
- **Alternatives:** No outward move, relying on the tolerances; a larger move.
- **May change:** Bounds of continuous columns are a few rounding units looser than the row gives. A column that dual fixing later fixes at such a bound gives an optimum that is off by about 1e-9 (AS-259).
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair (1e-9 is far below cuOpt's tolerances): the campaign and the triage of the two failing seeds.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-079 One explicit bound of a continuous column is tightened at most 16 times
- **Assumption:** After the implied bounds have become explicit, bound propagation tightens the same bound of a continuous column at most `max_explicit_steps` = 16 times. A further candidate is dropped and the presolve reports that it is not at a fixed point. The source comment says "the local testbed needs 3" without naming the instance set.
- **Set by:** Code.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_propagation.cpp:44-48, 130-139.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant).
- **Alternatives:** 3; 64; no limit, with the improvement step as the only guard.
- **May change:** Chains of continuous bounds that converge slowly stop early; the fixed-point flag is lost (AS-271).
- **Test at the end:** First a count of the instances of the 240 that hit the limit. A cluster pair 16 against 64 only if some do.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-080 A combined coefficient is a structural zero below 1e-12 relative; small coefficients are not dropped
- **Assumption:** When a substitution adds two coefficients t and s*p, the entry is deleted if the absolute value of the sum is at most 1e-12 times the sum of the two absolute values; the same test zeroes a combined cost. The plan also names the removal of a small coefficient with a budget per row of 0.1 times the feasibility tolerance; no such rule is in the engine. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the same test zeroes a cost that a free singleton substitution [R2, R5] moves onto another column, so that the zero-cost singleton rule and the forcing column then see an exact zero. 163,725 columns get a cost on 43 instances; the test zeroes a sum that is not exactly zero 0 times, and no moved cost lands within 1e-9 relative of zero (scratch counters of the worth reviewer), so neither exact zero only nor a wider band changes a result there. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, default mode): the two counts are now counters of the engine (`costs_cancelled`, `costs_near_cancelled`) and are 0 on all 240, as the scratch counters were.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md section 4, lines 542-543).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:37-38; gpu_presolve/src/rules_doubleton.cpp:134-142, 146-150. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:37-38; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:219-229.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.cancellation` (default 1e-12; no tool flag); no switch yet for the removal of small coefficients, which is not built.
- **Alternatives:** Exact zero only; 1e-9; dropping small coefficients as the plan describes.
- **May change:** Whether a residue of a cancellation stays as a tiny coefficient, which then keeps a lock on a column and can stop dual fixing and is bad for LP numerics, or a real small coefficient disappears.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run with 1e-12 against 0 and the correctness checks; a cluster pair only if reduced models differ.
- **State:** in the engine (cancellation; since commit `fa8430e24` also for a cost moved by the free singleton column); removal of small coefficients planned.

### AS-081 An equation is a row whose two sides are equal bit for bit
- **Assumption:** The doubleton rule and the subtraction of parallel rows take a row as an equation only when its lower side equals its upper side exactly. A row whose sides differ by 1e-12 is an inequality pair for them. Measured with milestone M1: the singleton family reads the same test. A visit takes the free singleton column of an equation [R2], which is primal, only when the two sides of the row are equal as doubles; a row whose sides differ in the last bit is an inequality for it, where the free singleton column [R5] needs a cost and is off in primal-only mode, or a row of the zero-cost singleton rule [R3]. No count of such rows was made on the 240.
- **Set by:** Code.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_doubleton.cpp:198-199; gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:359-362. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:409-411; gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:359-362.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Sides within the feasibility tolerance count as an equation (the start step joins only sides that cross).
- **May change:** Rows whose sides differ by rounding are not used for substitution.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of rows whose sides differ by more than 0 and at most 1e-6; a cluster pair only if there are any.
- **State:** in the engine (since commit `fa8430e24` also in the singleton-column rules).

### AS-082 Sides and bounds that cross by at most 1e-6 are joined, not reported as infeasible
- **Assumption:** In the start step row sides that cross by at most the feasibility tolerance are joined at the upper side, and column bounds likewise, after which the column is fixed. A later tightening that crosses the opposite bound by at most the tolerance fixes the column at that bound; the merge of parallel rows and the singleton row do the same with sides.
- **Set by:** Design (Stage 1 code structure).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:83-117; gpu_presolve/src/rules_bounds.cpp:20-29, 38-47; rules_parallel_rows.cpp:341-353; rules_rows.cpp:107-115.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one of its own (it uses `options_t::tol.feasibility`).
- **Alternatives:** Report infeasible on any crossing; fix at the midpoint of the crossing.
- **May change:** A model that is infeasible by less than 1e-6 is treated as feasible; the value at which the column is fixed is one end of the crossing.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: a correctness test on models that are infeasible by a small margin, and the status counts of every cluster arm.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-083 Forcing rows: tolerance over dynamism; with a continuous column only up to rounding
- **Assumption:** A row is forcing when an activity meets its side within the feasibility tolerance times the smallest coefficient over the larger of 1 and the largest coefficient. When the row has a free continuous column, it is forcing only if the activity can move towards the side by no more than its error bound plus 8 rounding units of its terms.
- **Set by:** Design for the tolerance (DESIGN.md section 4: "Forcing-row tolerance is feastol / dynamism"); code for the restriction on continuous columns (a repair; the reason is in the source comment, no HISTORY entry was found).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 4 (line 531); gpu_presolve/src/rules_rows.cpp:35-38, 140-190; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:144-151.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant `forcing_rounding_units` = 8); `options_t::enabled[rule::row_rules]` turns the whole family.
- **Alternatives:** The plain feasibility tolerance; forcing within the tolerance for continuous columns as well.
- **May change:** Fewer forcing rows on rows with continuous columns: those rows and columns stay in the model.
- **Test at the end:** Needs the switch. First a size run with the relaxed test and the correctness checks; then a standard cluster comparison if reduced models differ.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-084 Rows of integer columns are scaled to coprime integer coefficients within three limits
- **Assumption:** A row whose free columns are all integer is multiplied by a factor of at most `max_integral_scale` = 1000 and divided by the gcd of its coefficients when every scaled coefficient is within 1e-9 of an integer and at most `max_integral_coefficient` = 1e12, and the rounding of the coefficients moves the row by at most `coefficient_rounding_share` = 0.1 of the tolerance over the bounds of its columns.
- **Set by:** Design for the rule; code for the three constants.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_tightening.cpp:28-35, 53-72, 108-146, 169-205.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for the scaling alone; `options_t::enabled[rule::tightening]` (tool `--off tightening`) turns the whole family.
- **Alternatives:** No scaling; a larger scale limit; scaling without the division by the gcd.
- **May change:** The scale of all-integer rows changes (times up to 1000, or divided by the gcd): an absolute row tolerance of the solver then means something else in that row, and the LP sees other row norms. It is the precondition of the side rounding.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison: row tightening on against off; with the sub-switch, scaling alone off.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-085 Bounds moved to an integer column through a doubleton equation round with tolerance over scale
- **Assumption:** When the bounds of a substituted column become bounds of an integer kept column, they are rounded with the slack feasibility / max(1, |scale|) plus 4 rounding units. When the kept column is fixed afterwards, the substituted column is fixed at its image, moved into its own bounds if rounding put it outside.
- **Set by:** Code (named constant `transfer_error_units` = 4).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_doubleton.cpp:38-40, 66-102, 228-234.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** The general integer rounding of the engine (tolerance 1e-6 whatever the scale).
- **May change:** One unit of an integer bound of the kept column.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the campaign and the crush check of known solutions.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-086 Rows and columns are compared with at most 4 bucket neighbours in each direction
- **Assumption:** Inside one hash bucket of the parallel-row search a row is compared with at most `parallel_bucket_limit` = 4 rows above it and 4 below it; rows beyond that are not compared and the call reports "limited". The designed parallel-column rules reuse the option: 4 neighbours above and below, and at most 4 absorbers for a member of a bucket of singleton columns.
- **Set by:** Code for the rows (from the Stage 1 code structure, HISTORY Entry 14); design for the reuse (v2_design.txt:62, :65).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:156-157; gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:830-871; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:279-284; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:62, :65.
- **Switch:** `options_t::parallel_bucket_limit` (default 4).
- **Alternatives:** A higher limit (64); no limit, with the work budget as the only guard.
- **May change:** Parallel rows or columns in large buckets are missed and the fixed-point flag is lost.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run with 4 against 64 (rows, nonzeros, seconds) and the number of instances where a family reports "limited"; a cluster pair only if reduced models differ.
- **State:** in the engine for rows; designed for columns (milestone of dominance between parallel columns [M3]).

### AS-087 The subtraction of parallel rows is made only inside five guards
- **Assumption:** The subtraction is made only with a factor of at most 1 in size (exactly 1 when the equation has a singleton column), when the error growth factors are at most `max_growth` = 1e3, when no value involved exceeds 1e8, when the ratio of largest to smallest coefficient of the changed row does not rise above `max_dynamism` = 1e6 and above its old value, and, where an error would grow, only on a row that the presolve has not changed before. Of two equations the one with fewer singleton columns, then the smaller index, is subtracted.
- **Set by:** Design (made in the repairs of the rule, HISTORY Entries 18 to 31; Entry 23: "the repair takes only factors up to 1 in size"; the 1e6 follows the plan's veto for substitutions).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:364-381, 410-479, 811-814; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:308-338.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constants `max_growth` and `max_dynamism`); `options_t::near_parallel_rows` turns the whole step.
- **Alternatives:** Looser limits (factors above 1 with a wider error analysis, growth 1e6); no test on the history of the row.
- **May change:** Pairs that are left alone keep their nonzeros; a pair left because of the row's history costs the fixed-point flag.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run with relaxed guards and the correctness checks; a cluster pair only if the relaxed guards pass the checks and change reduced models.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-088 Where the column leaves the model, a bound is implied within about 2e-7
- **Assumption:** For the free singleton rules a bound of a column counts as implied when the bound that one row gives, recomputed at the test and moved outward by its rounding, is within `implied_tolerance` = 2 * (4 rounding units of the bound + 0.1 * feasibility tolerance), about 2e-7 for small bounds. Both ends of the column's range are tested. The shared rule since the design of the five further rules: a tolerance band is allowed only when the column then leaves the model; where bound and row both stay, only rounding is allowed. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the band decides nothing there: with the band scaled by 2 and by 1000 the hashes of the reduced models are identical on all 240 (a scratch build of the worth reviewer). In the campaign a band of 1e-3 (the wrong engine fs_band) gives 30 oracle and 34 structure failures in 1,440,000 models. The factor 2 and the 4 rounding units are bare constants of the groundwork. The band stacks with the growth limit: a restored value can pass its bound by the band and by up to 1000 times what the reduced point is off (AS-094), which the validity reviewer lists as remaining by design and as a matter of the owner's end test. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): an option for the band was built and removed again (AS-341). Its values 2 and 5 changed 0 of 240 reduced models in default and in primal-only mode (runs of the implementer, deleted with the option). Without any band (a scratch binary of the implementer, not rerun by the reviewer) 1 of 240 changes: rocII-5-11 from 134,280 / 12,860 / 5,282 to 134,288 / 12,864 / 5,286 in nonzeros / rows / columns, by 4 substitutions fewer of the 15,902 on the 240. The end test below (band against rounding only) therefore still has no switch. The reviewer of the switches showed that the stacking named above is a wrong result of the engine at the defaults, at a reduced point that is exactly feasible: 1.5e-4 outside a bound on a model (AS-353).
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:34, :99; the shared rule is the lesson of HISTORY Entry 43 and of v2_amendments.txt:92-121). HISTORY Entry 54 records that the option for the band was removed, with the model, and the defect at the defaults (AS-353); that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has not decided.

At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:23-40 and 163-181; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:163-185 (`implied_free`; the two tests are lines 174 and 180); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:88-92 (counter `free_singleton_rejected_explicit`) and 438-442. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:283-293 (why there is no option, with the model); gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:207-232 (`implied_free`; the two tests are lines 220 and 227); gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:32-40 (`implied_tolerance`); gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches_review.cpp:84-111 (the model at the band of the rule); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results", item 2, and "switches", row 9).
- **Switch:** no switch: not built. The option that the implementer of the switches built (`implied_band_scale`, a factor of 1 to 5 on the band, flag `--implied-band-scale`) was removed by the reviewer, because every value above 1 gives a wrong result on a model where the band of the rule does not: x + 1000 y + w = 500 and y + 0.5 z + 0.5 v = 1 with y in [9.5e-7, 1], x in [-501, 499.99905], w in [0, 1] with cost 1 and binaries z and v with cost -1; with five times the band the postsolved point has x = 500, which is 9.5e-4 above its bound (AS-341). A narrower band (rounding only) is right for every result and breaks the fixed point: the band is what two candidates of one bound can differ by, so a bound that is the candidate of the row itself could fail the test in one presolve and pass it in the next; it needs a fresh sum in every test or a floor of its own, a new mechanism. The band stays a function of `options_t::tol.activity_error_share` (default 0.1) and `options_t::tol.feasibility` (default 1e-6); its factor 2 and the named constant `candidate_rounding_units` = 4 (gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:25 and :39) have no switch.
- **Alternatives:** Rounding only: right for every result, breaks the fixed point, not built. A wider band: not valid (the model in the Switch line). The band divided by the growth factor of the column: the repair that the reviewer of the switches names for AS-353, not built. HiGHS's absolute tolerances; bounds implied through several rows (AS-023).
- **May change:** Which singleton columns count as free and are substituted; a restored value may pass its bound by the band; a band that is too wide removes a bound that was needed.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of the decisions that the band changes, and the campaign. A standard cluster comparison (band against rounding only) needs the switch.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`, unchanged) for the free singleton column [R2, R5]; bound propagation uses the same helper. No switch is built: a wider band is not valid and a narrower one needs a new mechanism.

### AS-089 For dominance between parallel columns the absorber's bound is implied up to rounding only
- **Assumption:** For the dominance fixing between parallel columns [R6] the bound of the absorbing column counts as implied only up to rounding, not within the band of AS-088, because bound and row both stay in the model. The first row of the absorber that implies the bound is taken.

### AS-090 Implied equation and locked compensator: a bound is implied up to rounding, with a cap
- **Assumption:** For the implied equation, and for a locked compensator of the dominated-column rule, a finite bound counts as implied by its row only when the row's candidate, moved inward by its own rounding slack (4 rounding units of its size plus the activity error), reaches the bound; no share of the feasibility tolerance is allowed. An amendment adds the cap of the forcing row: coefficient times the allowed excess is at most feasibility times the smallest coefficient over the larger of 1 and the largest coefficient of the row.
- **Set by:** Design (decision 2 of lowworth_v2_design.txt:239, replacing the plan's "strictly implied" and HiGHS's 1e-7; the cap is a binding amendment, lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:17-18; no owner answer in HISTORY up to Entry 46).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:24, :81, :223, :239; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:17-18; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:22-25.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; the designed statistic `ie_blocked_bound` counts what the test refuses.
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's 1e-7 for singleton columns; the plan's strict margin for columns with more than one entry; the first design's band of about 2e-7, which lost an optimum in the critic's model (-0.99999985 became -0.95).
- **May change:** On instances with non-integer data the engine can make fewer implied equations and fewer dominated-column fixings than HiGHS.
- **Test at the end:** First a size table (milestones of the implied equation [L1] and of the dominated column [L5] report `ie_blocked_bound` per instance on the 240). A cluster pair only after a looser test is built behind a switch.
- **State:** designed (milestones of the implied equation [L1] and of the dominated column [L5]).

- **Assumption:** Simple probing on an equation [R1] accepts a row when both conditional rows are forcing by the engine's test (AS-083): within tolerance over dynamism, and with a continuous column in the row only a gap of rounding size.

### AS-092 A column is replaced by a binary only when its range is at most 1e6
- **Assumption:** Simple probing [R1] and the single-lock alias [R8] replace a column by a binary only when its range upper minus lower is at most `alias_scale_limit` = 1e6; the range multiplies every coefficient of the column into the rows of the binary. The design names this the only guard of those substitutions, and the free singleton rules move cost with only the cancellation rule: there is no limit on the coefficient range of a resulting row or of the objective, unlike the plan's veto for aggregator substitutions (a ratio above the larger of the old one and 1e6, AS-110). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): for the free singleton rules, which are built, the missing limit shows in the costs and not in the matrix: the ratio of the largest to the smallest absolute cost grows on 32 instances, and the coefficient ranges of the matrix grow on none (a scratch tool of the worth reviewer; AS-324). Simple probing and the single-lock alias are not built.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:27, :76, :115, :269; a named constant, not an option).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:27, :76, :115, :269; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:391-394; DESIGN.md section 4 (lines 540-541).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant `alias_scale_limit`).
- **Alternatives:** A smaller limit such as 1e3 or 1e4; a veto when a row's ratio of largest to smallest coefficient exceeds the larger of its old value and 1e6; no limit.
- **May change:** Coefficients of up to 1e6 times the old ones enter the rows of a binary: a weaker and numerically harder formulation of big-M kind, against one column less; badly scaled rows or costs for cuOpt's LP, more so with scaling off.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison after the milestones of simple probing [M2] and of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]: 1e6 against 1e3 (two benchmark commits that differ in the constant), plus the count of aliases with a range above 1e3 and the coefficient range of the reduced rows and costs per instance on the 240. A second pair once a veto on the coefficient range exists with a switch.
- **State:** designed (milestones of simple probing [M2] and of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]); the constant is in the engine and no rule reads it yet.

### AS-093 One shared integrality test: ratios within 1e-9 and a drift guard of 0.1 tolerance
- **Assumption:** A row is "integral for" a column when every other free column is integer, each ratio and the side over the coefficient is within 1e-9 of an integer, and the drift of the rounded ratios over the bounds of the other columns is at most 0.1 times the feasibility tolerance. For a column that stays in the model (conversion, scaling) the drift is measured in the units of that column; row units are enough where the column leaves or moves only in its free direction (free singleton rules for an integer column [R2, R5], integer source of the implied equation). Otherwise the column is left. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the test is built as `integral_for` with `drift_within` and is used by the free singleton column of integer type [R2, R5] and by the edge of the alias forest between two integer columns; row tightening keeps its own constant and its own copy of the arithmetic. The drift guard refuses 0 columns, and the shares 0, 0.2 and infinity give the default sizes on every instance (infinity: identical hashes on all 240). A ratio that is not an integer exactly, on a column with an infinite bound, gives an infinite drift (the drift of a column is the deviation of its ratio times the larger of its two absolute bounds), so such a row is never integral for the column whatever the share is. Since the repair of the M1 validity reviewer the deviation of the side and the drift together must stay within the feasibility tolerance (AS-322), so a share above 1 admits no more than the tolerance. What the integrality condition itself refuses is in AS-025.
- **Set by:** Design (shared function `integral_for`, binding by v2_amendments.txt:97-101 and lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:11-14, after the validity review found that the first form loses an optimum for a coefficient below 0.1: -9989.999999995 became -9989.5).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:34; v2_amendments.txt:97-101; lowworth_v2_design.txt:24, :81, :152; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:11-14; the guard to copy is gpu_presolve/src/rules_tightening.cpp:35, 126-145. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/integral.hpp:24-39 (the form), 41-53 (`integral_term`), 55-64 (`integral_side`), 66-88 (`drift_within`) and 90-109 (`integral_for`); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:39-44; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:275-282; gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:50-63; the copy of row tightening is gpu_presolve/src/rules_tightening.cpp:35 and 126-145.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.integral_drift_share` (default 0.1; tool `--integral-drift-share <x>`) with `options_t::tol.zero` (default 1e-9) and `options_t::tol.feasibility` (default 1e-6); row tightening keeps the named constant `coefficient_rounding_share` = 0.1. The designed statistic `ii_refused_drift` is not in code.
- **Alternatives:** No drift guard (a ratio test only, which loses an optimum in a fixed test of the design: -1499999 becomes about -2000); another share; the integrality tolerance 1e-5; leave every integer singleton column.
- **May change:** How many integer columns are removed by substitution and how many columns are converted, scaled or accepted as integer sources on models with non-integer coefficients.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: a correctness guard, tested by two fixed models of the design in both modes and by two wrong engines. Quality is covered by the pairs of the rules that use it.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`) for the free singleton column of integer type and for the alias forest; binding for the implied equation [L1] and the implied-integer conversion [L2].

### AS-094 A chain of free singleton substitutions may grow a deviation by at most 1000
- **Assumption:** A free singleton column is substituted only when the composed growth factor of its chain (the largest coefficient of its pivot row over its own, times the factor earlier substitutions gave the column) is at most `pivot_growth_limit` = 1000. The undo never clamps, so a restored value can pass its bound by up to 1000 times what the reduced point is off: 1e-3 at a point that uses the full tolerance. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only; geometric mean of the ladder step `m1_a` over the engine over 237 of the 240 in nonzeros / rows / columns, and in brackets the instances behind `m1_a` in a measure; copied from the report of the worth reviewer, the default line and the refusals recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac/default): limit 1: 1.0013 / 1.0009 / 1.0013 (20); 100: 1.0017 / 1.0009 / 1.0014 (14); 500: 1.0019 / 1.0020 / 1.0020 (10); 1000, the default: 1.0020 / 1.0022 / 1.0022 (9); 2000: 1.0024 / 1.0026 / 1.0026 (4: irish-electricity, rococoC10-001000, piperout-08 by a row, traininstance2); 1e4: 1.0025 / 1.0029 / 1.0028 (2); 1e6 and no limit: 1.0026 / 1.0029 / 1.0030 (1, traininstance2, which is not a matter of this rule, AS-233). At 1000 the limit refuses 5,270 columns on 9 instances by the ratio of their pivot row (irish-electricity 5,187, rococoB10-011000 37, rococoC10-001000 36, supportcase7 3, physiciansched3-3 2, rocI-4-11 2, physiciansched6-2, radiationm40-10-02 and piperout-08 1 each) and none by the factor of earlier substitutions; at 2000 it refuses 3,317 columns on 4 instances. It is the cause for which eight of the nine instances behind `m1_a` are behind, among them irish-electricity, the one instance below 0.95 (0.957 / 0.935 / 0.894; without the limit 412,803 nonzeros, 71,555 rows and 43,092 columns, ahead of the reference). HiGHS has no such limit, and with the free column substitution of HiGHS switched off as well (rule bit 8 on top of the switch set of `m1_a`, a local tool of the worth reviewer) HiGHS ends at or above the sizes of the engine on all nine instances, so the whole difference lies in that one rule. Raising the limit makes no instance larger. The largest factor accepted is 1000 at the default (physiciansched6-2; the factor is above 100 on 12 instances and above 1 on 21, recounted) and, without the limit, 1e6 (rocI-4-11), 24,704 (irish-electricity) and 8,601 (piperout-08). In primal-only mode no limit gives 1.0004 / 1.0005 / 1.0003 over the default limit, 9 instances smaller. The stacking that remains by design, as the validity reviewer puts it: the implied-bound band (AS-088, about 2e-7) and the limit of 1000 let a restored value pass its bound by up to 1e-3 at a reduced point that uses the full tolerance. What the limit does to postsolved feasibility and to solution quality was not measured. What the ratio reads is AS-318. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, default mode, presolve only; geometric mean of the default size over the size with the value over 237 of the 240; no instance larger in any run, 234 fixed-point flags in all): the limit at 2000 gives 1.0003 / 1.0004 / 1.0004 in nonzeros / rows / columns, 7 instances change, and `m1_a` over the engine is 1.0024 / 1.0026 / 1.0026 (irish-electricity to 423,927 / 74,829 / 46,366; physiciansched3-3 from 377,879 / 69,768 / 17,870 to 371,068 / 69,327 / 17,428); no limit gives 1.0005 / 1.0007 / 1.0007, 9 instances, `m1_a` 1.0026 / 1.0029 / 1.0030 (irish-electricity to 412,803 / 71,555 / 43,092). These repeat the figures above from stored runs of the repository (`pivot_growth_limit_2000` and `pivot_growth_limit_off` under gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_switches). At 1000 the limit refuses 7,709 visits, which are 5,270 columns on 9 instances (counter `refused_growth_row`), and the factor of earlier substitutions refuses none (`refused_growth_history` 0). The stacking of the limit with the implied-bound band is a wrong result of the engine at the defaults, at a reduced point that is exactly feasible (1.5e-4 outside a bound on a model of the reviewer of the switches): AS-353. What the ratio reads now has an option (AS-318).
- **Set by:** Design (repair after the validity critique, v2_answers.txt item 6). HISTORY Entry 48 reports it to the owner as open ("a numerical guard of the design that costs irish-electricity and seven smaller cases against HiGHS"); the owner has not decided. HISTORY Entry 54 has the figures of this option in its table for the owner's decisions; that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has not decided.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:34-36, :269; v2_answers.txt:23-26; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:134-136; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:73-75. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:283-298; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:134-138; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:73-81 and 443-446; gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_presolve.cpp:181-182. - **Switch:** `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` (default 1000; tool `--pivot-growth-limit <x>`, where `inf` is no limit). What the ratio reads is `options_t::growth_ratio` (AS-318).
- **Alternatives:** 10 or 100 (the plan's general pivot rule is 0.01 of the row maximum); 1e6; clamping in the undo.
- **May change:** A lower limit leaves more columns and rows; a higher one lets the postsolve multiply a deviation of the reduced solution by more, so restored values can violate the original model, which cuOpt tests (AS-168).

Since commit `f05d48e31` the stored runs of the switches hold two more values of `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` on the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, 2000 and no limit (7 and 9 instances differ from the default); a pair of 1000 against one of them is read on those instances, and each arm sets the limit as the default of a benchmark commit (AS-152).

### AS-095 A free singleton substitution has no limit on the cost entries it creates
- **Assumption:** A free singleton substitution [R2, R5] deletes its pivot row and gives up to row length minus 1 columns a nonzero cost. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): 163,725 columns get a cost on 43 instances (most on supportcase7, 21,268); the objective nonzeros of the reduced models go from 5,910,656 without the family to 6,045,164 with it, next to 6,376,851 in the input models (recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac), more on 32 instances and fewer on 12. On 13 instances, by the count of the worth reviewer, a free singleton column on an objective-defining row turns an objective of one nonzero into a dense one (blp-ar98 1 to 15,806, blp-ic98 1 to 13,550, leo2 1 to 11,099, triptim1 1 to 10,898, germanrr 1 to 10,539); in the stored run 13 instances go from one objective nonzero to more than 1,000. The coefficient ranges of the matrix grow on no instance. Nothing limits the fill; the size of the created costs is AS-324.

- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for a limit; `options_t::sub_enabled[sub_rule::free_singleton]` and `[sub_rule::free_singleton_dual]` (tool `--off free_singleton`, `--off free_singleton_dual`) turn the rules.
- **Alternatives:** Refuse rows above a length or a cost-fill limit.
- **May change:** A dense objective in place of a long row (in HiGHS, mcsched, swath1, swath3 and neos-3627168-kasai each lose one to three long rows this way): objective-driven heuristics and the LP see many more cost entries.

- **Assumption:** The records of the singleton-column rules may save entries of rows that stay in the model up to `saved_row_factor` = 4 times the nonzeros of the input model; beyond that such a row is left and the family reports "limited". As built, the limit counts only entries of rows that stay in the model after a visit of the zero-cost singleton rule [R3], over the whole presolve. The 40 were not run.

### AS-098 Two columns are parallel within 1e-9 relative and 0.1 of the tolerance over the bounds
- **Assumption:** Two columns are parallel when they have the same live rows and one ratio s such that, in every row, the two coefficients differ from proportionality by at most 1e-9 relative, and that difference times the largest finite bound of the fixed column is at most 0.1 of the feasibility tolerance.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:62).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:62; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:25, :31.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.zero`, `options_t::tol.activity_error_share`, `options_t::tol.feasibility` (shared with other tests).
- **Alternatives:** Exact proportionality; HiGHS's tolerances.
- **May change:** The number of pairs found on models with rounded data.
- **Test at the end:** First a count of the pairs that pass only through the tolerance on the 240; no quality pair expected.
- **State:** designed (milestone of dominance between parallel columns [M3]).

### AS-099 Costs of parallel columns count as equal only to 1e-12 relative
- **Assumption:** The cost difference of a parallel pair counts as zero when it is at most 1e-12 times the sum of the two cost terms; the dominance direction uses the exact sign otherwise, and a merge needs a zero difference. HiGHS accepts a difference up to its dual tolerance.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:62-63, :83-84; named among the weaker forms in :261; chosen so that the objective identity of the checks holds).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:62-63, :83-84, :261.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.cancellation` (default 1e-12; shared with the structural-zero test).
- **Alternatives:** The dual tolerance 1e-7 as in HiGHS (the checks then need an exception).
- **May change:** Fewer merges and fewer two-way dominance cases on models whose costs agree only approximately.
- **Test at the end:** First a count on the 240 of parallel pairs with a cost difference between 1e-12 relative and 1e-7. A count of zero closes the entry for the benchmark set; otherwise the looser tolerance behind a switch and a standard cluster comparison, read on the instances whose reduced model differs.
- **State:** designed (milestones of dominance between parallel columns [M3] and of the merge of parallel columns [M5]).

### AS-100 Dual bound strengthening rounds to the safe side and needs an improvement above the tolerance
- **Assumption:** In dual bound strengthening a row's value is moved to the safe side by the candidate's rounding and, for an integer column, rounded as ceil(w - feasibility / max(1, |a|)); the new bound is taken only when it improves the old one by more than the feasibility tolerance. The attempt ends without a result at the first locking row whose rest is infinite or fails a guard.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:69-70; the integer rounding follows HiGHS).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:69-70.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one of its own (it uses `options_t::tol.feasibility`).
- **Alternatives:** Skip a row without a bound only when it does not lock; a relative improvement threshold.
- **May change:** The number of bound changes.
- **Test at the end:** Covered by the pair `dual_bound` on against off (AS-050).
- **State:** designed (milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]).

### AS-101 The single-lock alias forces a continuous column only up to rounding
- **Assumption:** For a continuous column the force test of the single-lock alias allows a gap of rounding size only (the form of the forcing rule), not the feasibility tolerance divided by the column's largest coefficient as version 2 of the design wrote. HiGHS is as loose as version 2.
- **Set by:** Design (a binding amendment after the validity review, v2_amendments.txt:61-62, with a model on which two aliases add up and the optimum goes from 9.9999982 to 100).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:61-62; v2_reviews.txt:38-41.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** The feasibility tolerance, as HiGHS.
- **May change:** Fewer aliases of continuous columns on rows that are tight only within tolerance.
- **Test at the end:** A correctness choice (a fixed oracle case with a wrong engine); a count of the aliases lost on the 240.
- **State:** designed (milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]).

### AS-102 The single-lock alias has no limit on the length of its lock row
- **Assumption:** `lock_alias_row_limit` = 0 means no limit on the free entries of the row through which a column is aliased. HiGHS leaves rows with more than 1000 entries; the design says that cap is not needed for the cost.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:79, :115).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:79, :115; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:138-139.
- **Switch:** `options_t::lock_alias_row_limit` (default 0, no limit).
- **Alternatives:** 1000, as HiGHS.
- **May change:** Aliases through long rows: more columns removed, many entries put into the rows of one binary.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison after the milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]: 0 against 1000, after a size run has shown that reduced models differ.
- **State:** designed (milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]); the option is in the engine and no rule reads it yet.

### AS-103 An implied-equation source needs a single lock and a cost beyond the dual tolerance
- **Assumption:** A column is a source only if its lock count in the cost direction is exactly 1, the lock row is not already an equation, and its absolute cost exceeds the dual tolerance 1e-7 times its coefficient. A zero cost, a cost inside the tolerance or two locks are not taken.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:24, HiGHS's strict test taken over).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:24; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:24.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.dual` (default 1e-7); no switch yet for the lock count.
- **Alternatives:** Accept zero-cost columns (weak form); accept two locks with further tests; another dual tolerance.
- **May change:** How many rows become equations.
- **Test at the end:** Covered by the pair of AS-063; a tolerance sweep would be a size run on the 240 first.
- **State:** designed (milestone of the implied equation [L1]).

### AS-104 A conversion rounds bounds inward and is refused where rounding would widen a bound
- **Assumption:** At an implied-integer conversion both bounds are set to their integer-rounded values. If a finite bound lies within the tolerance inside an integer without being equal to it, the rounded value would be wider and the conversion is refused. The weak test requires bounds that are exactly integers or infinite.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_answers.txt:82 and :22, the worth critic's second option).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:31, :130; lowworth_v2_answers.txt:22, :82; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:129-137.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; the designed statistic `ii_refused_bound` counts refusals.
- **Alternatives:** Widen the bound to the rounded value; tighten to the next integer inside.
- **May change:** Columns with bounds such as [1e-7, 6] are not converted; a few conversions that HiGHS makes may be missing.
- **Test at the end:** Not a cluster pair; the milestone of the implied-integer conversion [L2] reports `ii_refused_bound` per instance on the 240 and the 40.
- **State:** designed (milestone of the implied-integer conversion [L2]).

### AS-105 The column scaling works inside fixed limits
- **Assumption:** The integral scale of the row is at most 1000; the column factor must lie between 1/1024 and 1024 (`col_scale_limit`); every scaled coefficient and the scaled cost must be finite, nonzero and at most 1e12; a scaled bound within 4 rounding units of an integer is snapped to it (the only place where a bound may widen); any other bound that integer rounding would widen refuses the scaling. The proof is made on exactly the numbers the state will store.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:38, :70; the last sentence is a binding amendment, lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:19).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:38-39, :70; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:19; gpu_presolve/src/rules_tightening.cpp:29-32.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constants `col_scale_limit` = 1024 and `max_integral_scale` = 1000).
- **Alternatives:** Other limits; HiGHS's own limits (not quoted in the design).
- **May change:** Which columns are scaled, and how far the coefficient range of a scaled column moves (up to a factor of 1024).
- **Test at the end:** Part of the pair of AS-066; the milestone of the implied-integer conversion [L2] reports scalings per instance.
- **State:** designed (milestone of the implied-integer conversion [L2], second step).

### AS-106 The sums of column stuffing and the single-equation rule allow rounding only
- **Assumption:** The tests that decide a stuffing fixing and a single-equation fixing accept an excess of the activity over the side only up to the error bound of the sum plus 8 rounding units of the terms, and at most feasibility times the smallest coefficient over the larger of 1 and the largest. HiGHS accepts 1e-6 in both rules.
- **Set by:** Design (part of decision 8 of lowworth_v2_design.txt:245; the cap is a binding amendment, lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:17-18; no owner answer in HISTORY up to Entry 46).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:45-46, :52-53, :223, :245; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:17-18; gpu_presolve/src/rules_rows.cpp:38, 175-179.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant `forcing_rounding_units` = 8).
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's 1e-6; the feasibility tolerance.
- **May change:** Fewer fixings than HiGHS on rows whose slack lies between rounding and 1e-6; the reduced model never needs the tolerance to stay feasible.
- **Test at the end:** First a size table (milestones of column stuffing [L3] and of the single-equation extension [L4] report fixings next to HiGHS's counters). A cluster pair needs a looser test behind a switch.
- **State:** designed (milestones of column stuffing [L3] and of the single-equation extension [L4]).

### AS-107 The dominated-column rule limits prices to 1e7 and coefficient ratios to 1000
- **Assumption:** A compensator's price (cost over coefficient) is admissible only when finite and at most `price_limit` = 1e7 in absolute value, which is HiGHS's limit, and the ratio of the candidate's coefficient to the compensator's must be at most `comp_ratio_limit` = 1000.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:59, :70).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:59, :70.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constants).
- **Alternatives:** Other limits; no ratio limit.
- **May change:** Fixings with large prices or badly scaled compensations are not made; the ratio limit bounds how far a compensator moves in postsolve and crush.
- **Test at the end:** No cluster pair; the milestone of the dominated column [L5] reports fixings per instance against HiGHS's counters on the 240 and the 40.
- **State:** designed (milestone of the dominated column [L5]).

### AS-108 The sign of the dominated-column rate is tested to 1e-12 relative, with a capped rise
- **Assumption:** The rate of a column is the change of the objective per unit that the column moves towards the bound: its own cost in that direction less the price of the compensating columns in its locking rows. The column is fixed when its rate is at least -1e-12 times the size of its cost terms (HiGHS: 1e-7). A negative rate is accepted only when the other bound of the column is finite and the rise of the objective is within the identity tolerance of the checks; with an infinite other bound the rate must be at least 0.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:59-60; part of decision 8; the cap is a binding amendment, lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:21-23).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:59-60, :245; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:21-23; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:37-40; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:38.
- **Switch:** `options_t::tol.cancellation` (default 1e-12); no switch yet for the cap.
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's 1e-7; an exact sign test.
- **May change:** Columns whose cost equals the compensation cost up to rounding are fixed; a looser tolerance would fix more and could raise the objective of a feasible point.
- **Test at the end:** No cluster pair; the size table of the milestone of the dominated column [L5]. Loosening needs its own fixed test first.
- **State:** designed (milestone of the dominated column [L5]).

### AS-109 The five rules' dual tolerances stay stricter than HiGHS until their price is measured
- **Assumption:** The design keeps tolerances "so that the checks need no exception and a reduced model never needs the tolerance to stay feasible": rounding only in the implied test and in the sums of stuffing and the single equation (HiGHS 1e-7 and 1e-6), the sign of the reduced-cost bound exact to 1e-12 relative (HiGHS 1e-7), no status from an infinite target. They stay until the table of the milestone of the dominated column [L5] shows per instance what they cost against HiGHS.
- **Set by:** Design (decision 8 of lowworth_v2_design.txt:245; no owner answer in HISTORY up to Entry 46).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:223, :245.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one per tolerance (the design: each "can then be loosened with its own fixed test").
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's tolerances, paid with exceptions in the oracle and the crush check.
- **May change:** Fewer reductions than HiGHS on instances with non-integer data, against a reduced model that is feasible without leaning on a tolerance.
- **Test at the end:** First the per-instance table of the milestone of the dominated column [L5] on the 240 and the 40. A standard cluster comparison only after a looser variant is built behind a switch.
- **State:** designed (milestones of the five further rules [L1 to L5]).

### AS-110 Aggregator substitutions: pivot at least 0.01, fill-in at most 10, coefficient ratio at most 1e6
- **Assumption:** For substitutions a row or column of size 2 is always taken; otherwise the pivot must be at least 0.01 of the largest entry of its row or column and the net fill-in at most 10; all candidates are tested; a pivot is vetoed when a resulting row would have a ratio of largest to smallest coefficient above the larger of its old value and 1e6. The numbers are not tied to a measurement in the plan.
- **Set by:** Design (guards from the reviews, DESIGN.md section 4).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 4 (lines 535-541).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (the aggregator is not built).
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's limits (its aggregator pass ends after three fill-in failures); other thresholds.
- **May change:** Fill-in trades rows and columns for nonzeros and denser rows.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison when built: aggregator off against on, and fill-in limit 0 against 10.
- **State:** planned (step 2 of the build order).

### AS-111 Probing is limited by work in entries, a stall stop and caps per pass
- **Assumption:** The probing work budget counts matrix entries touched per nonzero of the model, not probes; a pass ends when work passes without a finding; new cliques and new implications are capped per pass, and reaching a cap does not end probing. The sizes are open. Basis on the 31: HiGHS probes 2.0% of its first-round candidates; probing all leaves 1.8% fewer nonzeros in the geometric mean at 15 times the probing time.
- **Set by:** The owner for the principle (change 5 of the Stage 0 report, accepted with decision 6, HISTORY Entry 13); the sizes are open.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 7 (lines 634-648) and section 13 (lines 809-811); gpu_presolve/results/stage0/STAGE0_REPORT.md section 13 (lines 626-656).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; on the HiGHS side `HIGHS_DIAG_PROBING_NOSTOP=1`.
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's limits counted in probes (it ends probing on its clique limit on two of the 31).
- **May change:** How many candidates are probed, and with them the fixings, implications and cliques the solver gets, whose value is not in the nonzero count.
- **Test at the end:** Before it is built: cuOpt with its presolver set to none on the 240 as presolved by HiGHS, `probing` on against off, and HiGHS's probing limits on against off (`HIGHS_DIAG_PROBING_NOSTOP=1` against unset; the family pairs of AS-113). When built: standard cluster comparison, the default budget against one large enough for every candidate.
- **State:** planned (step 4 of the build order).

### AS-318 What the growth ratio reads, and how the growth factor is carried
- **Assumption:** The growth limit of the free singleton column (AS-094) is tested on a ratio that reads the largest absolute coefficient among all other free entries of the pivot row, whatever the type of their columns, over the coefficient of the column. It applies to integer pivot columns as well, although the undo rounds an integer column and every other column of such a row is integer. A column without a finite bound has no bound to pass: it starts with the factor 0 and is limited only through a factor that earlier substitutions gave it; a column with a finite bound starts at 1. A substitution gives every column of its saved row the maximum of its own factor and the factor of the removed column times the ratio of the two coefficients. The factors are kept per column for the whole presolve; they are a maximum, not a sum; and a presolve of the reduced model starts without them, which is why a column that only this history refuses and that the visit leaves in the model makes the call report "limited". The two refusal counters count columns once per presolve, not visits. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, limit 1000, geometric mean of the ladder step `m1_a` over the engine over 237 of the 240 in nonzeros / rows / columns; scratch switches of the worth reviewer, not in the repository): with the ratio over continuous other columns only 1.0024 / 1.0027 / 1.0026 and 5 instances behind `m1_a` instead of 9 (irish-electricity then at 421,230 nonzeros, 74,364 rows and 45,901 columns with 2,822 columns still refused; rococoB10-011000, rococoC10-001000, rocI-4-11, piperout-08 and radiationm40-10-02 reach their values without a limit); with integer pivot columns exempt 1.0022 / 1.0026 / 1.0024 and 5 behind (the same five instances gain, irish-electricity does not). The factor of earlier substitutions refuses 0 columns at any limit tried. On irish-electricity the 5,187 refused columns have ratios from 1,008 to 24,704 (median 3,760): 2,822 are continuous columns on inequalities whose largest other coefficient is on a continuous column, 2,365 are continuous columns on equations whose largest other coefficient is on a binary. A sum instead of a maximum and a reset of the factors per call were not measured. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only; geometric mean of the default size over the size with the value over 237 of the 240; no instance larger in any run, 234 fixed-point flags in all): the two scratch switches are now values of the engine option `growth_ratio`, with the same figures. At the limit 1000 in default mode `continuous_columns` gives 1.0003 / 1.0005 / 1.0003 in nonzeros / rows / columns, 6 instances (irish-electricity 432,990 / 76,716 / 48,253 to 421,230 / 74,364 / 45,901; rococoC10-001000 10,102 / 735 / 2,560 to 9,850 / 699 / 2,524; rococoB10-011000 14,133 / 847 / 3,637 to 13,911 / 810 / 3,600; rocI-4-11, piperout-08 and radiationm40-10-02 by one or two rows), and `m1_a` over the engine is 1.0024 / 1.0027 / 1.0026; `integer_pivot_exempt` gives 1.0002 / 1.0004 / 1.0001, 5 instances (the same without irish-electricity), `m1_a` 1.0022 / 1.0026 / 1.0024; the default has `m1_a` 1.0020 / 1.0022 / 1.0022. With the limit at 2000 as well: `continuous_columns` 1.0004 / 1.0006 / 1.0005, 9 instances, `m1_a` 1.0025 / 1.0028 / 1.0027; `integer_pivot_exempt` 1.0004 / 1.0005 / 1.0004, 9 instances, `m1_a` 1.0024 / 1.0028 / 1.0027. In primal-only mode at 1000: `continuous_columns` 1.0003 / 1.0005 / 1.0003, 6 instances; `integer_pivot_exempt` 1.0002 / 1.0004 / 1.0001, 5. The factor of earlier substitutions refuses no column in any form (counter `refused_growth_history` 0). In every form the factor that a substitution passes to the columns of its saved row is computed over the whole row, and `column_stats_t::max_growth` is taken over the ratios that the form read (0 for an exempt integer pivot). `continuous_columns` scans the pivot row once more for every candidate that reaches the growth test and spends those slots on the budget of the call, so a call can stop on its budget earlier than with the default; that was not measured apart, and no call stops on its budget on the 240 (AS-359). In the campaign with a value pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models each, `continuous_columns` changes 4,590 results and `integer_pivot_exempt` 1,526, and none fails. What the two forms rest on is AS-344; none of the three forms bounds the stacking of the implied-bound band with the ratio (AS-353).
- **Set by:** Design for the factor and its start values (v2_design.txt, rule R2, condition, "Growth (changed)"; v2_answers.txt item 6); code (the M1 author) for what the design leaves open: that the ratio reads every other free column of any type, and that the counters count columns once. HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner what the limit costs (AS-094), not the two narrower forms; those are in the report of the worth reviewer. The two other forms and their contract were written by the implementer of the switches and completed by the reviewer (code). HISTORY Entry 54 has the figures of the two forms and of the limit in its table for the owner's decisions; that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has not decided.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:34; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:36-38 (the factors), 55-56 and 60-62 (refused columns and the counters), 113-120 (the two largest coefficients of a row), 235-243 (factors of the saved columns), 283-298 (the test) and 422-426 (limited); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:134-138; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:73-81, 443-446 and 457-458; stage1c_reports/m1/attack_worth.json (fields "assumptions" and "open"). At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:76-93 (the three forms and why each is right) and :252; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:234-249 (`largest_continuous`), 365-387 (the ratio and the two tests) and 305-313 (the factors of the saved columns and `max_growth`); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:91-94; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 3, and field "assumptions") and impl_switches.json (field "assumptions").
- **Switch:** `options_t::growth_ratio` (default `all_columns`: the largest coefficient among all other free entries of the pivot row; other values `continuous_columns`: the largest among those in continuous columns; `integer_pivot_exempt`: `all_columns` for a continuous pivot column and no limit for an integer one; tool flag `--growth-ratio <all|continuous|integer-exempt>`), next to `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` (default 1000; tool `--pivot-growth-limit <x>`; AS-094). How the factor is carried has no switch: not built (a sum instead of a maximum, a reset of the factors per call, a limit for columns without a finite bound).
- **Alternatives:** Read continuous other columns only: built, `continuous_columns`. Exempt integer pivot columns (their restored value is rounded, and every other column of the row is integer by the integrality condition): built, `integer_pivot_exempt`. Both are as right as the default for a deviation of the reduced point only while every record gives an integer column an integer inside its bounds (AS-344). Not built: limit columns without a finite bound as well; bound a sum instead of a maximum; reset the factors per call; pass the factor on over continuous columns only.
- **May change:** Which columns the limit refuses: with either narrower form the instances behind HiGHS at the default limit go from 9 to 5, at a larger possible deviation of the restored values of those columns.
- **Test at the end:** Needs the switch. With the form behind a switch: a size run on the 240 (the measurement above), then a standard cluster comparison of the built form against each narrower form, read on the instances that differ, with the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check in each arm. It is decided together with AS-094: without a limit the form does not matter. Since commit `f05d48e31` the switch exists and the size run is made on the 240 as local dumps on the Mac: the arms are `growth_ratio` = `all_columns` against `continuous_columns` and against `integer_pivot_exempt`, both at `pivot_growth_limit` 1000; 6 and 5 instances differ, so both pairs go to the standard cluster comparison, read on those instances, after the size run is repeated on the node.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`): the ratio over all other free columns by default, two narrower forms with an option; how the factor is carried is as before, without a switch; the form is for the owner to decide together with AS-094, and the owner has not decided.

### AS-319 The value of an integer forcing column is rounded into its row with a band of 8 rounding units
- **Assumption:** In the undo of a forcing record the value that the rows leave an integer column is rounded into the row (down for a column that was free to go down, up for the mirror), and a value that lies within 8 rounding units of the terms it was computed from (the side and the absolute terms of the rest, over the coefficient) of an integer counts as that integer: 9 - 3 * (7/3) is 1.9999999999999996 and stands for 2. A value taken from the bound of the column has no band. The structure check has the same constant for its own undo. Without the band the engine and the replay of the check disagreed by one unit on a campaign model, because another summation order gives another last bit. Measured with milestone M1: not measured on real instances; the 240 as local dumps on the Mac hold 117 forcing records, and no count of integer forcing columns or of values inside the band was made. The wrong engine whose undo rounds an integer column out of its rows (fc_round) is caught by the structure check (9,806 failures) and by the oracle (9,700) in 1,440,000 campaign models. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): the band is the engine option `forcing_rounding_units`, from 0 to 8. With 0 units 0 of 240 reduced models change, in default mode and in primal-only mode with the projections (the option acts in the undo only); the 240 hold 117 forcing records on 3 instances, and how many of them are integer columns was again not counted. In the campaign with 0 units pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models, 68 results change and none fails. A band above 8 misses the row on a model of the reviewer of the switches (AS-340). The structure check now takes the band of its own undo from the stack and keeps its constant only for what two summations can differ by, so it cannot say that a band is too wide (AS-354).
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author, a deviation from the design that its report names: the design says "floored for an integer" without a band). HISTORY Entry 48 does not name it. HISTORY Entry 54 records the range 0 to 8 with the model that a wider band breaks, and has the band in its table for the owner's decisions (no reduced model changes in size); the owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:234-238 (`forcing_rounding_units`) and 293-313; contract gpu_presolve/include/gprs/postsolve.hpp:124-128; gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:246-248 (`forcing_rounding`); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:50; v2_amendments.txt:67-69; stage1c_reports/m1/impl_m1.json (field "design", deviation c). At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:272-281 and 325-331; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/postsolve.hpp:178-184; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:564; gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:292-312 (the undo of a forcing record; the band is lines 305-306); gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:260 (`forcing_rounding`) and 1833-1867; gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:107-112; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 8).
- **Switch:** `options_t::forcing_rounding_units` (default 8; any value from 0 to 8 by `choices_in_range`, which presolve() and the family assert and outside of which the tools report a usage error; tool flag `--forcing-rounding-units <x>`); presolve() writes it to `postsolve_t::forcing_rounding_units`. A band above 8 is no value, because it is not valid (AS-340). The forcing row rule keeps a constant of the same name and value in gpu_presolve/src/rules_rows.cpp:38 (AS-083), without a switch.
- **Alternatives:** No band (floor and ceiling of the computed value): built, 0 units; the value is always inside the row and can be one unit short of what the exact value allows. A band between 0 and 8: built. A band above 8: not valid (k + y <= 1e8 is missed by 1.05e-6 at 64 units, AS-340). Not built: a band in units of the feasibility tolerance; a band that scales with the side only; rounding to the nearest integer and a test of the rows.
- **May change:** The value of an integer forcing column in the postsolved solution, by one unit where the computed value is within rounding of an integer; with a band that is too wide the value passes a row by up to the band times the coefficient.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the campaign and the fixed test of the milestone; on the cluster it would show only as a postsolved solution that fails the feasibility check, which every arm counts. Since commit `f05d48e31` a pair can be set, `forcing_rounding_units` = 8 against 0: the hashes of the reduced models are equal on 240 of 240 as local dumps on the Mac, so it can show only in the values of integer forcing columns of the postsolved solution (AS-340).
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`): 8 units by default, 0 to 8 with an option; verified on the Mac only (AS-357).

### AS-320 A restored value is computed as a quotient of the row, never as a start value plus a step
- **Assumption:** In the undo of an interval record the column that ends between its start value and its bound gets (side - the other terms) / coefficient, kept between the start value and the bound, and not the start value plus the step that the row still misses. The first version of the milestone implemented the design's wording (start at the preferred value, then move by what is missing) literally: a step from a start value of -1.28e9 to 597.33 lost eight digits, and a row of another scale then read the loss as a violation of 1.9e-6. The author states it as a rule for every later undo: a restored value is a quotient of the row, and an integer value of a row is rounded with the band of AS-319. Measured with milestone M1 (campaign on the Mac): the start-plus-step form failed 1 of 20,160,000 release models without a profile option (profile dense, seed 200045869); after the repair that campaign and 322 million further models of the author show no failure of the family, and the model is a fixed test. A related loss stays possible and is a defect of the base engine: a side or a value that is written at double precision into a row which an earlier merge of parallel rows kept reaches the removed row times the ratio of the two (AS-329). Measured with the switches of milestone M1: three options now reach the undo (the start value and the order of an interval record, the band of an integer forcing column; AS-057, AS-319), and with each of their values the column that ends between its start value and its bound still gets the quotient of the row; the start value nearest to zero, which the alternatives below name, is one of them. On the 240 as local dumps on the Mac none of the values changes a reduced model.
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author, a deviation from the design's wording that its report names; the contract of the record was written with it). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner the defect and its repair ("The undo now takes the quotient of the row").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:240-278 (`undo_interval`; the quotient is lines 270-275); contract gpu_presolve/include/gprs/postsolve.hpp:116-123; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:43; stage1c_reports/m1/impl_m1.json (field "design", deviation d).
- **Switch:** no switch yet for the quotient form: needs one only if another undo is to be tested.
