Since commit `f05d48e31` three options reach the undo (`options_t::interval_start`, `options_t::interval_order`, `options_t::forcing_rounding_units`; AS-057, AS-319); none of them changes the quotient.
- **Alternatives:** Start value plus step (wrong at large start values); double-double arithmetic in the undo; a start value nearest to zero, which makes the step small.
- **May change:** The value of one column per interval record in the postsolved solution, in its last digits; with the other form a row of the original model is violated after postsolve on models with bounds of 1e9 and more.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the fixed model and the campaign; in every cluster arm the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-321 A free singleton column is left when its cost cannot be moved
- **Assumption:** The free singleton column [R2, R5] moves its cost onto the other columns of the pivot row, as cost / a times their coefficients, and into the objective offset, as cost / a times the side less the fixed terms. The column is left in the model when cost / a is not finite, when it is zero for a cost that is not zero (a quotient that underflows), when its product with that side is not finite, or when a new cost of another column would not be finite. The first version of the milestone lacked the second case: with a cost of 4.9e-324 the quotient is 0, the cost was not moved and the column was removed with it, which tripped an assertion in the assertion build and silently dropped the cost in the release build. Measured with milestone M1: not measured on real instances: no dump of the 240 and no generated model has such a cost; the repair has a unit test.
- **Set by:** Design for the finite quotient and the finite new costs (v2_design.txt, rule R2, condition: "q = cost[k]/a and every new cost are finite"); code (the M1 author) for the product with the side; code (the M1 validity reviewer) for the quotient that underflows. HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner the repaired defect ("a cost that underflows (4.9e-324) was dropped with its column").
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:34; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:201 and 207-215 (new costs), 266-270 (the quotient and its product with the side); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:451-452; gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_review.cpp:186 (`columns_review_cost_that_underflows`).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if another treatment is to be tested.
- **Alternatives:** Drop a cost that underflows (an objective error of that cost times the value of the column); clamp the quotient.
- **May change:** Nothing on the benchmark set as far as measured; on a model with denormal or huge costs a column and its row stay in the model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the unit test and, once the generator draws such costs, the campaign. A count on the 240 of singleton columns whose cost over the coefficient underflows or overflows closes the entry for the benchmark set when it is zero.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-322 The integral side and the drift guard share the feasibility tolerance
- **Assumption:** Where a row gives an integer column its value (the free singleton column of integer type [R2, R5] and an edge of the alias forest between two integer columns), the deviation of the side from the integer it is taken as, |a * m - side|, plus |a| times the drift of the rounded ratios must stay within the feasibility tolerance together, on top of the drift's own share (AS-093). By the design text each had its own limit, so a row could be off by up to 1.1 tolerances: on 1000 k + (1000 - 9e-10) y = 1000 + 9.9e-7, with k an integer free singleton column and y integer in [0, 100], the side passed within 9.9e-7 and the drift within 9e-8, the column was substituted as k = 1 - y, and the reduced point y = 100, which is exactly feasible, postsolved to a point that misses the row by 1.08e-6. A drift share above 1 therefore no longer admits more than the tolerance. Measured with milestone M1 (on the Mac): the model is refused after the repair; no result changes on the 17 local dumps, on the 240 as local dumps or on 4,320,000 random models per build, so the repair refuses nothing there. The wrong engine with the two limits apart (fs_shared) fails unit tests only (AS-330).
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 validity reviewer, against the binding design text: amendment F.2 of v2_amendments.txt has each within its own limit). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner the repair, and that it is a defect of the design text that the code had inherited.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/integral.hpp:34 and 55-64 (`side_error`), 74-88 (`drift_within`; the sum is line 87); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:39-44; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:277-282; gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:57-63; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:447-450; gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_review.cpp:212; stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:97-101; the model is stage1c_reports/m1/probes/stack_tol.cpp.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for the sum; its two parts read `options_t::tol.integral_drift_share` (default 0.1; tool `--integral-drift-share <x>`) and `options_t::tol.feasibility` (default 1e-6).
- **Alternatives:** Each within its own limit, as the design text has it (a row off by up to 1.1 tolerances); the side exact and the whole tolerance for the drift.
- **May change:** Nothing on the benchmark set (no result changes); on models with a side that is integral only within the tolerance an integer singleton column stays in the model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the fixed model, the unit tests and a generator knob for ratios and sides that are integers only within the tolerance (AS-330).
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-323 New sides of the zero-cost singleton rule that cross: the visit does nothing
- **Assumption:** The zero-cost singleton rule [R3] computes the two new sides of its row as two compensated differences. When they end with the lower side above the upper one, which can happen when two differences are equal to the last bit, the visit does nothing and the columns stay; the rule does not join the sides, as the start step and the merge of parallel rows do with sides that cross within the tolerance (AS-082). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; scratch counters of the worth reviewer): the sides cross 0 times. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, default mode): the count is now a counter of the engine (`zero_cost_crossing`) and is 0 on all 240; of the two steps that the test below names, the statistic of the engine exists and the repeat on the node is missing.
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author; the design does not name the case). HISTORY Entry 48 does not name it; it is in the report of the validity reviewer.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:343-344 and 352-353; stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (field "assumptions").
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Set both sides to one value, as AS-082 does elsewhere.
- **May change:** A row keeps its zero-cost singleton columns where the range of the rest is a single value up to rounding. The refusal is a function of the state, so it does not clear the fixed-point flag.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the count on the 240 is zero on the local dumps, from a counter of a scratch build (AS-338). With the counter as a statistic of the engine and the count repeated on the node, zero closes the entry for the benchmark set; otherwise a size run with the hash of the reduced model, the sides joined against the visit that does nothing.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-324 A free singleton substitution has no limit on the size of the costs it creates
- **Assumption:** A free singleton substitution [R2, R5] gives every other column of its pivot row the cost of the removed column over its coefficient times their own coefficient, added to what they had. Nothing limits the size of that quotient or the range of the costs that results: the growth limit (AS-094) reads the coefficients of the row, not the cost, and only a cost that is not finite stops the substitution (AS-321). The entry on the alias scale names the same missing limit in passing (AS-092). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, the reduced models of the engine before and with the family; a scratch tool of the worth reviewer, not in the repository): the ratio of the largest to the smallest absolute cost grows on 32 instances, most on roi5alpha10n8 (from 1 to 9.9e5; the smallest cost is now 1e-6), supportcase7 (1 to 9.1e4), triptim1 (1 to 1.3e4), neos-3627168-kasai (2,740), irish-electricity (1,420), mcsched (999) and rococoB10-011000 (996); the largest cost goes to 1.7e8 on leo2 and 9.0e7 on leo1, on their objective rows. The coefficient ranges of the matrix grow on no instance (the range of the model is smaller on 14, the largest ratio within a row on 15). Whether a wide cost range hurts cuOpt's LP solves or its objective-driven heuristics was not measured. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the tool is now in the repository (gprs_quality with gpu_presolve/analysis/quality_compare.py) and gives the same with the family off and on: the cost range is larger on 32 instances, the coefficient range of the model larger on none and smaller on 14.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt, risks: the free singleton rules "move cost with only the cancellation rule"); the measurement is new. HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner that the cost range grows on 32 instances and lists the dense objective (AS-095) as open; the cost range itself is not on its list of open points.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:269; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:201 and 219-232; stage1c_reports/m1/attack_worth.json (fields "other_defects" and "reductions").
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for a limit; `options_t::sub_enabled[sub_rule::free_singleton]` and `[sub_rule::free_singleton_dual]` (tool `--off free_singleton`, `--off free_singleton_dual`) turn the rules.
- **Alternatives:** Refuse a substitution whose quotient cost / a, or whose largest new cost over the smallest cost of the model, is above a limit; scale the objective after the presolve.
- **May change:** The numerics of the LP relaxation and of the objective tests in cuOpt on the 32 instances; with a limit, rows and columns stay that the rule removes today.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison, read on the 32 instances: both free singleton reductions on against off (the pair of AS-095), with the root relaxation objective next to the quality measures; with a limit built, the limit on against off.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`), without a limit.

### AS-340 The widest forcing band is the default: `forcing_rounding_units` takes 0 to 8, and a band above 8 misses the row
- **Assumption:** The option for the rounding band of an integer forcing column (AS-319) accepts 0 to 8 rounding units, and 8, the constant of the engine before, is taken as the widest band that is right. The implementer of the switches had accepted 0 to `tol.side_error` / `rounding_unit` (90 with the default tolerances) and had measured, stored and drawn in the campaign the value 64; the range was argued with the side alone, while the band is the units times the rounding unit times (|side| + the terms of the rest). The reviewer of the switches showed a wrong result for every value above 8 and restricted the range. The model: k + y <= 1e8 with k integer in (-inf, 1e9] without cost and y continuous in [0, 6e7], and y + v >= 3 with a binary v; only the forcing column is active. At the reduced point y = 49999999.00000105, v = 1, which is exactly feasible, the row leaves k the value 50000000.99999895. With 8 units k = 50000000 and the point is feasible; with 64 units the undo takes the value as 50000001 and the row is missed by 1.05e-6; with 90 units and y = 49999999.0000012 by 1.2e-6. The default keeps the row within the tolerance while 8 rounding units of |side| + the terms stay below it, that is for terms up to about 1e9; with larger terms the default itself takes the value further, which is the behaviour of the engine before and is not changed. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the removed value 64 changed 0 of 240 reduced models (the option acts in the undo only; a run of the implementer, deleted with the value). In the reviewer's campaign on the implementer's commit 2,728,464 release models ran with 64 alone, 1 result changed and none failed: the generator has no side near 1e8 next to terms of that size (AS-355). The structure check was silent on the model at 64 and at 90 units (AS-354).
- **Set by:** Code (the reviewer of the switches wrote the range 0 to 8 and the test; the implementer had written 0 to 90; the default 8 is the constant of the M1 author, AS-319). HISTORY Entry 54 records the wrong result at 64 units and the range 0 to 8; the owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:272-281 (the option, its contract and the model) and 325-331 (`choices_in_range`); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/postsolve.hpp:178-184; gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:292-312 (the undo of a forcing record; the band is lines 305-306); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:107-112 (a value outside the range is a usage error); gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches_review.cpp:52-82 (`switches_review_forcing_band_above_the_rule_misses_the_row`); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results", item 1, "options_removed_or_restricted" and "assumptions") and impl_switches.json (field "assumptions").
- **Switch:** `options_t::forcing_rounding_units` (default 8; range 0 to 8 by `choices_in_range`; tool flag `--forcing-rounding-units <x>`). A value above 8: no switch, not built, because it is not valid (the model above).
- **Alternatives:** A band in units of the feasibility tolerance; a band that scales with the side only; a range up to 90 units (the implementer's form, wrong on the model).
- **May change:** Nothing at the default. A band above 8 would let the value of an integer forcing column pass its row by more than the tolerance on models with a side or terms near 1e8; a narrower band can leave the value one unit short of what the exact value allows.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the fixed test of the model; in every cluster arm the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check. A pair of 8 against 0 units would show only in the values of integer forcing columns (117 forcing records on 3 of the 240).
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`); verified on the Mac only (AS-357).

### AS-341 The implied-bound band has no option: a band above the rule's gives a postsolved point 9.5e-4 outside a bound
- **Assumption:** The implementer of the switches built an option `implied_band_scale` for the band of the implied-bound test of the free singleton column (AS-088): a factor from 1 to 1 / (2 * `tol.activity_error_share`), which is 5 with the defaults, argued by the band then staying within the feasibility tolerance; the values 2 and 5 were measured and drawn in the campaign. The reviewer of the switches removed the option, its flag `--implied-band-scale`, its two campaign values and its test, because every value above 1 gives a wrong result on a model where the band of the rule does not. The model: x + 1000 y + w = 500 and y + 0.5 z + 0.5 v = 1; x continuous in [-501, 499.99905], y continuous in [9.5e-7, 1], w continuous in [0, 1] with cost 1, z and v binary with cost -1. With the band of the rule y is no free singleton column after x has left with the first row (its lower bound is 9.5e-7 from what the second row gives, outside the band of about 2e-7); it leaves as a zero-cost singleton column, and the postsolved point is feasible (row violation 9.5e-7). With five times the band both x and y leave as free singleton columns, the reduced model is empty, and the postsolved point has y = 0 and x = 500, which is 9.5e-4 above the upper bound of x; the structure check is silent. The implementer's argument left out that a column restored after one that passed its bound by the band passes its own by the band times their ratio, up to the growth limit of 1000 (AS-094). A value below 1 is right for every result and breaks the fixed point (AS-088). So no valid other value is left. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the removed values 2 and 5 changed 0 of 240 reduced models in default and in primal-only mode (runs of the implementer, deleted with the option). In the reviewer's campaign on the implementer's commit 5,461,728 release models ran with 2 or 5 alone, 0 results changed and none failed: the generator has no bound within a few bands of what its row gives (AS-355). The same chain at the band of the rule is the defect of AS-353.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches built the option; the reviewer removed it and wrote the contract that says why there is none, with the model). HISTORY Entry 54 records the wrong result and the removal; the owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:283-293 (why there is no option, with the model); gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:207-232 (`implied_free`, which calls `implied_tolerance` as the engine before); gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:32-40; gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches_review.cpp:84-111 (`switches_review_bound_beyond_the_band_is_not_implied`, the model at the band of the rule); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results", item 2, "options_removed_or_restricted" and "switches", row 9) and impl_switches.json (field "assumptions", the removed range).
- **Switch:** no switch: not built (removed). A factor above 1 is not valid: the model above. A factor below 1 needs a fresh sum in every test or a floor of its own (AS-088).
- **Alternatives:** A factor of 2 or 5 on the band (the removed values, wrong on the model); the band divided by the growth factor of the column (AS-353); rounding only, with fresh sums.
- **May change:** Nothing at the default: the engine is the engine before. With a wider band more singleton columns would count as free, and a restored value could pass its bound by the band times the ratio of a later substitution.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the fixed test of the model. The pair of AS-088 (band against rounding only) still needs its switch.
- **State:** not in the engine: the option existed in commit `55b639503` of the implementer and is removed in commit `f05d48e31`.

### AS-342 The ranges of the options are contracts that presolve() asserts; the defaults are in range with every tolerance
- **Assumption:** Two of the options of the singleton-column rules take a number and have a range: `forcing_rounding_units` (0 to 8, AS-340) and `side_guard_scale` (AS-343). The range is a contract: the function `choices_in_range` tests it, presolve() and the singleton family assert it, and the tools report a value outside of it as a usage error; a release build that is called with a value outside the range runs with it unchecked. The default choices are in range with every tolerance: a factor `side_guard_scale` of exactly 1 is accepted whatever `tol.side_error` and `tol.feasibility` are, and the two inequalities of the range bind only a factor other than 1. In the implementer's form the range of the defaults depended on the tolerances: with a feasibility tolerance of 1e-10 (below 1e5 * `tol.side_error`), with `tol.side_error` = 1e-16 (below 8 rounding units), or with `tol.activity_error_share` above 0.5 the default choices were out of range, and presolve() of an assertion build stopped ("gprs_assert failed: choices_in_range(options)") on options that the engine before takes; the release build was not affected. The reviewer repaired it. No wrong engine changes `choices_in_range` or `sub_rule_active`: the script of the wrong engines copies gpu_presolve/src only, and both functions live in a public header, so only unit tests cover them. Measured with the switches of milestone M1: the test `switches_review_defaults_take_every_tolerance` failed and aborted on the implementer's commit and passes on commit `f05d48e31`; there is no figure on the 240.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches wrote the function and the assertions; the reviewer wrote the rule that the defaults are in range with every tolerance). HISTORY Entry 54 records the repair in one line; the owner has decided nothing about it. The rest is in the report of the reviewer (fields "wrong_results", item 3, and "assumptions").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:321-331 (`choices_in_range`); gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:562 and gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:764 (the assertions); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:57-60 and :112; gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches_review.cpp:32-50 and gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches.cpp:645 (`switches_ranges_of_the_choices`); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results" and "assumptions") and impl_switches.json (field "not_done").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a contract. The function is `gprs::choices_in_range(const options_t&)`; it binds `options_t::forcing_rounding_units` (0 to 8) and `options_t::side_guard_scale` (1, or a factor with `side_guard_scale * tol.feasibility >= 1e5 * tol.side_error` and `8 * side_guard_scale * rounding_unit <= tol.side_error`).
- **Alternatives:** Require the tolerance relations for the default as well (the implementer's form, which stops an assertion build on tolerances that the engine before takes); bring a value into its range instead of refusing it; return a status instead of an assertion.
- **May change:** Nothing in the reduced model. An end-test arm that changes a tolerance (AS-076 names `side_error` 1e-16) runs with the default choices; an arm that sets a value outside a range stops an assertion build and runs unchecked in a release build.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the unit tests; before any cluster arm that sets one of the two options, the value is checked against the range with the tolerances of that arm.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`).

### AS-343 The range of `side_guard_scale`: the lower end copies a private constant, the upper end counts one record per row
- **Assumption:** The factor on the size of a side that the singleton-column rules read (AS-076) is accepted from 1e-3 to 11 with the default tolerances. The lower end, `side_guard_scale * tol.feasibility >= 1e5 * tol.side_error`, keeps a side of 1e5 readable, which the state needs for a fixed point (the terms of fixed columns leave a row without an event up to that size). It holds the literal 1e5, which is the private constant `plain_size` of the state, and nothing ties the two. The upper end, `8 * side_guard_scale * rounding_unit <= tol.side_error`, leaves room for eight rounding units of the largest side read within the tolerance, which is the room for one record on a row. A row that the zero-cost singleton columns leave in several visits collects one rounding unit of its sides per visit at an extreme point, in every setting, and would use up the room after about 90 / factor visits that all round the same way. The reviewer of the switches found no wrong result with the factor 8 and built no model with such a ladder of visits; the contract of the option names what the range assumes. A factor below 1 only refuses reductions. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the factor 8 changes 0 of 240 reduced models, and the guards refuse nothing there; in the campaign with 8 pinned on every seed 33 of 5,040,000 results change and none fails; the check tool without a solver passes with 8 on the 17 local dumps and on the 240. The ends of the range (1e-3 and 11) were not measured.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches wrote the range; the reviewer named what its two ends assume and wrote it into the contract). HISTORY Entry 54 has the side guard in its table for the owner's decisions (no reduced model changes in size); what the two ends of the range assume is in the report of the reviewer only. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:295-313 and 328-330; gpu_presolve/src/state_storage.cpp:82-86 (`plain_size`); gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:764-768; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "assumptions", "switches", row 10, and "open") and impl_switches.json (field "unsure").
- **Switch:** `options_t::side_guard_scale` (default 1; 1e-3 to 11 with the default tolerances; tool flag `--side-guard-scale <x>`).
- **Alternatives:** No factor above 1; a count of the visits per row; a named constant shared by the state and the header for the lower end; a limit relative to the row.
- **May change:** Nothing at the default. With a factor above 1 the family reads larger sides, and a row that is visited many times could leave the tolerance by the rounding of its sides; with a factor below the lower end the reduced model of a fixed point would not be one.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair while the guards refuse nothing on the 240: a count of the visits per row on the 240, and a model with a ladder of visits at the factor 8 as a fixed test. If a model with sides above 1e8 enters the benchmark, the pair of AS-076.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`); the default is the engine before.

### AS-344 The two narrower growth forms rest on integer columns being restored exactly
- **Assumption:** The forms `continuous_columns` and `integer_pivot_exempt` of the growth ratio (AS-318) leave integer columns out of the limit. They are as right as the default for a deviation of the reduced point only while every record gives an integer column an integer inside its bounds: the postsolve rounds the integer columns of the reduced point before any undo; a fixed column has an integral value; an alias between two integer columns has a scale and an offset that are rounded to integers; a defined and a forcing record round. The reviewer of the switches checked this in the source and on a model (an integer pair with the coefficient 1.0000000005: the alias record is x3 = 1 * x1 + 0); the implementer's contract had named only the rounding of the reduced point. A later rule that restores an integer column otherwise (the implied-integer conversion [L2], the single-lock alias [M4], the merge of parallel columns [M5]) has to be read against this, and a caller that postsolves a point with integer columns far from integers gets no guard from the limit for those columns in these two forms. Neither form changes the stacking of the implied-bound band with the ratio (AS-353), because the ratio over the continuous columns is limited in all three forms. Measured with the switches of milestone M1: the model above; the two unit tests of the forms; no failure in the reviewer's sets in exact rational arithmetic with ratios of 2048; no figure on the 240 beyond those of AS-318.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches wrote the two forms and their first contract; the reviewer completed the contract and named the condition). HISTORY Entry 54 does not name it; it is in the report of the reviewer (fields "other_defects", item 1, and "assumptions") and of the implementer (field "unsure"). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:76-93; gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:47-63 (scale and offset of an alias between integer columns are rounded); gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:285-289 (a defined record rounds), 309-311 (a forcing record rounds) and :454 (the reduced point); gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches.cpp:182 and :216 (the two tests of the forms).
- **Switch:** `options_t::growth_ratio` = `continuous_columns` or `integer_pivot_exempt` (default `all_columns`, which does not rest on it); tool flag `--growth-ratio <all|continuous|integer-exempt>`.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the limit for integer columns (the default); a test in the postsolve that an integer column got an integer inside its bounds.
- **May change:** Nothing at the default. With a narrower form and a later record kind that gives an integer column a value that is not rounded, a deviation could grow past the limit without a refusal.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair of its own: at each later milestone that adds a record kind for integer columns, the contract of the two forms is read again and their two unit tests are extended; the pair of the forms is that of AS-318.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`), as a contract of two option values that are off by default.

## 4. Order of the rules, rounds, budgets, stopping

### AS-112 At run time all rules are phases of one loop with fixed alternations
- **Assumption:** "Stage" is a development stage. At run time the cheap cascade and the pairwise and structural phases run until they stall (less than 0.05% of rows and of columns removed), then the wide phases (probing, cliques, cuOpt's special reductions) run, and when they changed the model the loop returns to the cascade, for a fixed number of alternations at most. The plan gives no number of alternations.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md amendment A7), confirmed to the owner, who asked on 2026-11-27 (HISTORY Entry 26): "The stages run in a loop, right?"
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A7 (lines 87-91) and section 3 (lines 492-495).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for the stall share and the alternations; today the loop is bounded by `options_t::max_rounds` and `options_t::max_cascade_passes` (AS-118).
- **Alternatives:** Run to a fixed point without a cap; wide phases once (HiGHS runs several stages once); another stall share.
- **May change:** How far the presolve goes once wide phases exist: a low cap leaves reductions undone, a high one costs time.
- **Test at the end:** When the wide phases exist: alternations 1 against the default, first as a size run, then as a standard cluster comparison.
- **State:** planned (the Stage 1 rounds are in the engine).

### AS-113 After Stage 1 rules are built in order of nonzero worth on the 240
- **Assumption:** The order is: cheap column rules, then sparsify and the aggregator, then pairwise dominated columns, then enumeration, probing and cliques. It replaces the earlier order that put the GPU-wide phases (probing, cliques) before the parity rules, which the owner had approved on the assistant's recommendation (HISTORY Entries 6 and 13). The basis is one measurement: on the 240, probing and enumeration take 65% of HiGHS's time for under 2% of the nonzeros in the mean. Inside the cheap column rules the build order is by cost and by what later rules need, not by measured worth: the groundwork [M0], the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1], simple probing [M2], dominance between parallel columns [M3], dual bound strengthening and then the single-lock alias [M4], the merge of parallel columns [M5]; then the five further rules in the order of build cost: implied equation [L1], implied-integer conversion [L2], column stuffing [L3], single-equation extension [L4], dominated column [L5]. By worth the dominated column would follow the conversion; the order of the five is the design's, and the owner has not answered on it. Nothing of the five is built before the nine are finished. If the stage stops early, the rules built so far are the ones that can be tested.
- **Set by:** The owner (HISTORY Entry 29, 2026-11-27: "Yes, cheap rules first").
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A8 (lines 93-112), replacing the stage order of section 0 (line 200) and section 13; HISTORY Entries 28 and 29. Build order inside the stage: DESIGN.md amendment A8 (lines 120-124 against 133-138); stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:19-23; v2_reviews.txt:124; lowworth_v2_design.txt:17, :194-218; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:40-41; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:99-102; HISTORY Entry 38 (lines 2147-2150).
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a build order. At run time each family has its switch (`options_t::enabled`, `options_t::sub_enabled`), and on the HiGHS side each family has a name in `HIGHS_DIAG_OFF`, which gives a test before the family is built.
- **Alternatives:** Probing and cliques first (plan version 4): the GPU-wide work and the rules most likely to strengthen without removing nonzeros; an order by worth on the 40, which ranks the rules differently (AS-239).
- **May change:** Which rules exist when the end test runs. Until the last step exists the new presolve has no probing, no clique merging, no pairwise dominated columns and no aggregator, which PaPILO in cuOpt has. A worth measured in nonzeros understates rules whose effect is on strength (implications, clique rows, fixings).
- **Test at the end:** A test that can be run now, before the families are built: one pair per HiGHS family, each two arms of cuOpt with its presolver set to none (`presolve` = 0, `mip_scaling` 0) on the 240 as presolved by HiGHS with the family on and with it off: `probing`, `enumeration`, `clique_merging`, `clique_substitutions`, `sparsify`, `aggregator`, `dominated_columns`, `strengthen_inequalities`, `parallel_columns` in `HIGHS_DIAG_OFF`, and for the probing budget `HIGHS_DIAG_PROBING_NOSTOP=1` against unset. The two arms of a pair differ in one HiGHS switch and are scaled alike by HiGHS. The result is a ranking of the families by solution quality next to the ranking by nonzeros of HISTORY Entry 28. Its limit: with the presolver set to none cuOpt's own presolve (bound update, probing cache, block BVE) does not run either (AS-142). When the families exist in the engine: one-family-off arms of the standard cluster comparison, and a pair "wide phases off against on".
- **State:** in force; the cheap column rules are in work.

### AS-114 A round runs the cascade, parallel rows, doubleton equations, then the column families
- **Assumption:** A round is: the cascade (row rules, bound propagation, row tightening, then dual fixing on the locks they leave, repeated until their lists are empty), then parallel rows, then doubleton equations. Since the groundwork four column families follow as phases of their own, still empty: simple probing, singleton columns, dual columns, parallel columns. They come last "so that the first round does on the input model what it did without them", and dual fixing stays in the cascade and so runs before every column rule. Inside a family rows are served in the order of their first event. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the singleton family is built and is called once per round after the cascade, the parallel rows, the doubleton equations and simple probing, so a doubleton equation with a singleton column is the doubleton rule's, and the parallel rows read singleton columns before this family removes them. With the family right after the cascade instead (a scratch switch of the worth reviewer, not in the repository) the sizes are the same on all 240, with the growth limit at 1000 and without it; 12 reduced models differ in hash at equal size (corrected on 2026-11-28 from the switches: 10 with the committed engine, see below), 2 instances need one more round (timtab1, rd-rplusc-21), and the work of the family rises from 126,751,280 to 138,654,330 units. The engine is ahead of the ladder step `m1_a` on 33 instances in nonzeros, on 17 of them already before the milestone. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): the scratch switch is now the engine option `singleton_cols_early`. With it the sizes are equal on all 240 in default mode; 10 reduced models differ in hash at equal size (binkar10_1, cryptanalysiskb128n5obj14, cryptanalysiskb128n5obj16, gfd-schedulen180f7d50m30k18, highschool1-aigio, icir97_tension, momentum1, rocI-4-11, rocII-5-11, snp-02-004-104), not the 12 of the worth report; rd-rplusc-21 and timtab1 need one more round (709 rounds in the sum against 707); the work of the family is 138,654,330 against 126,751,280 units; 234 fixed-point flags with both values. In primal-only mode 10 differ at equal size and the rounds are 683 against 682. The hashes differ, so by the closing rule the pair goes to the standard cluster comparison, read on these 10 instances. In the campaign with the value pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models, 149,903 results change and none fails.
- **Set by:** Design (Stage 1 code structure, HISTORY Entries 14 and 15; for the column families v2_design.txt:123). HISTORY Entry 54 has the place of the phase in its table for the owner's decisions (no reduced model changes in size; the early phase changes 10 hashes at equal size); the owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 3 (lines 479-495); gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:287-303, 334-363; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:264-270 (the four column phases are presolve.cpp:349-355). At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:338-367 (`rounds`; the four column phases are lines 353-356); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:271-276; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:385-388. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:315-318; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:405-416 (`rounds`: the flag `early` around the call of the singleton-column phase); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:81-84; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 11).
- **Switch:** `options_t::singleton_cols_early` (default false: the singleton columns are the phase after simple probing; other value true: they are the first phase after the cascade, before the parallel rows and the doubleton equations; tool flag `--singleton-cols-early`). No switch for any other order (dual fixing after the column rules, doubleton equations before parallel rows); a family can otherwise only be switched off (`options_t::enabled`).
- **Alternatives:** HiGHS's order (singleton rows, doubleton equations and column singletons inside one fast loop); doubleton equations before parallel rows; column rules inside the cascade or before the parallel rows; dual fixing after the column rules. The singleton columns as the first phase after the cascade: built, `singleton_cols_early` (the same reductions on another state).
- **May change:** Where two reductions exclude each other the order decides which is applied: a column that dual fixing fixes is no longer there for an alias or a merge, and a substitution changes which rows are parallel. The engine is ahead of the reference on nine instances through the row addition, which reads singleton columns that the free singleton rules remove from the second round on.
- **Test at the end:** Needs the switch. Proposed pair once the column rules exist: dual fixing in the cascade against after the column rules; a size run first (no instance worse than the Stage 1 engine), then a standard cluster comparison if reduced models differ. Since commit `f05d48e31` the place of the singleton-column phase has its switch: `singleton_cols_early` = false against true. The size run on the 240 as local dumps on the Mac gives equal sizes and 10 different hashes in each mode, so by the closing rule that pair goes to the standard cluster comparison, read on those instances, after the size run is repeated on the node. The pair of dual fixing still needs its switch.
- **State:** in the engine (the row phases; of the four column families the singleton columns are built and, since commit `f05d48e31`, have an option for their place; the other three are still empty).

### AS-115 The two families of the five rules run last in a round, the conversion last
- **Assumption:** With the five rules a round ends with the exchange family (single equation, dominated column) and then the implied-integer family. The conversion is last "so that in every round the parallel columns, the zero-cost singleton rule and the dual rules see a column while it is still continuous". Stuffing is a step of the singleton-column family and the implied equation a test of the dual-column family.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:91; the conversion's place is part of its decision 7).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:91, :187, :227.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for the order.
- **Alternatives:** Conversion earlier (as HiGHS, where it blocks parallel-column reductions on blp-ic98 and blp-ar98); exchange family before the dual columns.
- **May change:** Which of two competing reductions wins on a column (merge of parallel columns or conversion; zero-cost singleton or conversion). Predicted: the engine keeps the smaller size on blp-ic98 and blp-ar98.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run (milestone of the implied-integer conversion [L2]: the two blp instances not above their size before it). A cluster pair would need a switch for the phase order.
- **State:** designed.

### AS-116 Inside a column family the reductions are tried in a fixed order
- **Assumption:** Singleton family: per row visit the free singleton with an equation pivot, else with an inequality pivot, else the zero-cost singleton rule; the forcing column for listed columns that are no singletons. Dual family: dual bound strengthening for all candidates, then the single-lock alias, which collects its rows after the column pass. Parallel family: dominance, then merge. Candidates are taken in index order. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): as built, a call serves the column list before the row list and takes both lists again until they stay empty; a row is visited at most once per pass over the lists; a visit tries the free singleton column first (on an equation [R2], on any other row [R5]; the row needs two or more free entries) for the singleton columns of the row in increasing index and takes the first that passes, and only when none leaves does the zero-cost singleton rule [R3] run; every listed live column, a singleton column or not, is tested as a forcing column [R4] after the visit of its row (the sentence above and the design have that test for listed columns that are no singleton columns). 6,916 of the 15,902 substitutions happen in a row with two or more singleton columns (6,866 on savsched1, 16 each on seymour and seymour1, 10 on icir97_tension), so the choice of the column matters on one instance. The worth reviewer argues that the zero-cost rule first would lose nothing: for a zero-cost column "the row implies its bounds" is the same as "the row without it is redundant", and the row rules then delete the row. No other order was measured. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): with `singleton_order` = `zero_cost_first` no reduced model changes in default mode (0 of 240; timtab1, momentum1 and rd-rplusc-21 need one more round, and the record stacks differ), so there the worth reviewer's argument holds for the sizes. In primal-only mode with the projections (AS-004) it gives 0.9982 / 1.0000 / 1.0005 in nonzeros / rows / columns over 237 of the 240 (size with the default order over size with the value): 5 instances change, 4 of them larger in nonzeros and rows (swath3 27,760 / 482 / 6,403 to 34,034 / 483 / 6,354; swath1 to 34,029 / 483 / 6,349; physiciansched6-2 45,011 / 12,156 / 6,927 to 45,445 / 12,157 / 6,398; physiciansched3-3 390,639 / 75,603 / 19,758 to 390,971 / 75,604 / 19,298; neos-4722843-widden 77,401 to 77,381 columns); `m1_a` over the engine is 0.9540 / 0.9621 / 0.9413 against 0.9557 / 0.9621 / 0.9408. In the campaign with the value pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models, 20,593 results change and none fails. The values `largest_pivot` and `single_pass` are in AS-127; what the code fixes for the three other orders is AS-345.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:117-121; v2_amendments.txt:113-114). HISTORY Entry 54 has the order in its table for the owner's decisions with the words that no reduced model changes in size. That holds in default mode for `zero_cost_first` and `largest_pivot`; it does not hold for `zero_cost_first` in primal-only mode with the projections (5 instances, 4 of them larger in nonzeros and rows) nor for `single_pass` (one instance larger, AS-127). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:117-121; v2_amendments.txt:113-114. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:123-127 (increasing index), 398-427 (`visit`) and 556-589 (the service of the lists); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:420-435. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:95-114 (the orders) and :267; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:569-636 (`visit`; the option is read in lines 605, 612 and 626) and 780-820 (the service of the lists); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:95-98; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 6).
- **Switch:** `options_t::singleton_order` for the singleton family (default `smallest_index`, the order above; other values `largest_pivot`: the singleton columns of a row are tried by decreasing absolute coefficient; `zero_cost_first`: the zero-cost singleton columns leave before a free singleton column is tried; `single_pass`: a call ends after the first pass over its lists that makes a reduction; tool flag `--singleton-order <index|largest-pivot|zero-cost-first|single-pass>`). The forcing column after the visit of its row, and the orders of the dual and the parallel family, have no switch.
- **Alternatives:** The zero-cost rule before the free singleton column: built, `zero_cost_first`. The largest pivot first and one pass per call: built (AS-127). Not built: other orders, for example the forcing column before the visit.
- **May change:** Which reduction takes a column that several could take.
- **Test at the end:** No pair planned; covered by the on-against-off pairs of the reductions (AS-050). Since commit `f05d48e31` the order inside a visit can be set: `singleton_order` = `smallest_index` against `zero_cost_first`. In default mode the hashes of the reduced models are equal on 240 of 240 as local dumps on the Mac, so the size run repeated on the node closes that pair if it gives the same; in primal-only mode with the projections 5 instances differ, so that pair (both arms with `primal_only` and `primal_projections` true) goes to the standard cluster comparison, read on those 5, if the projections are switched on in the mode (AS-004).
- **State:** in the engine for the singleton family (commit `f05d48e31`: the designed order by default, three other orders with an option); designed for the dual and the parallel family (milestones of the nine column rules [M3 to M5]).

### AS-117 The column rules run both before and after the switch to explicit bounds
- **Assumption:** The four column families run in the rounds before the implied bounds become explicit, where a continuous column still has its input bounds, and in the rounds after. The loss is assumed to be limited to columns that become singletons after the switch. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the counter `free_singleton_rejected_explicit`, which counts one refusal for each singleton column that fails the implied-bound test in a visit of its row after the switch, is 35,327 on 24 instances (neos-827175 10,248, snp-02-004-104 7,935, irish-electricity 7,797), and 67,024 on 30 instances in primal-only mode (recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac). The counter is not zero, so the entry is not closed by it; it counts refusals, so a column whose row is visited several times is counted several times, and it does not say how many of the refused columns would have passed before the switch. The 40 were not run.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_answers.txt item 32).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:123; v2_answers.txt:127-130. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:47-48, 271-274 and :526; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:424-425; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:88-92 and 494-497.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Column rules only before the switch; the switch at the very end.
- **May change:** Singleton columns whose bound propagation tightened fail the implied-free test; the counter `free_singleton_rejected_explicit` counts them.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run when the singleton-column rules are built: the counter `free_singleton_rejected_explicit` on the 240 and the 40. A counter of zero on the 240 closes the entry for the benchmark set. Otherwise an order switch has to be built, and a size run with the hash of the reduced model at both values decides: identical hashes on all 240 close the entry, any difference sends it to the standard cluster comparison, read on the instances that differ.
- **State:** in the engine for the singleton family (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-118 The presolve has at most 20 rounds in all and 100 cascade passes per round
- **Assumption:** `max_rounds` = 20 counts the rounds of the whole presolve, those after the switch to explicit bounds included; `max_cascade_passes` = 100 limits the passes over the four cascade families within one round. The round limit is not raised for the column rules, although each alternation such as singleton column, lock rule, redundant rows costs one round. Today no instance of the 240 ends on it (the largest count is 13). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): with the singleton-column rules the largest round count is still 13 (neos-3555904-turama) and no instance ends on `max_rounds`; 8 instances need more rounds than before (neos-3402294-bobin, neos-3402454-bohle, pg, fast0507 and dws008-01 one more; seymour, seymour1 and neos-3627168-kasai two more) and 18 need fewer (the two counts and the largest recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac); the median is 3.
- **Set by:** Code for the values; taken as recommended for not raising it (v2_amendments.txt:90: "max_rounds is not raised"; decision 6 of v2_design.txt:281 says raising it needs the owner's word).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:141-145; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:291, :337; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:125, :208, :267, :281. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:143-147; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:341.
- **Switch:** `options_t::max_rounds` (default 20) and `options_t::max_cascade_passes` (default 100).
- **Alternatives:** Higher limits; a separate limit for the rounds after the switch to explicit bounds.
- **May change:** An instance that ends on a limit keeps reductions undone and loses the fixed-point flag.
- **Test at the end:** First a count of the instances whose result has 20 rounds, after each milestone, and a size run 20 against 100. A standard cluster comparison 20 against a small value such as 2, and 20 against 100 if any instance ends on it.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-119 A new equation or converted column reaches the other rules only in the next round
- **Assumption:** An implied equation reaches the doubleton equations and the free singleton rule only in the next round, and a converted column reaches dual bound strengthening only in the next round; likewise the findings of the column families reach the cascade one round later. Chains through the new rules can therefore end on the round limit.
- **Set by:** Design (a consequence named as a risk, lowworth_v2_design.txt:232).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:232; v2_design.txt:125.
- **Switch:** `options_t::max_rounds` bounds it; no switch yet for applying follow-up rules inside the same round.
- **Alternatives:** Follow-up rules inside the same round; a higher round limit.
- **May change:** Reductions lost on the round limit; the milestones of the implied equation [L1] and of the implied-integer conversion [L2] report the rounds (map10 is named).
- **Test at the end:** First a size run (rounds and fixed-point counts per instance on the 240 at the milestones of the implied equation [L1] and of the implied-integer conversion [L2]); then the pair of AS-118 if instances end on the limit.
- **State:** designed.

### AS-120 The presolve ends at the first round without a finding, even after a budget stop
- **Assumption:** Rounds repeat until one reports no finding; a recorded implied bound is not a finding. If a call of that round stopped on its budget or stall limit, the presolve still ends, the rows left on the lists are not served, and such rounds are not taken up again after the switch to explicit bounds.
- **Set by:** Design (the engine driver; written into the contract of `presolve()`).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:331-363, 411-435; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:296-297, 327-331.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; reachable only through the budgets (AS-121).
- **Alternatives:** Go on while a list is not empty; start the stopped family again with a new budget.
- **May change:** A family that stalled leaves its list unserved; the model keeps what that list held.
- **Test at the end:** Covered by the budget test of AS-121: identical reduced models on the 240 with every budget times 10 close it.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-121 Every rule family has a work budget and a stall stop; the values are placeholders
- **Assumption:** A call of a family may spend floor + passes * pass work units and may go floor + stall_passes * pass units without a finding, where pass = free nonzeros + 8 * (live rows + live columns). Defaults {passes, stall passes, floor}: row rules, bound propagation and row tightening {16, 2, 100000}; doubleton equations {16, 4, 100000}; parallel rows {8, 8, 100000}; dual fixing {4, 2, 100000}; for the four column families, which are still empty, simple probing {4, 2, 100000}, singleton columns {8, 4, 100000}, dual columns {8, 4, 100000}, parallel columns {8, 8, 100000}; designed for the five rules: implied integers {4, 2, 100000}, exchange {8, 4, 100000}. The motive: 25.1% of HiGHS's presolve time on the 31 is stage runs that remove nothing. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the budget of the singleton family, {8, 4, 100000}, never binds: none of its 457 calls stops on it and none reports limited; the largest work of the family over a whole presolve is 2.17 passes of the input model (blp-ic98; recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac/default). The campaign runs one seed in ten with tiny budgets. What the family charges is AS-327.
- **Set by:** The owner for the principle (change 4 of the Stage 0 report, accepted with decision 6, HISTORY Entry 13); code for the values (HISTORY Entry 14: "all budget defaults are placeholders"). The principle entered the plan as part of the block of thirteen changes, not one by one; the values were fixed during the six hours of independent work, without a check-in.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 2.1 (lines 317-320) and section 3 (line 495); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:80-99, 113-122; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:101; lowworth_v2_design.txt:70. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:80-99 and 113-122 (the singleton columns are line 120).
- **Switch:** `options_t::budget[rule]` = {passes, stall_passes, floor}; not settable from the tool or the adapter.
- **Alternatives:** Unlimited (run to a fixed point); values tuned per family; a larger stall limit.
- **May change:** A call that stops on its budget leaves reductions for the next round or undone and the fixed-point flag is lost, so the reduced model can be larger; the result stays deterministic. On an instance where most rows hold a slack with a cost the exchange family rescans many rows every round.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run with every budget times 10 on the 240 and the 9 instances above 1e7 nonzeros (remaining nonzeros, presolve seconds, count of calls stopped on a budget). If reduced models differ: standard cluster comparison, default budgets against times 10; the primal integral is the measure that sees the presolve time.
- **State:** in the engine (ten families, three of them still empty since commit `fa8430e24`); designed (two families).

### AS-122 Work counts one unit per matrix entry and eight per row or column visited
- **Assumption:** Budgets are in work units of the Stage 0 cost model: one unit per entry read or written and `line_visit_work` = 8 units per row or column visited; they never depend on time. The pass that scales a budget counts rows and columns with weight 8 because, with nonzeros alone, a first scan of a model with short rows would stall before every row was seen once.
- **Set by:** Design (the Stage 0 cost model measured a work unit at 10 ns and a row visit at 8 units; the correction of the pass is a finding of the review of the Stage 1 code structure, HISTORY Entry 14).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:21-23; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:80-84, 92-99; DESIGN.md section 2.1 (lines 322-338).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant `line_visit_work`).
- **Alternatives:** Other weights; a budget in seconds (not deterministic).
- **May change:** How the budgets of AS-121 cut on models with many short rows.
- **Test at the end:** Covered by the budget test of AS-121.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-123 Work ceilings of the column families are reports only until measured
- **Assumption:** Proposed for the four new families together: at most 2 passes of the input model in the median and 6 at the 90% quantile, no instance on the round limit, at most 5 instances losing the fixed-point flag. The review showed that 2 passes is about the cost of the first scan (median 1.70 to 2.03 passes on the 240 input models), so ceilings are stated per family and only reported until the milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] has measured them. Time and work of the two families of the five rules were not measured either. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; work in passes of the input model, nonzeros + 8 * (rows + columns); recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac/default): the singleton family over a presolve has a median of 0.45, 0.99 at the 90% quantile and 2.17 at most (blp-ic98, then blp-ar98 2.10, icir97_tension 1.90, mushroom-best 1.87, germanrr 1.47); its first call 0.40 / 0.88 / 1.94, all later calls 0.00 / 0.31 / 0.69 (neos-1122047). The 90% quantile is taken by interpolation here; the comparison script takes the 217th of the 240 values and prints 1.00, 0.89 and 0.32 for it. No instance is above 2.2, none ends on the round limit and none loses the fixed-point flag. In work units the family takes 126,751,280 of 2,041,111,657 (6.2%; all families 1,901,179,984 before, +7.4%); 89% of the rows it visits hold no live singleton column and cost one visit and no scan (28.4 million units, 22% of its work). All families together are at 5.70 / 9.53 / 24.51 passes (before 5.06 / 8.30 / 23.75) with the quantile of the script; by interpolation the 90% quantile is 9.43 (before 8.29). The 40 were not run.
- **Set by:** Taken as recommended (HISTORY Entry 40: "work ceilings are reports until measured"; v2_amendments.txt:74-76, :90).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:207, :281; v2_amendments.txt:74-76, :90; v2_reviews.txt:91-94; lowworth_v2_design.txt:103, :231. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:195-200 (`first_work`); gpu_presolve/analysis/bench240_stage1c.py:946-962.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one if a ceiling is to become a gate (a limit in a gate script); today the figures are only reported.
- **Alternatives:** Hard ceilings as gates.
- **May change:** Presolve time, not the reduced model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: work in passes per family on the 240 and the 40 at each milestone.
- **State:** measurement only; measured for the singleton family on the 240 as local dumps.

### AS-124 The time limit is only an abort; it never changes which reductions are tried
- **Assumption:** A wall-clock limit only ends the presolve with the status "limit" and a valid partial result, and it is tested before every call of a family; budgets and stall stops count work, not time. The default is no limit.
- **Set by:** Design (for determinism, DESIGN.md section 2.5).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 2.5 (lines 467-469); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:147-149; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:237-243, 481-483.
- **Switch:** `options_t::time_limit` (default infinite); tool `--time-limit <seconds>`. Inside cuOpt it is the remaining solve time (AS-155).
- **Alternatives:** A time budget per phase (not deterministic); a work limit in place of a time limit; a cap on presolve time as a share of the solver's limit.
- **May change:** With a finite limit the outcome depends on the speed of the machine, and inside cuOpt a presolve that hits the limit hands the solver the unreduced problem (AS-156).
- **Test at the end:** Not a pair: report presolve seconds and the count of "limit" results per arm.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-125 The dual column rules run once per round, not inside the cascade
- **Assumption:** Dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias are called once per round on the listed columns and the columns of listed rows. HiGHS reruns its dual-fixing extensions after every change (301.6 s against 157.3 s with them off on the 240).
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:72, :120).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:72, :120.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Inside the cascade, as HiGHS.
- **May change:** Findings reach the row rules one round later; more rounds against the round limit.

- **State:** designed (milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]).

### AS-126 Simple probing applies the aliases of a call as one batch through the alias forest
- **Assumption:** The aliases of all rows of a call are edges of the doubleton family's alias forest. A row is taken only when no two of its free columns are already connected, a row with a cut edge stays (an integer column is never mapped to a continuous root), and the forest picks the root, which need not be the binary.
- **Set by:** Design (repair of a work bound, v2_answers.txt item 24).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:27; v2_answers.txt:95-98; v2_reviews.txt:33-37.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** One row at a time with the binary as root.
- **May change:** Which column represents a group; rows deferred to the next round.
- **Test at the end:** No quality pair; a work and determinism choice. Alias depth and entries moved are reported at the milestone of simple probing [M2].
- **State:** designed (milestone of simple probing [M2]).

### AS-127 The free singleton rule takes the smallest column index and finishes chains inside one call
- **Assumption:** Of several singleton columns of a row that pass, the smallest column index is substituted; a column that the deletion makes a singleton is visited in the same call; new costs follow the cancellation rule (zero within 1e-12 relative) and must be finite. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): built as designed; the 457 calls of the family make 584 passes over its lists (most in one presolve on snp-02-004-104, 37). A chain is behind the loss to the growth limit on physiciansched3-3: two refused columns (ratio 1001) start a chain there, and with the limit at 2000 the engine makes 194 substitutions instead of 3 and equals the reference (AS-094). A new cost that would not be finite, or a cost that underflows, leaves the column in the model (AS-321). Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only): `singleton_order` = `largest_pivot` changes 0 of 240 reduced models in default and in primal-only mode, although 6,916 substitutions happen in rows with two or more singleton columns (6,866 on savsched1; counter `pivots_among_several`). `single_pass` changes one instance in each mode, snp-02-004-104 (default mode 354,940 / 77,337 / 174,980 to 355,027 / 77,368 / 175,008 in nonzeros / rows / columns; primal-only mode 355,046 / 77,357 / 175,101 to 355,118 / 77,383 / 175,119): it ends on the round limit of 20 rounds, is larger and loses its fixed-point flag (233 flags against 234, and 232 against 233; 731 rounds in the sum against 707). In the campaign with a value pinned on every seed, 5,040,000 models each, `largest_pivot` changes 0 results (the generator has no row with two singleton columns that both pass, AS-355) and `single_pass` 623, and none fails. The 457 calls and 584 passes above are now counters of the engine.
- **Set by:** Design (chains: v2_answers.txt item 22). HISTORY Entry 54 has the order in its table for the owner's decisions with the words that no reduced model changes in size; that holds for `largest_pivot` and not for `single_pass`, with which snp-02-004-104 is larger. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:34; v2_answers.txt:87-90. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:123-127 and 415-417 (smallest index), 554-559 (the lists are served until they stay empty) and 219-229 (new costs); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:421-424 and 431-433. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:95-114 and :267; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:553-567 (`order_pivots`), 612-616 (the column of a visit) and 816-819 (the end of a single-pass call); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (field "switches", row 6).
- **Switch:** `options_t::singleton_order` (AS-116; tool flag `--singleton-order <index|largest-pivot|zero-cost-first|single-pass>`): `largest_pivot` for the column of a row (default `smallest_index`), and `single_pass` for one link of a chain per round (by default the lists are served until they stay empty). The rule of the new costs has no switch yet: its tolerance is `options_t::tol.cancellation` (default 1e-12, AS-080), and a cost that is not finite leaves the column without a switch (AS-321).
- **Alternatives:** The column with the largest pivot: built, `largest_pivot` (equal coefficients go to the smaller index). One link of a chain per round: built, `single_pass`. Not built: the smallest growth ratio including the factor of earlier substitutions.
- **May change:** Which column of a row is removed and how large the growth factor gets (AS-094).
- **Test at the end:** No quality pair; covered by the pair of the free singleton reductions (AS-050). Since commit `f05d48e31`: for `singleton_order` = `smallest_index` against `largest_pivot` the hashes of the reduced models are equal on 240 of 240 as local dumps on the Mac in both modes, and the same on the node closes that pair; `smallest_index` against `single_pass` differs on snp-02-004-104 in both modes, so by the closing rule that pair goes to the standard cluster comparison, read on that instance.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`: the smallest index and whole chains by default, the largest pivot and one link per round with an option).

### AS-128 Dominance between parallel columns follows fixed type rules and a fixed order of cases
- **Assumption:** An integer absorber needs an integer moved column and an integral ratio; a continuous absorber takes either type; a continuous column is never moved onto an integer. The four cases are tried in a fixed order, one pair at a time, ties to the smaller column index. HiGHS has the same four cases.

- **Alternatives:** Prefer fixing the integer column; another case order.
- **May change:** Which of two parallel columns is fixed and at which bound.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison after the milestone of dominance between parallel columns [M3]: `parallel_dominance` on against off.
- **State:** designed (milestone of dominance between parallel columns [M3]).

### AS-129 A parallel pair is merged only when no dominance case applies
- **Assumption:** The parallel-column family tries the dominance fixing first and the merge only for pairs with equal cost that no dominance case takes; in primal-only mode pairs go straight to the merge. In a bucket of singleton columns only neighbours by cost over coefficient are tested for a merge.

- **State:** designed (milestones of dominance between parallel columns [M3] and of the merge of parallel columns [M5]).

### AS-130 The single-lock alias applies all candidates of one binary in one transaction
- **Assumption:** All candidates of one side that pass are aliased to the binary in one transaction; the candidate that is the binary itself is tested afterwards on the new state, and a column that became the target of an alias in a call is not aliased in that call. For two binaries that could each be aliased to the other, exactly one alias is expected.

- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** One alias per call.
- **May change:** Which of two mutually aliasable binaries stays.
- **Test at the end:** A unit test; no quality pair.
- **State:** designed (milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4]).

### AS-131 The implied equation runs between dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias
- **Assumption:** Inside the dual column family every candidate column is tested first by dual bound strengthening, then by the implied equation; the single-lock alias collects its rows afterwards. The implied equation therefore takes two-entry lock rows that the single-lock alias would otherwise alias.
- **Set by:** Design (lowworth_v2_design.txt:86, :156; the effect on the alias counts was pointed out by the worth verifier).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:86, :156, :228; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:111-112.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for the order; the designed `implied_equations` off removes the effect.
- **Alternatives:** Single-lock alias first; a family of its own for the implied equation (the first design).
- **May change:** The verifier expects "the same alias with the row gone" as outcome, but the alias counts measured at the milestone of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4] move when the implied equation [L1] is built (1,908 integer sources of this kind on bab6, 206 on netdiversion, input models).
