### AS-205 With its new rules off, a version must give the results of the version before
- **Assumption:** A version with the rules of a milestone switched off must give the same status, sizes, hash of the reduced model, records and fixed-point flag as the version before it on the 240 and the 40. This is what makes two arms that differ in one switch a clean comparison. ### AS-218 Quality against HiGHS is to be measured by cuOpt, presolve off, on HiGHS-presolved models
- **Assumption:** To compare formulation quality with HiGHS inside the solver, the plan adds a cluster arm in which cuOpt with its own presolve off solves the models HiGHS presolved. HiGHS writes its presolved model power-of-two scaled and cannot read indicator constraints, so that arm sees scaled models and misses such instances; with cuOpt's presolve off its probing cache and block BVE do not run either. A tool writes the engine's reduced model as an MPS file for the same kind of run. Nothing of it has been run. Until the HiGHS arm is written before HiGHS's final scaling, the two arms differ in scaling as well as in the presolver.

For the two arms to differ in the presolver only, the HiGHS arm has to use the reduced model before HiGHS's final scaling (the baseline tool writes it before the stage `final_scaling`, or undoes the scaling), so that both MPS files are unscaled. Until that tool change exists the pair is not a one-switch test and is to be marked so. The pairs that switch one HiGHS rule (AS-014, AS-113) are not affected, because both of their arms are scaled by HiGHS in the same way.
- **State:** planned.

### AS-220 The two arms of the main comparison differ in presolve seconds as well as reductions

### AS-221 Correct means structure check, crush check and solver check pass; solver quality never decides
- **Assumption:** A result counts as correct when three checks pass: the structure check (reads the postsolve records and proves that no reduction relaxes), the crush check (known feasible points of the original model are carried into the reduced model and back; a result without a dual finding, which primal-only mode guarantees, must give the point back unchanged) and the solver check (HiGHS on the LP relaxations and the reduced MIP). Whether a reduction is right is decided by these, the unit tests, the random campaign and wrong engines the checks must catch, never by a solver result; a rule that leaves a new postsolve record comes with the check extension that reads it.
- **Set by:** Design (after the audit of HISTORY Entry 17, Entries 20, 21 and 25; item 8 of the gate in v2_design.txt:209). Entries 17 to 21 belong to the six hours of independent work, without a check-in.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/gprs_check.cpp:8-46; gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:10-16; gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:72-82; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:136, :155, :190, :209.
- **Switch:** flags of gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py: `--primal-only`, `--lp-time-limit`, `--mip-time-limit`, `--require-points`, `--cost-variants`, `--push-points`.
- **Alternatives:** Exact solves of both models; a certificate per reduction; the cluster run as a correctness test as well.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; the end test over solution quality is read as a statement about strength only.
- **Test at the end:** In every cluster arm count wrong statuses and postsolved solutions that fail the original model, apart from the quality measures; both counts must be zero.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-222 The correctness checks pass violations up to their own tolerances
- **Assumption:** The checks accept what the table lists. A point of a solution file is used when it meets the original model within 1e-7 with integer columns within 1e-5 of integers; violations are relative to the largest term of the row or 1. The structure check counts a row whose test would cost too much, or that is made of more than 16 equations, as not tested, neither passed nor failed (0.19% of such rows in one campaign of 132 million generated models). Measured with milestone M1 (the constants that came with the records of the singleton-column rules are the last row of the table): in the campaign of 20,160,000 release models per option 32, 170 and 159 snapshots of interval records were not tested (no option, `--singletons`, `--columns`); on the 240 as local dumps on the Mac 0 snapshots and 0 kept rows were not tested, 17,216 boxes and 17,901 snapshots were tested, and the largest scaled violation of a box is 5.6e-17. The saved row of an interval record is tested at a replayed point only when the rest lies inside the sides of the snapshot, and the objective test takes a held cost below `composed_zero` of its parts as zero; each of the two removed a false alarm on a named campaign model.

| Name | Value | Place |
|---|---|---|
| `point_tolerance`, `point_integrality` (a stored point is used) | 1e-7, 1e-5 | gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:76-77 |
| `check_tolerance` (derived points) | 1e-6 | crush_check.hpp:78 |
| `identity_tolerance` (two objective values of one point, relative to the cost terms) | 1e-9 | crush_check.hpp:80 |
| `objective_tolerance` (values of different points; a proved reduced optimum against the known one) | 1e-6 relative | crush_check.hpp:82; gpu_presolve/check/gprs_check.cpp:794-796 |
| `structure_tolerance` (relative to the largest term and to the multiplier of an equation the row is made of) | 1e-5 | gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:147 |
| `max_kept_row_work`, `max_kept_work`, `max_row_equations` | 5e7, 2e10, 16 | structure_check.hpp:157-158, :161 |
| `solve_tolerance` of the oracle | 1e-9 | gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:59 |
| `integral_ratio` (a ratio taken as an integer where a saved row defines an integer column), `max_replay_points` (interval records that get a point of their own), `forcing_rounding` (rounding units of the terms within which a forcing value is an integer), `max_proof_visits` (steps of a proof walk, above which the obligation counts as not tested) | 1e-7, 8, 8, 1,024 | gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:243, :245, :248 and :1189, at commit `fa8430e24` |
- **Set by:** Design (the checks' authors and reviewers, HISTORY Entries 20, 21 and 31; not decided by the owner).
- **Where:** the places of the table; HISTORY Entry 31 (lines 1842-1866). At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:213-248, 1188-1189 and :1876.
- **Switch:** the constants of the table, such as `structure_tolerance`, `point_tolerance` and `check_tolerance` (check headers and oracle); no option.
- **Alternatives:** The engine's 1e-6 for the structure check; MIPLIB's 1e-6 for published points, judged against the violation the point came with; exact arithmetic on small models.
- **May change:** Nothing in the engine. A relaxation between 1e-6 and 1e-5 relative, or on a row that is not tested, passes the structure check; an objective error below 1e-6 relative passes; published points that violate rows by more than 1e-7 are rejected (AS-276).
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: wrong engines and the campaign show what a tolerance lets through; rerun the structure check on the 240 with 1e-6 and list what fails; report the count of rows not tested with every run.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-223 The solver-based check uses HiGHS with short limits, below 1e7 nonzeros only
- **Assumption:** Below 1e7 nonzeros the check solves the LP relaxation of the original and the reduced model (120 s limit) and the reduced MIP with gap 0 (60 s by default, 300 s in the node scripts). Above 1e7 nonzeros no solve is made. HiGHS can ignore its limit (it ran 35 minutes past a 120 s limit on var-smallemery-m6j6), so the tool leaves such a solve behind. A cut-off optimum is seen only where HiGHS proves the optimum of the reduced model: on 9 of the 31 with the merged engine (HISTORY Entry 39).

- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/gprs_check.cpp:44-45, 124-140, 489-499; gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py:57-66; gpu_presolve/tools/stage1_host_runs.sh:179-198; HISTORY Entries 16 and 39.

- **Alternatives:** Longer limits; another solver as the checker.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; how often a cut-off optimum can be seen.
- **Test at the end:** The end test adds what this check lacks: every postsolved cuOpt solution tested in the original model, and the optimal count against best known values on all 240.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-224 On the 240 a known objective value is a bound, not the optimum
- **Assumption:** On the testbed the objective of an instance is the published optimum unless marked with a star. On the 240 every value comes from cuOpt's table of best known values and is passed as an upper bound only, so a reduced optimum below it is not a failure there.

- **Test at the end:** The cluster's primal gap uses the same table, so both use one source of best known values.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-227 Every rule change needs a random campaign of tens of millions of models
- **Assumption:** Every rule change needs tens of millions of generated tiny models in the release and in the assertion build, with and without added singleton columns, judged by the exact oracle next to the structure check and, on one seed in four, by a second presolve of the reduced model, which must come back unchanged, because the one wrong reduction found so far occurred 30 times in 80 million models (6 under default options). Seeds are consecutive integers from a first seed, each used for 12 generator profiles; the options follow the seed's last digit (three in ten default, one each primal-only, one family or one column reduction off, a random subset of families and reductions with a random mode and a random `merge_integers`, rebuild always, rebuild never, tiny budgets, few rounds). The first seeds of past campaigns are not recorded in the files read. Measured with milestone M1 (on the Mac): the tool has a third option, `--columns`, with which the generator draws what the singleton-column rules act on (up to two singleton columns per row and four per model, most of them line columns, half of them without a cost, and forcing columns), and the option draw of a seed sets the two limits of the family to their tightest (`pivot_growth_limit` 1, `saved_row_factor` 0) on half of the seeds with the last digit 9. Campaigns of the milestone: the author 20,160,000 release and 10,080,000 assertion models for each of no option, `--singletons` and `--columns`, then 100,800,000 release models per option on fresh seeds; the validity reviewer 40,800,000 release models per option on the final tree (first seeds 9900000000, 9950000000, 10000000000) and 15,600,000 assertion models per option (first seeds 10100000000, 10150000000, 10200000000), with 13,994,580 second presolves of a fixed point in the release runs, none changed. That the generator reaches the rules: with `--columns` the 20,160,000 release models of the author give 693,407 results with a record of the free singleton column on an equation [R2], 476,581 on an inequality [R5], 117,202 of the zero-cost singleton rule [R3] and 809,706 of the forcing column [R4]. No failure of the four rules is left; the failures that remain are the base engine's (AS-259, AS-333). The campaign of the author found one wrong result of the new code, in 20 million models (AS-320). The first seeds of the author's campaigns are not in the reports. Measured with the node campaign of 5.4 billion models (engine commit `daa4f5d68`, 24 processes, 09:49 to 12:34 node time on 2026-11-28; HISTORY Entry 52): six runs, each over the 12 profiles. Release build, 100,000,000 seeds per profile and 1,199,999,808 models each: no profile option (first seed 1000000000000), `--singletons` (1000100000000) and `--columns` (1000200000000). Assertion build, 50,000,000 seeds per profile and 599,999,904 models each: first seeds 1000300000000, 1000400000000 and 1000500000000. That is 5,399,999,136 models. Oracle failures: 5, 4 and 4 in the release runs and 1, 4 and 0 in the assertion runs, 18 in all, one in 300 million models: 6 objective differences near 1e-9 (AS-259), 11 failures at points with huge entries (AS-333) and 1 lost optimum at an ordinary point (AS-358). No assertion fired; the structure check failed on no result and missed all 18, none of them a relaxation by the campaign's count; of 617,372,720 second presolves of a fixed point none changed the model. The one wrong answer at an ordinary point came once in 5.4 billion models, out of reach of the campaigns of the milestone on the Mac: the rule above, tens of millions of models per rule change, excludes a rate of about one in the number of models run and no rarer one.
- **Set by:** Design (a rule the assistant set after the finding of HISTORY Entry 18; written into gpu_presolve/tools/run_campaign.sh). The node campaign was started by the assistant (HISTORY Entry 50) and is recorded in HISTORY Entry 52, which has no message of the owner.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tools/run_campaign.sh:11-14, 35-40; gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:8-36, 62-166, 232-280, :342; HISTORY Entries 18, 19, 21, 25, 31 and 44. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:8-46 (the options and the line `columns`), 238-248 (`with_columns`), 250-307 (the option draw; the two limits are lines 299-302) and 379-386 (`GPRS_FUZZ_OFF`); gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:121-147; gpu_presolve/tools/run_campaign.sh:71-80. gpu_presolve/results/stage1c/campaign/s1c_m1_big1 (log.txt with the first seeds, and the totals `<run>.txt`); gpu_presolve/tools/node_campaign.sh; HISTORY Entry 52.
- **Switch:** gprs_fuzz `<first seed> <seeds per profile> [--singletons] [--columns] [--profile <name>]`; the environment variable `GPRS_FUZZ_OFF=<rule family>` switches a family off for every seed. Since commit `f05d48e31` also `[--switches] [--pin <value>]` (AS-348).

### AS-228 The check extensions designed for the nine rules accept what the rules' tolerances allow
- **Assumption:** Two designed check clauses fix what the checks accept. In primal-only mode a column removed by a free singleton record is compared with the original point up to the check tolerance plus the row residual and the difference of the saved columns, divided by the pivot (a pivot growth of up to 1000 is tolerated); columns of the zero-cost and forcing records and merged pairs are exempt. The structure check's own undo asks a saved row to hold only where the rest of the point lies inside the range recomputed from the record, so a wrong undo of the zero-cost rule is caught by the crush check and the oracle, not by this test. Measured with milestone M1 (on the Mac): both clauses are built for the records of the singleton-column rules. Clause 5 of the crush check exempts the columns of interval and forcing records, gives a column of a defined record the allowance of the amended formula, and an alias of such a column the scale times the move of its representative. The clause is generous: the allowance of a defined column contains its own residual, so the comparison of that column with itself is vacuous and only the other columns and the aliases behind it are tested; on a model of the validity reviewer a record with the pivot coefficient 0.5 or -1 instead of 1 still passes the round trip and the row test (the row violation of 1e-4 is below the relative tolerance). A wrong value of a defined column is caught by the replay of the structure check and by the oracle, not by this clause. On the 17 local dumps with points no result of the right engine fails it. The weakness of the postsolve part of the structure check is AS-331. The validity reviewer read the extended structure check (1,608 changed lines) in its contract and constants and attacked it through wrong engines, not line by line.
- **Set by:** Design (two binding amendments after the validity review, v2_amendments.txt:48-52 and 57-58).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:48-52, 57-58; v2_design.txt:155; v2_reviews.txt:13-16, 28-31. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:18-29 (clause 5) and 421-466 (reach kinds and allowance); gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:1831-1841, :1876 and 1916-1981 (the postsolve part).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if the stricter clause is wanted (exempt the column and require the saved row to hold); the two clauses are in the check code, without an option.
- **Alternatives:** Exempt the column and require the saved row to hold.
- **May change:** What the checks can detect for the singleton-column rules and the merge.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test.
- **State:** in the check code (commit `fa8430e24`) for the records of the singleton-column rules; designed for the merge of parallel columns [M5].

### AS-229 Cluster builds carry no engine assertions
- **Assumption:** The engine's assertions are compiled in only in cuOpt's assertion build. Instead of asserting on the cluster, the assertion build of the engine and the structure check run on all 240 on the dev node, which takes minutes (an abort on the cluster loses a batch of 40 instances). It is assumed that this screen is repeated for the exact engine version and options of each cluster arm. Every precondition of the state operations and the consistency check after a rebuild are such assertions, and the status "failed" is never returned, so a broken invariant in a build without them goes on silently. Measured with milestone M1 (a wrong engine of the validity reviewer, on the Mac): `push_forcing_col` with no saved row is guarded by an assertion only, so a wrong engine that takes the infinite side of every row of a forcing column ends the release build with a segmentation fault instead of failing a check; and before the repair of the reviewer a cost that underflows tripped an assertion in the assertion build and was dropped silently in the release build (AS-321).
- **Set by:** Taken as recommended (DESIGN.md amendment A5, HISTORY Entry 23: "So the cluster build needs no engine assertions. This replaces my recommendation of Entry 22"; the owner had asked what the open point meant in Entry 22 and did not decide).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A5 (lines 62-64); cpp/CMakeLists.txt:642-647; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:70-74; gpu_presolve/tools/stage1c_engine_runs.sh:219-224; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/assert.hpp:16-23; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:179-180; gpu_presolve/CMakeLists.txt:15; DESIGN.md section 8 (a failure is an assertion in the standalone project and in benchmark commits). At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/postsolve.cpp:428-432 (`push_forcing_col`).
- **Switch:** the CMake option `DEFINE_ASSERT` of the cuOpt build; `-DGPRS_ASSERT=ON` for the standalone build.
- **Alternatives:** Assertions on in the cluster build; cheap checks that stay in a release build, with a fallback to the unreduced problem.
- **May change:** Inside cuOpt the engine gets the model as cuOpt hands it over, which is not byte for byte the dump tested on the dev node (AS-281); a broken invariant in a release build goes on silently and can give a wrong reduced model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: one assertion-build run of the presolve-only report inside cuOpt on the 240 before the end test, and the dev-node screen for every arm's engine commit and options.
- **State:** measurement only; the screen was run on the merged Stage 1 engine and on the engine with the groundwork (HISTORY Entry 46).

### AS-347 The counters of the singleton family are always on: what a counter counts, and what counting costs
- **Assumption:** Since the switches of milestone M1 the refusal counters of the review are statistics of the engine: `column_stats_t::counts`, 47 counters named in the namespace `column_count`, filled by the family per call and summed by the engine over the calls. They have no switch: every presolve counts. Counting reads the state and spends no work units, so the results at the defaults are those of the engine before. What a counter counts was fixed by the implementer of the switches. A column that is tried and stays counts once per visit of its row, at the first test that fails, in the order of the tests, so the sums count visits and not columns (the two older statistics `growth_refused_row` and `growth_refused` still count each column once per presolve). On an inequality a refusal by the implied-bound test counts once more as `refused_ahead` or `refused_behind`, decided from the state as it is and without a fresh sum (an end that would need one counts as not implied). `zero_cost_integer_integral` asks for exact integer ratios and for all other free columns to be integer, not for the side. A cost counts as small up to 1e-7 and a moved cost as nearly cancelled within 1e-9; both constants decide no reduction. `rows_visited` and `pivots_tried` are sums of other counters, taken at the end of a call. With `dual_integer_pivots` off an integer singleton column on an inequality whose cost is not zero counts in `refused_integer` before the side, cost and implied-bound tests, whatever else would refuse it (one with a cost of zero counts in `refused_zero_cost`, the test before), so the other refusal counters of such a run cannot be compared column by column with a default run. The columns that are left at the end are counted by a tool step and not in the engine: gprs_presolve with `--left` presolves the reduced model once more with the same options and one round and reads the counters of that one call of the family. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, release build, default options, one process, minimum per instance, summed; other processes kept the machine at a load average of 6 to 12): the counting makes the family itself slower by about 9% (0.116 s to 0.127 s and 0.112 s to 0.123 s in the two runs of the implementer; 9.2% to 12.6% in the runs of the reviewer); the increase is about 0.12% of the total. It is largest where the family visits many light rows and finds nothing (thor50dday 7.1 ms to 8.7 ms; AS-335). In the sum the engine with the switches is +0.7% and +0.2% in the two runs of the implementer, where two copies of one binary differ by 0.2%, and -0.0% and -0.3% for the final commit in two runs of the reviewer (copies of the two binaries +0.5% and +0.3%); a first run of the reviewer with unbalanced positions gave +2.1% and is not trusted by its author. Work units are identical (2,041,111,657 over all families on the 240). The sums of the M1 worth report are reproduced exactly by the engine's counters (457 calls, 584 passes, 4,004,898 rows visited, 452,152 rows scanned with 10,497,776 slots, 557,325 columns tried). Not timed on the node when the entry was written; the time is a step of the node chain that is running (AS-357).
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches). HISTORY Entry 54 records that the counters are statistics of the engine and does not name their cost; the timing is in both reports (field "timing" of the implementer, field "evidence" of the reviewer). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:65-163 (the counters and what each counts); gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:31-33 (the two sizes), 102-105 (`count`), 189-205 (`lower_end_implied`), 329-330, 408-428 (`integral_singletons`) and 821-835 (the two sums); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_presolve.cpp:23-28 and 272-278 (`--left`); gpu_presolve/tools/time_multi.py; gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_switches.cpp:698-819 (four tests of the counters, among them `switches_counters_spend_no_work`); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "counters", "timing" and "assumptions") and attack_switches.json (fields "evidence" and "assumptions").
- **Switch:** no switch: the counters are always on (`column_stats_t::counts`; gprs_presolve prints them as "column_counts"). The tool flag `--left` of gprs_presolve adds the count of what is left ("left" and "second" in its report).
- **Alternatives:** A build or an option without the counters; count each column once per presolve; count with the tolerances of `integral_for`; a statistic of what is left that the engine computes after its last round; test the type last when `dual_integer_pivots` is off.
- **May change:** Presolve time by about a tenth of the family's share (the family is 1.4% of the engine time on the 240, AS-335); nothing in the reduced model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the time of the engine with and without the switches on the node, on the 240 and the 40 (AS-357), and the counters repeated there, where the sums must be those of the Mac.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `f05d48e31`); timed on the Mac only.

### AS-348 The campaign draws the options from a stream of its own: `--switches`, `--pin`, and a table that is append-only
- **Assumption:** With the flag `--switches` the campaign tool draws other values of the choices of the singleton-column rules for a seed: six seeds in ten get one other value, three get each value with probability 0.3, and one keeps the defaults. The values come from a random stream that is seeded by the seed alone and read by nothing else, after the option variant of the seed, so without the flag every seed gives the model, the options and the output lines of the engine before. Every result is compared with that of the presolve without the drawn values: it counts as changed when the status, the hash of the reduced model, the hash of the stack or the postsolved images of two points of the reduced model differ. The table of the drawn values (`switch_values`) is indexed by the draw, so it is append-only: an entry that is removed or inserted changes what every seed draws. That happened once: the reviewer removed the three entries of the removed option values (AS-340, AS-341), the table has 17 entries (it had 20), and a seed of `--switches` draws other values than with the implementer's commit `55b639503`; the model and the older options of a seed are unchanged. The flag `--pin <value>`, which the reviewer added, gives every seed that one value and draws nothing. When two values of one choice are both drawn, the later one of the table stands. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (campaigns on the Mac): per value about 3% of the models have it alone and 9% among several. The models and the older options are equal to those of the engine before on 18,000,000 traced models and on the four known seeds. With the drawing the reviewer ran 60,480,000 release and 30,240,000 assertion models on the implementer's commit and 15,120,000 and 7,560,000 on the final engine, and with each of the 17 values pinned 85,680,000 release models on the final engine: no oracle failure, no structure failure, and no second presolve of a fixed point changed. The one failure of the implementer's own campaign is AS-356; the values that the generator never reaches are AS-355. The first seeds of these campaigns are not in the two reports (AS-227 asks for them with every campaign), except 7,700,000,000 and 7,900,000,000 for the identity runs on random models and the seed of the one failure.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches wrote the draw and its shares; the reviewer removed three table entries and added `--pin`). HISTORY Entry 54 names the draw only in its lesson on the values that the generator cannot reach (AS-355). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:47-57 (the usage text), 321-354 (the table, 17 entries), 356-373 (`draw_switches`; the stream is line 360), 375-398 (`same_result`), 448-459 and 512-518 (`--pin`); gpu_presolve/tools/run_campaign.sh:80-93 (the line "switches"); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "assumptions" and "for_merge") and attack_switches.json (fields "assumptions", "evidence" and "for_merge").
- **Switch:** tool flags `--switches` and `--pin <value>` of gprs_fuzz (a value is a name of the table, for example `no_dual_integer_pivots`); the table `switch_values` and the shares (6, 3 and 1 seeds in ten; probability 0.3) are in the code, without a flag.
- **Alternatives:** Other shares; more values per option (the ends of the ranges); keep a removed entry as a value that sets nothing, so that old seeds keep their draw.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; which option values a campaign of a given size covers, and whether a seed of an older campaign with `--switches` can be run again (only with the commit that drew it, or with `--pin`).
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: every later option of the engine is appended to the table with a wrong engine that only its value catches, and the campaign of each milestone runs once with `--switches`.
- **State:** measurement only (commit `f05d48e31`).

### AS-349 The identity tool holds copies of the campaign's tables
- **Assumption:** The identity tool (gprs_ident with run_ident.sh) compares two builds of the engine on random models and on dumps: per model it prints the hash of the reduced model, the fixed-point flag, the rounds, the rebuilds, a hash of the record stack field by field and every statistic that the engine before the switches has (it uses only what the engine had at milestone M1, so that it compiles against the older tree: its lines hold none of the 11 options, of the 47 counters or of the two undo choices of the stack, AS-360). Its random models and option variants are meant to be those of the campaign, and it holds copies of the campaign tool's profile table and of its functions `add_singletons`, `with_columns` and `variant`. The reviewer of the switches compared the copies text for text: equal except two comments. Nothing enforces it. When a later milestone adds a profile, a profile option, a generator knob or an option variant to the campaign and not to the copy, the identity tool compares other models than the campaign runs; the comparison of two builds on the models that it has stays valid. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (on the Mac): with the tool the implementer's commit and the final commit equal the engine before on 8,064,000 random models per build each (0 of 1,344 chunk hashes differ in each of 12 comparisons per build) and on the 17 local dumps in 8 option variants per dump and three modes (136 lines per mode, 0 differ), in the release and in the assertion build.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches, who took the tool into the repository and notes the copies itself; the reviewer compared them). HISTORY Entry 54 does not name it. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_ident.cpp:22-24, :227, :330, :342 and :400 (the copies); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:84-188 (the profile table), 193-260 (`add_singletons`, `with_columns`) and 267-319 (`variant`); gpu_presolve/tools/run_ident.sh; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "other_defects", item 9, "identity" and "for_merge") and impl_switches.json (fields "unsure" and "for_merge").
- **Switch:** no switch: needs one only if the tables are shared (one header with the profile table and the option variants that both tools include).
- **Alternatives:** A shared header; a test that compares the two tables; the campaign tool itself printing the identity lines.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; whether an identity claim of a later milestone (AS-205) is made on the models of that milestone's campaign.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: at every milestone that touches the campaign's tables, the copies are compared again or replaced by a shared header.
- **State:** measurement only (commit `f05d48e31`; the copies are equal today).

### AS-350 The stored runs of the switches are reduced summaries; which values were measured
- **Assumption:** Every measurement of an option value on the 240 as local dumps is stored as a reduced summary, gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_switches/<label>/summary.json: 41 files, made by the reviewer of the switches from commit `a017fa221` (whose sources are those of `f05d48e31`) with a clean tree. They are the 37 that remain of the implementer's 43, equal to the implementer's in every instance and figure, and 4 for the growth limit (`pivot_growth_limit_2000`, `pivot_growth_limit_off`, `growth_ratio_continuous_limit_2000`, `growth_ratio_integer_exempt_limit_2000`); the six of the removed values are deleted. A reduced summary keeps of each instance the name, band, status, sizes, reduced sizes, hash, fixed-point flag, rounds and objective nonzeros and what the singleton family reports (its line of the rules without seconds, its four reductions, the column statistics, the counters, and "left" and "second" where they were run); it drops the seconds, the other families and every zero counter, so a missing counter is zero. Which values were measured: every named value of every option; of the options with a range, `forcing_rounding_units` 0 (and 64, removed) and `side_guard_scale` 0.01, 2 and 8 (and the band factors 2 and 5, removed), not the ends of the ranges (1e-3 and 11). The options that act in the undo or on projections (the start and the order of the interval undo, the forcing band, the two zero-cost options, `zero_cost_first`) were measured in primal-only mode only together with `primal_projections`. Each run is presolve only, on the Mac, with one repetition. After the next milestone these are the runs of the engine before it. Measured with the switches of milestone M1: the assistant rebuilt commit `f05d48e31` from a clean export on the Mac and reproduced the stored runs of three option values (`dual_integer_pivots` off, `primal_projections` on in primal-only mode, `growth_ratio` continuous) on 240 of 240, and the engine before at the defaults on 240 of 240 in default and in primal-only mode.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches for the form of the reduced summary and the choice of the values; the reviewer for the 41 files that are stored). HISTORY Entry 54 records that one run per value is stored and that the assistant reproduced three of them. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_switches (41 directories with one summary.json each); gpu_presolve/tools/reduce_summary.py; gpu_presolve/tools/switch_table.py (runs the 240 once per value and prints the table); gpu_presolve/tools/run_engine.py; gpu_presolve/tools/compare_runs.py; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "tools" and "assumptions") and attack_switches.json (fields "evidence" and "for_merge").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: gpu_presolve/tools/switch_table.py with `--store` writes the reduced summary of each run; the values are set with the flags of gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp and with `--pivot-growth-limit`.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the zeros (about 20 MB for 43 files, by the implementer) or the full summaries; measure the ends of the ranges; measure every value in primal-only mode without the projections as well.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; what can be compared later without a rerun: the times and the other families of these runs are not stored.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the same table from the node, on the 240 and the 40 (AS-357); the hashes on the 240 must be those of the stored runs.
- **State:** measurement only (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; not repeated on the node).

### AS-351 The structure check accepts the records of the projections in a primal-only result only with the options of the presolve
- **Assumption:** The structure check has to know two options of the presolve to judge a result. It accepts interval and forcing records in a result of primal-only mode only when it is given options with `primal_projections`, and a saved row of an interval record with one finite side only with `zero_cost_one_sided`; without them it fails such a result, as before. The older form of the call (model, result, primal-only) still compiles, with the two new flags off by default, and an overload takes `options_t`; the campaign tool, the check tool and the test fixture call the overload. A caller that passes the options of another presolve than the one that made the result gets a wrong verdict, in either direction. Measured with the switches of milestone M1: unit tests check both the acceptance and the refusal; on the 240 as local dumps on the Mac the structure check of primal-only mode with the projections passes with 13,213 defined, 117 forcing and 17,901 interval records, and with one-sided rows as well with 24,465 interval records.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches). HISTORY Entry 54 does not name it. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:31-41 (the contract of the records), 419-431, 557-558, 598-600 and 2125-2132 (the overload that takes the options); gpu_presolve/tests/test_columns_support.hpp:95 (the fixture passes its options); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "assumptions" and "for_merge").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the options that are passed to `gprs_check::structure_check` (`options_t::primal_only`, `primal_projections`, `zero_cost_one_sided`).
- **Alternatives:** Accept the records always; carry the two options in the result or in the stack, so that the check cannot be given other ones.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; a check that is run without the options of the presolve fails right results of the two option values.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: every arm of the end test that sets one of the two options runs the structure check with the options of that arm.
- **State:** in the check code (commit `f05d48e31`).

## 7. Findings that later choices rest on

### AS-233 Finding: the Stage 1 engine is at or above HiGHS with the same rule set
- **Assumption:** Against HiGHS restricted to our Stage 1 rule set the engine leaves fewer nonzeros on 18 of the 240, the same on 220 and more on one (traininstance2, 3 nonzeros), ratio 1.002; on the 40 the ratio is 1.0028 (0.9955 before the row addition and the rounds on explicit bounds), with nothing below 0.95. Stage 1 is taken as passed on that basis and the cheap column rules build on it. The three counts of the log add up to 239; the nine-rule design gives 18, 221 and 1. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): traininstance2 stays one row and three nonzeros behind, before and after the milestone and with any growth limit (21,183 nonzeros, 5,472 rows and 2,746 columns against 21,180, 5,471 and 2,746 at the ladder step `m1_a`; before the milestone 21,189 / 5,473 / 2,747 against 21,186 / 5,472 / 2,747 for HiGHS with the Stage 1 rule set). HiGHS finds one duplicate row there that the engine's path never produces: the engine's reduced model holds no pair of parallel rows. Which step of HiGHS creates it was not traced. It is the only instance behind `m1_a` that the growth limit does not explain (AS-094).
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entries 26, 28 and 39; Stage 1 is taken as passed on it).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A2 (lines 37-39); HISTORY Entry 28 (lines 1716-1718) and Entry 39 (lines 2184-2186); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:199. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac/default/summary.json; HISTORY Entry 48.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the finding is reproduced with gpu_presolve/analysis/stage1_compare.py; in the engine the Stage 1 rule set is `options_t::enabled` with the four column families off.
- **Alternatives:** None; it can be repeated.
- **May change:** If it stopped holding after a later change, the base of the Stage 1c gate and the per-instance lower bound would be wrong.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: rerun the Stage 1 comparison on the 40 and the 240 with the final engine restricted to the Stage 1 families.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-234 Finding: what each presolve leaves on the 240; belief that fewer nonzeros mean better quality
- **Assumption:** Presolve only, no scaling, geometric mean of remaining nonzeros on 237 of the 240: our Stage 1 engine 0.788, HiGHS with our Stage 1 rule set 0.789, HiGHS with all cheap rules 0.753, HiGHS full 0.676, PaPILO with cuOpt's reductions 0.689 (Entry 37, merged engine: 0.7885, with cuOpt's four 0.7862, PaPILO 0.6905, HiGHS full 0.6767). Over all 240: engine 0.790, PaPILO 0.616, HiGHS full 0.613. The distance to PaPILO is largest between 5e5 and 1e7 nonzeros (52 instances: 0.723 against 0.385). The order of the remaining work rests on these numbers, and the plan expects the solver outcome with the Stage 1 rules to be below PaPILO; no cuOpt run has tested either.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entries 27, 28 and 37; the expectation that the solver outcome is below PaPILO is the plan's, DESIGN.md section 13, line 797).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A8 (lines 93-97); HISTORY Entry 27 (lines 1657-1686), Entry 28 (lines 1724-1730), Entry 37 (lines 2131-2133).
- **Switch:** `presolve_gprs` for the test; the finding itself is reproduced with gpu_presolve/analysis/bench240_reductions.py and bench240_stage1c.py.
- **Alternatives:** A cluster run now, to learn early how reductions and quality relate.
- **May change:** It sets the size of the gap to close and the order of work; if quality follows other properties (added rows, integer merges, coefficient strength), the order may be wrong.
- **Test at the end:** The main comparison, which could be run once early as a calibration: `presolve_gprs` 0 against 3, scaling off, on the 240. Repeated after each stage it shows how quality follows size.
- **State:** measurement only; no cluster run exists.

### AS-235 Finding: HiGHS spends most of its presolve time in probing and enumeration for little size
- **Assumption:** In HiGHS's full presolve on the 240 (mean share of an instance's nonzeros removed; share of time): initial scan 10.9% (3%), parallel rows and columns 4.7% (6%), fast loop 4.2% (9%), sparsify 1.7% (0.4%), enumeration 1.3% (13%), pairwise dominated columns 0.8% (12%), probing 0.6% (52%), aggregator -0.3% of the nonzeros but 1.7% of the rows (0.1%). About a third of the gap from the engine to full HiGHS is cheap rules, which cost HiGHS 146 s more (302 against 156 s). Probing and enumeration decide single instances all the same (enumeration removes 56 to 76% on ex9, ex10, neos8 and sorrell3).
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entry 28, with the instrumented HiGHS; the owner chose the build order on it: "Yes, cheap rules first", Entry 29).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A8 (lines 97-98); HISTORY Entry 28 (lines 1732-1744); gpu_presolve/analysis/bench240_highs_stages.py.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a measurement with the instrumented HiGHS (gpu_presolve/analysis/bench240_highs_stages.py). On the HiGHS side the names `probing` and `enumeration` in `HIGHS_DIAG_OFF` give a pair before the phases are built (AS-113).
- **Alternatives:** None; a measurement of size, not of formulation strength.
- **May change:** The build order rests on it. Probing and cliques give fixings, implications and clique rows whose value for the solver is not in the nonzero count; ranking them last by size may postpone the part that strengthens most.
- **Test at the end:** Before the phases are built: the HiGHS family pairs of AS-113 for `probing` and `enumeration`. Once they exist: standard cluster comparison, wide phases off against on; the cluster run after the probing and clique stage against the run before it.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-236 Finding: four cheap rules carry the stage; the textbook column rules are worth nothing measurable
- **Assumption:** With one rule switched off in HiGHS (all cheap rules otherwise on, the 240, nonzeros without over with): parallel columns 1.010 (39 instances change), simple probing on equations 1.010 (8), free column substitution 1.009 (44), dual-fixing substitution 1.006 (40), zero-cost singleton 1.001 (16), implied-integer conversion 1.0003 (7), implied equations 1.0002 (11), dominated, weakly dominated and forcing column 1.0001 each, column stuffing 1.0000. Switching off HiGHS's whole dual fixing loses exactly what its substitution extension loses, so the lock rule the engine has is all of the rest. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; the engine with one reduction of the singleton family taken out, geometric mean of without over with over 237 of the 240 in nonzeros / rows / columns): free singleton column on an equation [R2] 1.0059 / 1.0015 / 1.0013 (38 instances change), zero-cost singleton columns [R3] 1.0086 / 1.0122 / 1.0134 (20), forcing column [R4] 1.0002 / 1.0005 / 1.0000 (3), free singleton column on an inequality [R5] 1.0010 / 1.0015 / 1.0013 (23). These are not the numbers of HiGHS above: there a rule is switched off with all cheap rules on, here with the Stage 1 rules and the singleton family only.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entry 29, table added to the entry).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 29 (lines 1789-1807); gpu_presolve/results/bench240/cheap_ablation.
- **Switch:** in the engine `options_t::sub_enabled` and `options_t::enabled` hold the switches of these rules (the four of the singleton family are read since commit `fa8430e24`); the finding comes from the HiGHS rule bits and names of AS-188.
- **Alternatives:** None; a measurement.
- **May change:** The build order of the nine rules and the first proposal to leave five rules out rest on it.
- **Test at the end:** Engine pairs with one reduction switched off each, first as size runs, then on the cluster for the rules that change many instances (AS-050).
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-237 Finding: the five further rules are worth about 0.06% in nonzeros on the 240
- **Assumption:** With all left-out rules switched off together in HiGHS on the 240: remaining nonzeros 1.0006, rows 1.0015, columns 1.0006, 19 instances change (largest: ns1208400 1.110, where the engine already has the smaller size by another route; neos-3656078-kumeu 1.029 and neos-2746589-doon 1.028, mostly the forcing column, which is among the nine required rules and is not built yet; csched007 1.016). In HiGHS's forms the five rules are what is left between the nine rules and all cheap rules: 0.9994 / 0.9985 / 0.9993 on 237 of the 240 and 0.9974 / 0.9998 / 0.9972 on the 40. Per rule, with HiGHS on 237 of the 240:

| Rule | Worth in HiGHS (nonzeros / rows / columns without over with) | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Implied equation | 1.00017 / 1.00079 / 1.00033, 11 instances | csched007 and roi5alpha10n8 are reached by the free singleton with an inequality pivot; without the two the bound is 0.00009 / 0.00013 / 0.00021 in the mean log |
| Conversion with scaling | 1.00030 / 1.00017 / 1.00009, 7 instances | without ns1208400 HiGHS ends smaller in nonzeros and columns without the conversion (blp-ic98 and blp-ar98 end 3% smaller); supportcase42 is the instance that needs it |
| Column scaling alone | no switch in HiGHS; by counters at most physiciansched3-3's 28 rows | built for completeness |
| Column stuffing | one column and one nonzero on sp97ar | seymour1 is not a stuffing instance (refuted by the worth verifier) |
| Single-equation extension | 1 row, 8 columns, 20 nonzeros on two instances | no change on either set when the other dual-fixing parts are on; it has a cost side (rd-rplusc-21) |
| Dominated-column tests beyond the forcing column | 1.00003 / 1.00003 / 1.00005, three instances | no change on the 40 although HiGHS credits 38,984,796 weakly dominated fixings there |
- **Set by:** Design (a finding measured on HiGHS by the design agents and recomputed by the worth verifier, HISTORY Entries 36 and 42; the owner saw the figure on the 240 and decided "Build them too", Entry 38).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 36 (lines 2082-2096) and Entry 42 (lines 2326-2347); stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:28, :35, :42, :49, :56, :63, :163-183; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:63, :71, 84-87, :106; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:31-35, 46-51.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; the eight designed switches of AS-011 (`implied_equations` to `dominated_columns`) are not in code. The finding comes from HiGHS reference runs with the names and rule bits of AS-188.
- **Alternatives:** None; if the engine's forms of the nine rules reach less than HiGHS's, the five could matter more or less.
- **May change:** This belief is the reason the rules were ranked last and the reason their tolerances and restrictions are chosen for safety, not reach. The size belief says nothing about solution quality.
- **Test at the end:** Engine sizes at the milestones of the five further rules [L1 to L5]; then the cluster pairs of AS-011.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-238 Finding: the rule ladder, and where the engine stands before the column rules
- **Assumption:** HiGHS with all cheap rules over HiGHS with a growing rule set, nonzeros / rows / columns, on 237 of the 240 and on the 40: Stage 1 set 0.9544 / 0.9640 / 0.9406 and 0.7986 / 0.8235 / 0.7634; with the singleton column rules 0.9726 / 0.9788 / 0.9562 and 0.9233 / 0.9113 / 0.9081; with simple probing 0.9826 / 0.9892 / 0.9772 and 0.9377 / 0.9355 / 0.9297; with parallel columns 0.9932 / 0.9907 / 0.9919 and 0.9396 / 0.9363 / 0.9340; with dual bound strengthening 0.9958 / 0.9937 / 0.9947 and 0.9529 / 0.9566 / 0.9486; with the binary substitution (the nine rules) 0.9994 / 0.9985 / 0.9993 and 0.9974 / 0.9998 / 0.9972. The engine of today against all cheap rules: 0.9563 / 0.9653 / 0.9424 on the 237 (39 / 19 / 32 instances below 0.95). Predicted after the milestone of the merge of parallel columns [M5] by measured single-rule losses: 0.9918 / 0.9874 / 0.9921 without neos-3402294-bobin and 0.9988 / 0.9994 / 0.9975 with it; the review computes 0.9900 / 0.9864 / 0.9898 with `range_kept` and without the engine's present lead. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac/default): the engine with the singleton-column rules against HiGHS with all cheap rules is at 0.9746 / 0.9810 / 0.9584 on 237 of the 240 (HiGHS's own ladder step with the singleton column rules: 0.9726 / 0.9788 / 0.9562), and the geometric mean of remaining over input nonzeros is 0.774 (0.788 before; for HiGHS with all cheap rules the recount from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/stage1c_refs/d3_cheap gives 0.754, where HISTORY Entry 48 and AS-234 have 0.753). The engine's gain from the four rules, 1.0191 / 1.0163 / 1.0170, is at least the gain that HiGHS gets from the same rules, 1.0191 / 1.0154 / 1.0166 (stored references `highs_stage1c` to `m1_a`). The 40 were not run.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entries 40 and 42, with two builds of the instrumented HiGHS; the end values are a prediction of the design, recomputed by the worth review).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A8 (lines 138-143); HISTORY Entry 40 (lines 2224-2229) and Entry 42 (lines 2326-2347); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:199, 219-223; v2_reviews.txt:77, 86-89.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a measurement of HiGHS reference runs (gpu_presolve/baseline/stage1c_reference_runs.sh); the engine's counterpart is `options_t::enabled` and `options_t::sub_enabled`.
- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** The milestone order and the acceptance values of each milestone come from it; the predicted margin is smaller than one family.
- **Test at the end:** Size runs per milestone; the cluster pair "four column families off against on" measures what the stage is worth in quality.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-239 Finding: two large instances carry the testbed distance, and the two sets rank rules differently
- **Assumption:** On the 40, HiGHS with all cheap rules over the engine of today is 0.8008 / 0.8238 / 0.7654 (nonzeros / rows / columns), carried by neos-4332810-sesia (HiGHS 407,609 nonzeros, the engine 34,283,539) and dlr2 (10,542,701 against 72,603,249); without them 0.9357 / 0.9210 / 0.9184 over 38. Both are reached by the singleton column rules followed by the lock rule. The dual-fixing extensions are worth 6% in nonzeros on the 40 and 0.6% on the 240. Measured with milestone M1: the size run on the 40 that this entry asks for was not made, because the node reservation had ended (AS-336). On the 15 of the 17 local dumps on the Mac that have a testbed reference at the ladder step `m1_a`, the engine equals the reference on 13 and is ahead on brazil3 and unitcal_7 (geometric mean 1.0007 / 1.0010 / 1.0004; before 0.9921 / 1.0000 / 0.9989); neos-4332810-sesia and dlr2 are not among the local dumps.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entry 41).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 41 (lines 2247-2265, 2296-2300).
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a measurement; the two instances are taken out of a mean with `--without neos-4332810-sesia,dlr2` in gpu_presolve/analysis/bench240_stage1c.py.
- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** It confirms the build order and is the reason both sets are asked for in order decisions.
- **Test at the end:** Size runs after the milestones of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] and of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4] on the 40.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-240 Finding: the zero-cost singleton rule followed by the lock rule reaches neos-3402294-bobin
- **Assumption:** With HiGHS restricted to our Stage 1 rule set plus only the zero-cost singleton rule (2 instances run), neos-3402294-bobin ends at 32,932 rows, 792 columns and 381,000 nonzeros, its size with all cheap rules on. That instance alone is a third of the summed gap in rows on the 240; the rows gate and the build order (singleton columns first) rest on it. The mechanism in the engine is a hypothesis until the milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] runs. Measured with milestone M1 (the local dump on the Mac): the engine reaches neos-3402294-bobin exactly, 381,000 nonzeros, 32,932 rows and 792 columns (2,003,808 / 577,300 / 2,904 before; recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac), by 264 zero-cost singleton columns in 132 records, then 1,848 lock fixings and 544,236 redundant rows in 16 cascade passes; it costs +0.071 s of presolve time there, spent in the row rules. The hypothesis holds in the engine; the run on the node is missing.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entry 40; v2_amendments.txt:25-32).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 40 (lines 2220-2223); stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:25-32; v2_design.txt:225, :260; gpu_presolve/baseline/stage1c_reference_runs.sh:72 (`BOBIN=8913312`).
- **Switch:** `options_t::sub_enabled[sub_rule::zero_cost_singleton]` in the engine (default on; tool `--off zero_cost_singleton`); the finding on HiGHS is a run with `--presolve-rule-off 8913312` (a bit mask of the rule bits 5, 7, 8, 19 and 23; `BOBIN` in gpu_presolve/baseline/stage1c_reference_runs.sh:72).
- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** Without bobin the predicted rows value ends at 0.9874, below the gate.
- **Test at the end:** A size run: the engine's own run on bobin after the milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1].
- **State:** measurement only; confirmed by the engine on the local dump (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-241 Belief: the nine rules reach the gate if the engine's forms reach what HiGHS's reach
- **Assumption:** In HiGHS's forms the nine rules are within 0.1% of all cheap rules in every measure on the 240 and within 0.3% on the 40, so the gate of 0.99 is taken as reachable with the nine rules. The engine's forms are weaker in six named ways: explicit bounds only in every test, no dual bound from another column of the row, the force test of the single-lock alias on the lock row only, exact zero cost for the zero-cost singleton and the forcing column, costs equal to 1e-12 relative for a merge, no single-equation extension. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): for the four rules of the singleton family the engine's forms reach what HiGHS's reach, 1.0020 / 1.0022 / 1.0022 against the ladder step `m1_a`, and of the six named weaker forms the three that concern these rules (explicit bounds only, no dual sign from another column, exact zero cost) cost no instance at that step: without the growth limit the engine equals or passes `m1_a` on every instance but traininstance2 (AS-094, AS-233). The other three forms belong to rules that are not built. A seventh difference does cost instances, which the list does not name: the growth limit of the free singleton column, which HiGHS does not have (5,270 columns on 9 instances, AS-094).
- **Set by:** Design (a belief that rests on a measurement on HiGHS, HISTORY Entry 42; the condition is stated there, and the weaker forms are listed in the risks of the design).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 42 (lines 2339-2342); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:211, :261.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one per weaker form; the six forms are entries of their own (AS-022, AS-027, AS-031, AS-096, AS-099, AS-038).
- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** The expectation that the gate is reachable; by measured single-rule losses the margin at the end is 0.002 without bobin and 0.006 to 0.009 with it.
- **Test at the end:** A size run after each milestone: the engine against HiGHS with all cheap rules, with the instances below 0.95 traced and the engine's findings per reduction compared with HiGHS's counters.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-242 Finding: part of the gap to HiGHS has no single-rule attribution
- **Assumption:** On 237 of the 240, 2.2 of the 10.6 summed log gap in nonzeros, 3.1 of 8.4 in rows and 2.2 of 14.1 in columns are explained by no single switch (bobin, neos-2746589-doon, neos-827175, unitcal_7, n5-3, icir97_tension and others, where rules stand in for each other). Whether the built rules reach them is unknown. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): of the named instances neos-3402294-bobin is reached exactly (AS-240), and neos-2746589-doon, icir97_tension and unitcal_7 are ahead of the ladder step `m1_a`; neos-827175 keeps 10,248 integer singleton columns with zero cost that the zero-cost singleton rule does not take (AS-029).

- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:217, :262; v2_reviews.txt:77.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: recomputed from the HiGHS runs with one rule off (`HIGHS_DIAG_OFF` and `--presolve-rule-off`, with the names and rule bits of AS-188).
- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** These instances are the first candidates for the list below 0.95.
- **Test at the end:** Size runs; each instance traced by a run that names the rule.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-243 Finding: in Stage 1 the time is in building the state, not in propagation
- **Assumption:** On the finished host engine (the 40, 75.0 s) building the state is 45.7% of the time, the rules 35.8% (parallel rows 11.9, doubleton equations 9.2, bound propagation 6.9, row tightening 5.4, row rules 2.1, dual fixing 0.3), writing the reduced model 8.1% and its hash 8.0%. The first propagation call holds 92.5% of the propagation work, and none of 392 later calls on the 17 local dumps would pass the rule that selects the GPU. Inside the state build the transpose with cross positions and locks is 86 to 94%.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding, HISTORY Entries 24 and 32).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md amendment A4 (lines 48-53); HISTORY Entry 24 (lines 1464-1489).
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a measurement, repeated on every run by gpu_presolve/analysis/overheads.py; the choice that rests on it is the size switch of the device state build (AS-304), which is not built.
- **Alternatives:** None; the shares may shift once the column rules, probing and cliques exist, and the later calls were measured on instances up to 3 million nonzeros only.
- **May change:** It moved the GPU work of Stage 1 from propagation to the state build (AS-304); if the shares change with later rules, that placement is wrong.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: gpu_presolve/analysis/overheads.py on every run shows whether the shares still hold; repeat the split on the 240 and the 40 after the column rules.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-244 Belief: below 500,000 nonzeros the GPU wins only on wide work; cascades stay on host
- **Assumption:** At 5e4 to 5e5 nonzeros a full matrix sweep costs 0.5 to 3.1 ms on one CPU thread and 0.06 to 0.24 ms on the GPU, and a device step costs 77 to 93 microseconds before any kernel work (Stage 0, one H200, 32 matrices from 71,477 nonzeros up). So the cascade below 5e5 nonzeros runs on the sequential host engine and the GPU is aimed at probing, cliques, pairwise tests and aggregator candidates. A batched probing round was not measured.
- **Set by:** Design (a belief that rests on the Stage 0 measurements, HISTORY Entries 8, 11 and 12; the conclusion is the plan's, change 1 of the Stage 0 report, which entered with the block of thirteen changes).
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 1 (lines 240-252) and section 2.1 (lines 312-314); gpu_presolve/results/stage0/STAGE0_REPORT.md section 6.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (the size switch between host and device, AS-306); the engine is host only today.
- **Alternatives:** GPU cascades at all sizes; another size boundary.
- **May change:** The division of work between host and device; measured on one GPU type and one CPU. Nothing in the reduced model if both engines give the same bits.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: a time measurement with the device forced on and off once a wide phase exists.
- **State:** measurement only. Overruled by the owner on 2026-11-28 (HISTORY Entry 51, plan amendment A11): Stage 1 gets a complete GPU acceleration, hybrid with the host for small and non-parallel work, before Stage 2 starts; the design round is `stage1_gpu_design`.

- **Assumption:** The plan expects parity through a complete rule set of its own and a surplus from coverage where HiGHS limits itself (its probing contingent starting at 1,000, the aggregator pass that ends after three fill-in failures, stages it runs once) and from clique strengthening. One source was measured: with HiGHS's probing limits off the 31 end with 1.8% fewer nonzeros in the geometric mean, for 15 times the probing time and 31 times the probes; the gain sits on supportcase7, bab6 and brazil3. The other two are not measured.
- **Set by:** Design (a belief of the plan); the one measurement is from the Stage 0 report, section 13.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 1 (lines 294-298) and section 13 (line 818); STAGE0_REPORT.md lines 626-656.
- **Switch:** no switch in the engine yet: needs one when probing is built; on the HiGHS side `HIGHS_DIAG_PROBING_NOSTOP=1`.
- **Alternatives:** No surplus from probing; a surplus only from rules HiGHS lacks.
- **May change:** It sizes the probing work budget and is a reason for building probing at all. It was measured in nonzeros only; what the fixings, implications and cliques are worth to the solver was not.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison when probing exists: a budget at HiGHS's coverage against complete coverage (AS-111).
- **State:** measurement only (one of three sources measured).

### AS-251 Finding: what cuOpt's probing and clique table do today (read from source on 2026-11-26)
- **Assumption:** cuOpt's probing runs one variable per OpenMP task with dense state, its budget allows about 375 variables at 1e7 nonzeros and it does not use cliques; the load-balanced propagation was dropped from the build in September 2025; the clique table is host-only, built inside branch and bound, extends only set-packing rows below 128 entries once each, removes no dominated cliques and is not built in deterministic mode.

### AS-252 Belief: every row-dual bound HiGHS uses is a function of the present state
- **Assumption:** The design of the five rules rests on the reading that HiGHS's original row-dual bounds are only 0 or infinite, so every implied bound those rules read is cost over coefficient of one non-integer column with at most one lock in a direction. That is why no dual state is kept (AS-301). It was read in the HiGHS source by three designers, two critics and the repair agent, not run.
- **Set by:** Design (a belief from reading the HiGHS source, HISTORY Entry 41, lines 2279-2283; lowworth_v2_design.txt:68, :238).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/lowworth_v2_design.txt:68, :238; HISTORY Entry 41.
- **Switch:** no switch in the engine: no dual state is kept (AS-301); on the HiGHS side `implied_row_dual_bounds` in `HIGHS_DIAG_OFF` freezes its bounds.
- **Alternatives:** If HiGHS derives a dual bound in another way somewhere, the lock form would miss reductions that HiGHS makes.
- **May change:** If wrong, the engine would stay behind HiGHS on some instances after the milestone of the dominated column [L5] without the design predicting it.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the per-instance comparison with HiGHS's counters on the 240 and the 40 when the dominated column [L5] is built.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-253 Belief: the exchange rules are valid; evidence is exact tests of tiny models and reading
- **Assumption:** The proofs of the inequality-pivot singleton, dominance, dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias were derived on paper; the review enumerated 90,000 tiny all-integer models in exact arithmetic without a failure. For the five further rules, 105,366 tiny models in rational arithmetic gave no changed outcome in 32,067 applications of the implied equation, 34,110 of the dominated column and 2,335 of the single equation; stuffing was tested exactly on 60,000 random rows; the weak test and the scaling rest on reading. None of these tests has tolerances, crush maps, stored activities or the interplay of the rules, and nothing was run in the engine.
- **Set by:** Design (a belief of the two rule designs and their validity reviews; HISTORY Entry 43).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:263; v2_reviews.txt:9-11; lowworth_v2_design.txt:10, :221; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:9, 50-51; HISTORY Entry 43 (lines 2374-2379).
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: evidence, not a setting; each rule it covers has its switch (`options_t::sub_enabled` for the nine, the designed switches of AS-011 for the five).
- **Alternatives:** None before the code exists; the campaign of each milestone is the first test of the engine's form.
- **May change:** An error in the engine's floating-point form of a rule would show as a cut-off optimum or an infeasible postsolved point.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the campaign of each milestone and the checks on the 240. On the cluster, an arm that ends above the best known objective on an instance the other arm solves is a signal to look at.
- **State:** measurement only.

- **Assumption:** HiGHS credits millions of fixings on neos-4332810-sesia, dlr2 and neos-3402294-bobin to its weakly dominated column test, but all three are reached by the singleton column rules followed by the lock rule. Only a run with a rule switched off decides whether the rule is needed.

### AS-257 Inferences about reach that no engine run has confirmed, and unknowns left open
- **Assumption:** Three chains are inferred from counters, sizes and source reading: supportcase42 is reached by a weak-test conversion, then dual bound strengthening on the converted column, then redundant rows, then the free singleton rule or the lock rule; the general form of the dominated-column rule covers b1c1s1; the engine's own integrality proof avoids the 173 rows that the conversion costs HiGHS on physiciansched3-3 when implied equations are off. Still unknown: the source types of the implied equations on physiciansched3-3 and physiciansched6-2, and what the engine reaches on instances whose reductions depend on the running state (the map10 pair, rocII-5-11, snp-02-004-104, seymour1, neos-5093327-huahum, sp97ar, the last 30 rows of neos-3656078-kumeu). None is credited in a gate before it is measured.

- **Alternatives:** None.
- **May change:** If the supportcase42 chain breaks at a link, the conversion's only clear size instance is lost and the rule is a pure change of the formulation; the source-type unknown decides how much the switch `integer_sources` matters.
