- **State:** measurement only (inference).

### AS-328 Finding: what the singleton-column rules leave on the 240: 375,627 singleton columns, by their first refusal
- **Assumption:** A second presolve of each reduced model, whose first call of the family classifies the singleton columns that are left, finds 375,627 singleton columns in 317,741 rows on 126 of the 240 instances (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac). By the first test that refuses them: 216,683 because the row does not imply the bounds (197,808 an end of the range passes a finite bound, 18,875 the range is infinite; savsched1 61,832); 148,916 integer columns whose row is not integral for them (neos-873061 87,610, thor50dday 53,130); 5,244 by the growth limit; 4,784 with zero cost on an inequality. Of all of them, 66,676 on 35 instances are integer columns with zero cost in rows with two finite sides, which the zero-cost singleton rule would take if they were continuous. The extensions that could reach them are entries of their own: implied bounds read from other rows or through chains (AS-022, AS-023), the growth limit and what its ratio reads (AS-094, AS-318), the integer extension of the zero-cost singleton rule (AS-029), the bound behind the move (AS-317). None is needed for the reference of the milestone: without the growth limit the engine is behind the ladder step `m1_a` on traininstance2 only (AS-233). Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, default mode): the counters of the engine with the tool flag `--left` give the same figures: 375,627 singleton columns in 317,741 rows on 126 instances; 216,683 for the implied-bound test (197,808 and 18,875) on 108 instances, 148,916 not integral on 16, 5,244 by the growth limit, 4,784 with zero cost on an inequality; 782 that fail only the bound behind the move against 62,594 that fail the bound ahead; 66,676 zero-cost integer columns in rows with two finite sides on 35 instances, of which 50,771 on 23 instances stand in a row that is integral for them (new; AS-352). The second presolve is unchanged on 239 instances and reduces neos-1122047, which has no fixed-point flag.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding of the worth reviewer of the milestone of the singleton-column rules [M1], made with an instrumented scratch build that is not in the repository; the entries named rely on it). HISTORY Entry 48 gives the owner the growth limit and its cost, not these counts.
- **Where:** stage1c_reports/m1/attack_worth.json (field "reductions", "What is left"; fields "assumptions" and "open"); HISTORY Entry 48. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:65-163 (the counters); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_presolve.cpp:23-28 and 272-278 (`--left`); the stored run gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_switches/left/summary.json; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (field "counters").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: since commit `f05d48e31` the counts are counters of the engine (`column_stats_t::counts`, which gprs_presolve prints as "column_counts"), and the columns that are left are counted with the tool flag `--left` of gprs_presolve (one more presolve of the reduced model with one round; "left" and "second" in the report; AS-347). In the engine two of the classes are behind `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` and the reductions of `options_t::sub_enabled`; of the others, the growth ratio has an option (AS-318) and the rest have no switch (AS-022, AS-029, AS-317).
- **Alternatives:** None; a measurement.
- **May change:** Which extension of the singleton-column rules is worth building next, by count; a count of columns is not a size gain, because only a free singleton column takes its row with it.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the counters as statistics of the engine, then a size run for each extension that is built.
- **State:** measurement only (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; reproduced from the engine's counters at commit `f05d48e31`; not repeated on the node).

### AS-352 Finding: 50,771 zero-cost integer singleton columns on 23 of the 240 stand in rows that are integral for them
- **Assumption:** The count that AS-029 asks for, before the integer extension of the zero-cost singleton rule [R3] is decided, is made, and it is not zero. Of the 66,676 zero-cost integer singleton columns that the engine leaves in rows with two finite sides on 35 of the 240 instances, 50,771 on 23 instances stand in a row that is integral for them by its ratios: every other free column of the row is integer and has a coefficient that is an exact multiple of theirs (the side is not asked, and the test has no tolerance). The largest: rail02 9,292, neos-3004026-krka 8,580, rail01 8,272, bab6 8,268, highschool1-aigio 7,529. Over the presolve the rule passes over integer columns in 82,868 visits on 37 instances, 50,829 of them on 24 instances in such a row. What taking them would gain is not measured: a column would leave with its one nonzero, and its row would stay with sides rounded onto the grid, so the count is of columns and not of rows. The rule that would take them is a new mechanism (AS-029) and is not built. Instance set: the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, default mode, counters of the engine at commit `f05d48e31`; not repeated on the node.
- **Set by:** Design (a measured finding of the implementer of the switches, recounted by the reviewer; AS-029 relies on it). HISTORY Entry 54 names the count next to the choices that are not built; that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has not decided whether the extension is built.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:408-428 (`integral_singletons`) and 480-483; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:131-138 (the two counters); the stored run gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_switches/left/summary.json; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "counters" and "switches") and attack_switches.json (fields "evidence", "switches", row 5, and "open").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the counters `column_count::zero_cost_integer` and `zero_cost_integer_integral` of the engine, with `--left` of gprs_presolve for the columns that are left; the extension itself has no switch (AS-029).
- **Alternatives:** None; a measurement. A count with the tolerances of `integral_for` and with the side tested would be the count of what the extension as designed could take.
- **May change:** Whether the integer extension of the zero-cost singleton rule is built: up to 50,771 integer slack columns on 23 instances would leave, each with one nonzero, and their rows would stay.
- **Test at the end:** As AS-029: build the extension behind a switch, then a size run on the 240 with the hashes and the standard cluster comparison, read on the 23 instances.
- **State:** measurement only (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; not repeated on the node).

## 8. Known defects and blind spots accepted for now

### AS-258 Three numerical defects of the base engine on models with extreme constants are open
- **Assumption:** Reviews with planted models found that the base engine gives wrong results on models with coefficient ratios of 1e9 inside a row or values of 1e15 and more (24 and 101 of 300,000 planted models). Three defects were named and not repaired: a merge of parallel rows of different scale next to a fixed term of 1e7 and more returns "infeasible" for a feasible model; integer row tightening rounds a side of 1.9e9 times a scale of 125 the wrong way; a doubleton substitution leaves a coefficient of about 1e-9 that the row rules take literally. The current sources hold guards that look aimed at the first two; that all three are repaired could not be confirmed, and no guard for the third was found. None was seen on the 240 or the 44 testbed and status instances.
- **Set by:** Code (found by reviewers, HISTORY Entries 22 and 23; listed as open in Entry 39).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 22 (lines 1342-1347), Entry 23 (lines 1422-1426), Entry 39 (lines 2189-2192); gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:423-446; gpu_presolve/src/rules_tightening.cpp:36-38.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if a size guard is built.
- **Alternatives:** Repair before any cluster run; one size rule in the state that leaves such rows alone.
- **May change:** A wrong status or a cut-off optimum on badly scaled models (constants of 1e9 and more).
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: a campaign of planted models with extreme constants after the repair; a list of the 240 instances with such constants checked by hand; in every cluster arm, wrong statuses and infeasible postsolved solutions.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-259 Two oracle failures of 1.1e-9 are untriaged: dual fixing at an outward-moved bound
- **Assumption:** At the base commit the campaign with added singleton columns fails on two models (seed 32077278, profile huge_parallel; seed 5302642968, profile open_ends; a rate of about 1 in 67 million): the reduced optimum is 1.1e-9 below the true one and the oracle allows 1e-9. Dual fixing fixes a column at an explicit bound that bound propagation had moved outward by the error bound of an activity with a huge term (in the second model a continuous column with a lower bound of -5e21 and a term just under 1e20). Whether the oracle's tolerance or the outward rounding is at fault is not decided. The oracle also compares objectives relative to the value, so costs scaled by 2^30 give false alarms. Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns on the Mac): a third model of the class exists (profile huge_parallel, seed 10100538218, option variant 8, no profile option, assertion campaign: reduced optimum 0.99999999889066382 against 1); the base engine fails it identically in both builds, also with the singleton family off, and the family has no finding on it. Of the two known seeds, 32077278 no longer fails with the family on and 5302642968 fails as before. With costs scaled by 2^30 or 2^50 the oracle gives 655 alarms on 1,200,000 models of the option `--columns`, 452 with the base engine on the same models, all of the kind "relative to the value" or outside the oracle's class; the cost that the free singleton column [R2, R5] moves adds rounding of the cost terms, and the structure check fails on none of the 645,070 results there. A fourth failing model of the base engine is of another class (AS-333). A triage of the seeds was started beside the next milestone (HISTORY Entry 48). Measured with the node campaign of 5.4 billion models (engine commit `daa4f5d68`, 5,399,999,136 random models in six runs; HISTORY Entry 52): 6 of the 18 oracle failures are of this class, objective differences of 1.1e-9 to 2.2e-9 against the oracle's relative threshold of 1e-9. Release build, no profile option: profile dense, seeds 1000003670828 (0.33333333210073723 against 0.33333333333333348) and 1000070054017 (-1.85e-9 against 0), and seed 1000052005627 in the two profiles huge_parallel and huge_equations (-1.0000000011093362 against -1). Release build, `--columns`: profile open_ends, seed 1000294564032 (-1.48e-9 against 0). Assertion build, `--singletons`: profile bounded_continuous, seed 1000427998608 (1.999999997781327 against 2). That is about one in 900 million models. The structure check failed on none of the six and missed all of them: it compares no optimal values, and by the campaign's own count none of the 18 results that it missed is a relaxation (the line "structure check" of the six runs: missed 18 in the sum, of them relaxed 0), which leaves a restriction or an error of the objective, and those it cannot see (AS-277). No assertion fired. The reading of the failure lines is the assistant's, not yet the verdict of the triage. With the engine of the switches of milestone M1 at the defaults the known seeds of the Mac behave as before: 5302642968 fails with -1.109e-09 against 0, 10100538218 with 0.99999999889 against 1, and 32077278 fails with neither binary.
- **Set by:** Code (found by the groundwork implementer and its reviewer; HISTORY Entries 44 and 45: "an entry for the register"). HISTORY Entry 52 records the counts of the node campaign by class as the assistant's reading; it has no message of the owner.
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 44 (lines 2419-2423) and Entry 45 ("Also in this interaction"); gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:59; gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:23-30, 163-181. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.cpp:936 (objectives are compared relative to the value); HISTORY Entry 48. gpu_presolve/results/stage1c/campaign/s1c_m1_big1 (log.txt, the totals `<run>.txt`, the lines `<run>.failures.txt` and the part files with the printed models); HISTORY Entry 52.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one if the outward move is changed (AS-078); the campaign flags are `--singletons` and `--columns`.
- **Alternatives:** Widen the oracle's tolerance; make dual fixing fix at the bound before it was moved outward.
- **May change:** An objective value off by about 1e-9 relative on models with huge terms; far below cuOpt's tolerances.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: triage of the two seeds, then the campaign.
- **State:** in the engine (base commit, commit `fa8430e24` and commit `f05d48e31`); three seeds of the Mac's campaigns and six failures (five seeds) of the node campaign are known and are with the triage (HISTORY Entry 52); no verdict yet.

### AS-260 A column can be fixed at any finite bound, however large
- **Assumption:** Dual fixing fixes a column at its target bound whenever that bound is not an IEEE infinity; there is no limit on its size. The constant term that a column fixed at a huge value leaves in its rows swamps the sides, and each family guards itself against that separately (AS-076).
- **Set by:** Code (noted as a lesson in HISTORY Entry 18: "The engine allows fixing at bounds up to 1e20; that deserves one rule in the state rather than a guard in each family").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_dual_fixing.cpp:42-58; the guards are in rules_parallel_rows.cpp:317-320, rules_tightening.cpp:184-189, state_storage.cpp:150-198.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Refuse to fix at a bound above a limit (for example 1e8); treat such a bound as infinite.
- **May change:** Sides that lose precision next to a huge constant; a presolve that cannot move the fixed terms into the sides and so ends without a fixed point; the status "unbounded or infeasible" on a model with a huge finite bound.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair first: the campaign with huge bounds and a structure check on the instances of the 240 with bounds above 1e8. A cluster pair only after a limit exists as a switch.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-261 The error bound of an activity is a first-order estimate, not a proven bound
- **Assumption:** Each stored row activity carries the error bound updates * 2^-106 * (largest term the sum ever held), and every guard compares against it. The review measured errors up to 38 times that formula on rows of same-sign terms, and the engine's own consistency check uses 9 * (n + 1)^3 times that amount because valid states exceed the estimate. The rule families sum a row from scratch before a test that the error could decide.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md section 2.4); left open by the state review (HISTORY Entries 14 and 15: "Open, it sits in the plan").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/numerics.hpp:121-126; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/state.hpp:333-340; gpu_presolve/src/state_storage.cpp:682-696; HISTORY Entry 15 (lines 943-946).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one; `options_t::tol.activity_error_share` sets when a row is summed again.
- **Alternatives:** The proven bound in the guards (sum of absolute terms); a fresh sum before every test; plain double sums with a relative tolerance.
- **May change:** Only models with huge terms or long rows of same-sign terms with many updates: a test can be decided by an activity whose true error is above the estimate.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: a campaign with long rows of same-sign terms, many updates and huge terms.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-262 Doubleton equations are substituted without a numerical acceptance test or a fill limit
- **Assumption:** Every equation with two free columns is used to replace one column by the other when the scale is finite and not zero and the offset is finite. There is no limit on the size of the scale, on the number of entries of the substituted column, or on the growth of coefficients in the rows that receive the kept column.
- **Set by:** Code (left open by the review, HISTORY Entry 14: "aliases have no numeric acceptance test").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:27-30, 39-64; gpu_presolve/src/rules_doubleton.cpp:110-156.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for a limit; `options_t::enabled[rule::doubleton]` turns the family.
- **Alternatives:** A limit on the absolute scale or on the coefficient ratio of the changed rows (the subtraction of parallel rows vetoes above 1e6); a limit on the entries of the substituted column.
- **May change:** Rows with coefficients of very different size; the postsolve multiplies a deviation of the kept column by the scale, so a postsolved point can violate bounds or rows of the original model.
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison: doubleton equations on against off; with a limit, a pair with a scale limit; the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check in each arm.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-263 The alias forest accepts an integer map without a drift guard
- **Assumption:** An integer column is replaced only by an integer column, when scale and offset are within 1e-9 of integers and the rounded map satisfies the equation in its own units; what the rounding does over the range of the kept column is not bounded. With a column of a huge range the removed equation can be violated by more than the tolerance after postsolve (the design's model: x - 1.0000000005 y = 0 with y integer up to 2,000,000). The repair, the shared integrality test (AS-093), is scheduled with the milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1], not before. Measured with milestone M1 (on the Mac): the repair is built: the edge test of the alias forest between two integer columns reads the shared integrality test with the drift guard (AS-093, AS-322), and the design's model x - 1.0000000005 y = 0 is a test. The doubleton equations use the forest, so the repair reaches a built rule; no result changes on the 17 local dumps, on the 240 as local dumps or on 4,320,000 random models per build. The wrong engine without the guard (r_alias_drift) fails unit tests only (AS-330).
- **Set by:** Code (found by the design round of the five rules, HISTORY Entry 43; v2_amendments.txt:92-121).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:38-64; stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:97-102, 120-121; lowworth_v2_design.txt:15, :224. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/alias_forest.cpp:50-63; gpu_presolve/src/integral.hpp:74-88.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the guard reads `options_t::tol.integral_drift_share` (default 0.1; tool `--integral-drift-share <x>`) and `options_t::tol.feasibility` (default 1e-6).
- **Alternatives:** Repair it now in the Stage 1 code.
- **May change:** A row violated by more than the tolerance at some reduced points on models with a coefficient that is integral only to 1e-9 and a large column range.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: a fixed oracle model and a wrong engine; on the cluster it would show only as an infeasible postsolved solution.
- **State:** repaired in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-264 Parallel rows are found through a hash on 20 mantissa bits, blind at rounding boundaries
- **Assumption:** Rows are grouped by a hash of their coefficients, normalised and rounded to 20 mantissa bits. Two rows that are parallel within 1e-9 but have a coefficient on a rounding boundary of the hash fall into different groups and are not compared. Of two parallel rows the one with the smaller index stays and takes the intersection of the sides. Measured with milestone M1 (two models of the validity reviewer): that the row with the smaller index stays, whatever the scales of the two rows, is the cause of a wrong result of the base engine (AS-329).
- **Set by:** Design (the blind spot is stated in the rule's contract).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:29-32, 42-71, 793-806; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:267-269, 285-289. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:795-797; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:299-303.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** A second hash with shifted rounding; an exact canonical form.
- **May change:** A few parallel pairs stay in the model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: count the missed pairs on the 240 against an exhaustive search.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-265 A free singleton chain's growth factor does not follow a column through an alias
- **Assumption:** When a saved column of a free singleton record is later replaced by an alias, the growth factor is not carried on, so the bound of 1000 (AS-094) can be exceeded through that alias. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): built as designed: the family keeps the factors by column index, and nothing carries a factor to the representative when a saved column is later replaced by an alias. The factor of earlier substitutions refuses 0 columns at any limit tried, and no count of the case itself was made.
- **Set by:** Design (stated as a risk and confirmed by the validity review).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:269; v2_answers.txt:26; v2_reviews.txt:64. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:36-38 and 235-241.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one.
- **Alternatives:** Carry the factor through aliases.
- **May change:** Rare larger bound violations in postsolved solutions.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the count of postsolved points outside the tolerance on the 240.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-266 The zero-cost singleton rule turns equations into ranged rows
- **Assumption:** After the zero-cost singleton rule an equation with a slack-like column is a ranged row, which the doubleton rule and the later aggregator cannot use as a pivot row. It is judged by the cheap-rules gate only, and is to be looked at again when sparsify and the aggregator exist. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, the reduced models of the engine before and with the family; a scratch tool of the worth reviewer): equations go from 1,107,275 to 1,076,219 and ranged rows from 36,599 to 53,431. With the whole family the largest finite side of the reduced model is larger on 11 instances (germanrr 1 to 171, snp-02-004-104 8,000 to 640,000, blp-ic98 1 to 32, physiciansched3-3 4,900 to 56,570, and dws008-01, csched008, blp-ar98, timtab1, neos-2746589-doon, icir97_tension and supportcase12). Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the tool is now in the repository (gprs_quality with gpu_presolve/analysis/quality_compare.py) and gives the same with the family off and on: equations 1,107,275 to 1,076,219, ranged rows 36,599 to 53,431.
- **Set by:** Design (stated as a risk, v2_design.txt:271).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:271. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:343-344 and 379-388.
- **Switch:** `options_t::sub_enabled[sub_rule::zero_cost_singleton]` (default on; tool `--off zero_cost_singleton`).
- **Alternatives:** Apply the rule after the aggregator; keep equations.
- **May change:** Fewer substitutions in later stages; a different LP form (ranged rows instead of equations with slacks).
- **Test at the end:** Standard cluster comparison: `zero_cost_singleton` on against off, repeated when the aggregator stage exists.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-267 No reduction looks at what it does to the integrality of the objective
- **Assumption:** cuOpt takes the objective as integral when every nonzero cost is an integer within 1e-9 and sits on an integer or implied-integer column, and it computes an objective step from the costs and the rows; branch and bound uses both to round its bound and to set its cutoff. Engine reductions move cost: a doubleton substitution moves it to the kept column times a ratio, a free singleton substitution spreads it over up to row length minus 1 columns, a merge of a mixed pair keeps the continuous column, the column scaling multiplies it. No design checks whether the objective of the reduced model is still integral. Measured with milestone M1: nothing on the integrality of the objective. Since this milestone the free singleton column [R2, R5] spreads cost in the engine: 163,725 columns get a cost on 43 of the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, each new cost being the cost of the removed column over its coefficient times the coefficient of the other column (AS-095, AS-324); no test asks whether the new costs are integers on integer columns.
- **Set by:** Design (an omission; not noted in DESIGN.md, HISTORY or the two rule designs).
- **Where:** cpp/src/mip_heuristics/problem/problem.cu:1282-1296 and :1335-1410; cpp/src/mip_heuristics/solver.cu:340-360; cpp/src/branch_and_bound/branch_and_bound.cpp:500-541; gpu_presolve/src/rules_doubleton.cpp:144-152; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:37 and :83; lowworth_v2_design.txt:37-42. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:201 and 219-232.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (a guard per rule, or one option that keeps an integral objective). On cuOpt's side `mip_objective_step` (default 1) switches the use of the step.
- **Alternatives:** Refuse a substitution, merge or scaling that makes an integral objective non-integral; accept the loss; export what the engine knows about the step.
- **May change:** Whether cuOpt rounds node bounds and sets its cutoff a step below the incumbent: optimal count and final gap on models with an integral objective, with no trace in any size. It adds to AS-164, which is the same effect through the missing implied-integer flags.
- **Test at the end:** In every pair count per arm the instances with the log lines "Objective function is integral" and "Objective step size" (cpp/src/mip_heuristics/solver.cu:347 and :353); where the counts differ, read optimal count and final bound on those instances apart. With a guard built: standard cluster comparison, guard on against off.
- **State:** in the engine (doubleton substitution, and the free singleton rules since commit `fa8430e24`); designed for the merge of parallel columns [M5] and the column scaling [L2].

### AS-268 After the singleton rules the parallel rows hash many rows again; that cost is unmeasured
- **Assumption:** Each round expands listed rows to their columns for the dual family, and after the singleton rules many rows change and are hashed again by the parallel rows. In HiGHS that stage takes 80 s on rd-rplusc-21; the engine was not measured on such states. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, timing run of the worth reviewer): with the singleton-column rules the parallel rows take 0.021 s more of 7.6 s in the sum, the row rules 0.052 s more (+9.0%) and dual fixing 0.015 s more (+34%); the largest single change is neos-3402294-bobin (+0.071 s, in the row rules). rd-rplusc-21 needs one more round only with the family placed right after the cascade (AS-114). Not measured on the node or on the 40; the dual family is not built.
- **Set by:** Design (stated as a risk, v2_design.txt:268).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:268.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if the cost proves high (a limit on hashing rows again); `options_t::budget[rule::parallel_rows]` bounds the work of a call.
- **Alternatives:** None stated.
- **May change:** Presolve time on a few instances.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: timing and work per family at the milestones of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] and of dual bound strengthening and the single-lock alias [M4].
- **State:** in the engine for the singleton-column rules (commit `fa8430e24`), measured on the 240 as local dumps; designed for the dual family.

### AS-269 Each column family keeps its own row list; a switched-off family still pays for it
- **Assumption:** The state pushes every row and column event onto the lists of the four new families whether or not a family is switched on. The design's rule was: engine time on the 240 within 3% of the time before, or the three column families share one row list. Measured on the node for the groundwork (minimum of three alternating passes, one process at a time, node load 12 to 40): +1.07% on the 240 (16.50 s to 16.68 s), +2.27% on the 31 and +1.91% on the 9 (dlr2 +5.1%, neos-4332810-sesia +3.2%); with the four families off +0.50%, +2.46% and +1.68%. By the criterion on the 240 each family keeps its own list, although the 31 and single large instances pay more. Two wrong engines (an event that does not feed the new lists) are caught by unit tests only until a rule reads the lists. The first rule milestone was started before the node had confirmed the groundwork. Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns of 1,440,000 models and the timing run on the Mac): now that the singleton family reads its lists, the wrong engine ev_unread is caught by the second presolve of the campaign on 2 models with the final sources and fails 9 unit tests; ev_scale is still caught by no campaign check and fails 5 unit tests, because the singleton family reads nothing that only a column event of a row scaling would bring. With the family off the engine takes 7.421 s against 7.432 s for the base on the 240 as local dumps (-0.1%, inside the noise of 0.14%).
- **Set by:** Taken as recommended (decided by the assistant "by the design's own criterion", HISTORY Entry 46; the criterion names the 240 only).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 46 (lines 2537-2543), Entry 44 (lines 2413-2417) and Entry 45; stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:107, :183, :233; v2_amendments.txt:78-79; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/state.hpp:159-243; gpu_presolve/analysis/stage1c_time.py. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:473-490 (`ev_unread`, `ev_scale`).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (events masked by the enabled families, or the shared row list); `options_t::enabled` switches the four families but not their lists.
- **Alternatives:** One row list shared by the three column families (the design's fallback); events masked by the enabled families.
- **May change:** Presolve time by 1 to 2% in the sum and up to 5% on single large instances. The results are identical: status, sizes, hash, fixed-point flag, rounds and counts equal the engine before on the 240, the 31 and the 9. A missing event stays unseen until a rule reads the lists.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the same three alternating passes when the first rules read the lists, on the 240 and on the 9.
- **State:** in the engine (merged into feat/gpu-presolve as `6eed652cf` and `74350acb3`); the singleton family reads its lists since commit `fa8430e24`.

### AS-270 Implementation starts from designs with known open points
- **Assumption:** The nine-rule design (version 2) has sixteen open points, called minor, and the design of the five further rules (version 2) has fourteen, five of them serious (six from the validity review, eight from the worth, cost and state review). Each has a model and a fix and is binding for the milestone it belongs to; none is repaired in the design text itself. The verifiers did not read everything: the validity verifier of the five rules did not read the structure check, the oracle source, the plan or the result files, and the worth verifier's work counts are static counts on input models. Measured with milestone M1: the amendments that are binding for the milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] (D.1, D.4, D.9, F.2, F.3, F.4 and F.7 of v2_amendments.txt) are built; the validity reviewer confirmed F.3 and F.4 by reading and F.7 also for its own addition. One amendment was itself defective: by the text of F.2 the integral side and the drift guard each use the tolerance on their own, which left a row off by 1.08e-6 at an exactly feasible reduced point; the reviewer repaired it in the code (AS-322), and the design text is unchanged.
- **Set by:** Taken as recommended (accepted by the assistant on the word of two verifiers per design, HISTORY Entries 40 and 43).
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 40 (lines 2200-2204) and Entry 43 (lines 2358-2361); stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:46-82 and 92-121; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:10-23, 25-44; lowworth_v2_reviews.txt:7-40, 59-112.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: each open point is binding for its milestone and has a fixed test or a wrong engine named with it (stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt, lowworth_v2_amendments.txt).
- **Alternatives:** A third design version before any code.
- **May change:** If an amendment is missed in a milestone, the rule is built with the hole the review found: a conversion that cuts feasible points for coefficients below 0.1, an implied equation with a term above about 1e9 that loses an optimum (3332.33 becomes 4332.33 in the verifier's model), a wrong fixed-point flag for the weak test, a stalled dual column family on supportcase40.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: each amendment has a fixed test or a wrong engine named with it, checked by the milestone's review.
- **State:** designed (binding per milestone); built and reviewed for the milestone of the singleton-column rules [M1] (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-271 The presolve can end without a fixed point, and the reason is not reported
- **Assumption:** The engine sets a flag when a second presolve of its result would change nothing; the reduced model is returned in either case. The flag is missing on 6 of the 240 (icir97_tension, comp21-2idx, neos-1122047, co-100, neos-5052403-cygnet, neos-2075418-temuka) and on 3 of the 40 (neos-5052403-cygnet, a2864-99blp, dlr2), the result does not say which limit caused it, and no gate asks for it. Even with the flag set, a second presolve can decide a test otherwise where the last bits of a stored activity decide it. For the column rules the flag is argued from the event sites by reading; four limits clear it on purpose (bucket limit, absorber limit, saved-row limit, growth refusal). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; recounted from gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac): with the singleton-column rules the flag is set on 234 of the 240 as before, the same six are without it, and in primal-only mode it is set on 233 (lectsched-5-obj is the seventh without it; that none is lost against the engine before in that mode is the count of the worth reviewer, and no run of the engine before in that mode is stored). The second presolve of the reduced model returns unchanged on every instance with the flag, in both modes; neos-1122047, without the flag, loses 4 more nonzeros in default mode. The two limits of the family that clear the flag on purpose (the saved-row limit, and a refusal by the growth factor of earlier substitutions alone) clear it on no instance; a refusal by the ratio of the pivot row is a function of the state and does not clear it. Two defects of the first version of the milestone concerned the flag and were repaired by the validity reviewer (AS-325, AS-326).
- **Set by:** Design (stated in the contract of `presolve()`); listed as open in HISTORY Entry 39 ("the reason of a missing fixed-point flag is not reported (nine instances)").
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:227-235, 305-325; gpu_presolve/src/presolve.cpp:437-438; gpu_presolve/results/bench240/v4pre/release_all/summary.json and gpu_presolve/results/stage1/host/v4 (field `fixed_point`); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:127, :266; lowworth_v2_design.txt:93-99; lowworth_v2_amendments.txt:26-28. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:232-242; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:356-360 and 422-426; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:112-120.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for a report of the cause; the limits behind it are `options_t::max_rounds`, `max_cascade_passes`, the budgets and `parallel_bucket_limit`.
- **Alternatives:** Report the cause per instance; require the flag in a gate; run the presolve on its own result until it returns unchanged.
- **May change:** On these instances reductions are left that a second presolve would find; a missed event site loses reductions and makes the flag wrong.
- **Test at the end:** First a size run: presolve the reduced model again on the 240 and the 40 and compare the hashes. If every second presolve returns its input unchanged, the entry is closed. Any difference sends it to the standard cluster comparison, read on the instances that differ: one presolve against the presolve applied twice, or larger limits against the defaults.
- **State:** in the engine.

### AS-272 The limit on row dynamism is stated but not measured for the engine
- **Assumption:** The plan says the 99th percentile of the ratio of largest to smallest coefficient per row of our reduced model may be at most 10 times that of HiGHS's reduced model. The comparison script writes the reference's dynamism to its table, but nothing computes the engine's, so the limit is not checked. On rd-rplusc-21 the first engine had 35 times HiGHS's row dynamism.
- **Set by:** Design (DESIGN.md section 12: "Row dynamism is a constraint, not a goal"); the missing measurement is code.
- **Where:** DESIGN.md section 12 (line 766); gpu_presolve/analysis/stage1_compare.py:65-66, 119-135, 271-272; HISTORY Entry 17 (item 4).
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (single rules have their own limits, for example `max_dynamism` = 1e6 in gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:375).
- **Alternatives:** Write the row dynamism of the reduced model in the engine tool's report and gate on it; a tighter factor; an absolute limit.
- **May change:** The numerical quality of the model handed to cuOpt's LP and heuristics, more so with scaling off.
- **Test at the end:** Report the 99th percentile per instance for both sides on the 240 with the final engine, and the dynamism of both arms next to the quality figures.
- **State:** planned (not enforced in the scripts).

- **Assumption:** The plan keeps 36 held-out instances, drawn by the same rule with the main set's families excluded, that are not to be looked at before the completion stage. 23 of them are among the 240 that every decision now uses (for example nw04, rail02, neos-3656078-kumeu, supportcase10), so only 13 are still unseen. Nobody decided what replaces it.

### AS-275 The checks cannot see a restriction that cuts no tested point
- **Assumption:** A reduction that wrongly cuts off feasible points is seen only if it cuts a point that is tested or makes HiGHS prove a worse optimum. Known wrong engines that passed every check: an implied bound made 1e-4 too tight (uccase9, triptim1, kenana), propagation over a row with an infinite term (uccase9), and any restriction on the infeasible instances. A support test for each tightened bound, named as the remedy, is not built; pushed points are a partial remedy.
- **Set by:** Design (found by the attack with wrong engines, HISTORY Entry 20, "Still invisible"; accepted for now).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:51-60; gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:26-33; HISTORY Entry 20 (lines 1212-1216).
- **Switch:** `--push-points` (at most 4 per point) and `--cost-variants` of gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py widen what is tested; no switch closes the gap: needs a support test per tightened bound.
- **Alternatives:** A support test per tightened bound; many more points per instance; exact solves.
- **May change:** A slightly too tight bound can cut the optimum on a real instance while every check passes.
- **Test at the end:** The end test is the strongest detector available: an arm that loses optimal solutions or ends with a worse primal gap on instances the other arm solves points to a restriction; look at instances that close the gap at an objective worse than the best known value.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-276 Instances without a usable point get no crush check
- **Assumption:** The crush check needs a feasible point that meets the original model within 1e-7. On the 240, 56 instances have no usable point in the newest check (the groundwork engine, tag s1c_m0: 184 instances with 2,593 points, recounted from the stored summary; with HiGHS points of 60 s alone it was 173 instances with 2,153 points, HISTORY Entry 27); on those the evidence is the presolve status and the structure check alone. On the 40, all 31 below 1e7 nonzeros have points since the published MIPLIB points were converted; of the nine large instances hawaiiv10-130 has none (its two published solutions violate rows by up to 5.3e-5 on the model HiGHS reads), so the check of the large set fails on that one item in every node chain.
- **Set by:** Design (accepted for now, HISTORY Entries 30 and 39, listed as open; `--require-points` makes the missing check a visible failure).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py:26-28, :67, 354-356; HISTORY Entry 27 (lines 1653-1655), Entry 30, Entry 39 (lines 2168-2179), Entry 46 (lines 2532-2536); gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m0/check_all/summary.json (field `crush.points_used`).
- **Switch:** `--require-points` of run_check.py; solution files in every `--solutions-dir`.
- **Alternatives:** Accept published points at MIPLIB's 1e-6 and judge the crushed and postsolved point against the violation it came with; polish the points; longer HiGHS runs; cuOpt's own incumbents as points.
- **May change:** Nothing in the engine; restrictions on about a quarter of the 240 are tested by no point.
- **Test at the end:** The end test covers these instances through cuOpt's own solutions, which can be fed back into the crush check; before it, points from earlier cuOpt runs could be converted.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-277 The structure check cannot prove the dual restrictions
- **Assumption:** The structure check verifies the stack, the types and the column map, but cannot prove that a dominance fixing had its absorber bound implied, that a dual bound saw every locking row, that a lock alias had its row released, that an implied equation had its lock single, or that a weak conversion or a fixing by stuffing, single equation or dominated column keeps an optimal point. Only the crush check on feasible points and the oracle test these. Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns of 1,440,000 models per wrong engine, on the Mac): the first dual reduction of the column rules is built, the free singleton column on an inequality [R5], and the structure check has its part for the record of the rule. It fails on no result of two wrong engines of that rule: the one that takes the other side of the row when the side that the cost pushes the column against is infinite (fs_any_side; the oracle fails on 1,305 results) and the one that records the reduction as a primal one (fs_not_dual; the oracle fails on 1,212 results, by its crush test). The validity reviewer states it as a rule: where the structure check shows no failure of a wrong engine, the engine restricts, which the check cannot see by design.
- **Set by:** Design (v2_design.txt:153; lowworth_v2_design.txt:110-115).
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:153; lowworth_v2_design.txt:110-115. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:775-798 (`fs_not_dual` and `fs_any_side`, each with the check that sees it); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (field "evidence").
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if a dual certificate per record is built; the structure check has no option for it.
- **Alternatives:** A dual certificate per record.
- **May change:** A wrong dual reduction on a real instance is seen only if a known point is cut off or the reduced optimum is proved.
- **Test at the end:** Not testable by solution quality as a rule: the crush check on the 184 benchmark instances with points and the campaign. On the cluster a lost optimum shows as a worse best objective on an instance where the other arm finds the known optimum; compare per instance, not only means.
- **State:** in the check code for the free singleton column on an inequality [R5] (commit `fa8430e24`); designed for the other dual reductions.

### AS-278 The exact oracle decides only a small class of models and sees no tolerance
- **Assumption:** The oracle solves tiny random models by enumeration: at most 5 rows, 5 enumerated integer columns of width up to 3 and 2 other columns, integer coefficients up to 3 and powers of two, at most one non-enumerated column per row. It cannot generate rows with several continuous columns or sides above 1e10 (363 models of a 204-million campaign ended outside its class), cannot show a wrong tolerance (tolerance cases rest on a handful of fixed models), is exact for parallel columns only when both are continuous, and tests the singleton rules with a second continuous column in the pivot row, the dominated column with a compensator that has further rows and the single equation with absorbers that have further rows by sampling only. The 31 have free columns on 6 instances only and no finite bound of 1e8 or more, so the oracle is the only test for both. Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns on the Mac): of 20,160,000 release models per option, 77 reduced models with `--singletons` and 86 with `--columns` end outside the oracle's class. For the four rules of the singleton family the validity reviewer added what the oracle cannot give, with a tool in exact rational arithmetic that is not in the repository: 60,000 tiny models with the design's conditions applied by hand (0 failures, 3,168 not decidable), 100,000 through the engine in seven option sets (0 failures; the structure check silent on 123,004 results with several continuous columns per row, which lie outside the oracle's class), and 40,000 with bounds and sides moved by up to 3e-7 and ratios by 5e-10 (postsolved points within 2e-6).
- **Set by:** Design (the oracle's author; the limits were named by reviewers, HISTORY Entry 22, and by both rule designs). The oracle was built during the six hours of independent work, without a check-in.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:10-47, :59, 74-81, 241-244; gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:459-467; DESIGN.md section 13 (lines 846-859); stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:160-161, :272; lowworth_v2_design.txt:119-132, :230.
- **Switch:** fields of `generator_options_t` (gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:76-130), set per profile in gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:62-166, for example `p_singleton_col` (0 by default; the campaign flag `--singletons` raises it) and `p_parallel_row` (0.15). A knob `p_parallel_col` for parallel columns is designed only (v2_design.txt:159, :244) and is not in code.
- **Alternatives:** Planted models with a known solution for larger structures; a generator with non-integer data and an exact reference solver; a second exact solver for integer twins; a larger oracle class.
- **May change:** A wrong reduction that needs a structure outside the class, a tolerance error outside the fixed models, or a wrong merge of integer parallel columns (the case of AS-001) would pass the campaign and first show on real instances.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test by design; the end test covers real structures, which is why per-instance results of each arm should be looked at, not only means.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-279 The checks' proofs cover the rules that exist; a new rule must bring its part
- **Assumption:** The crush check's proof is an induction over the reductions with one sentence per dual rule: a dual rule that is added must bring its sentence and, where it moves a column that stays in the model, a record that the crush replays; the proof has to be reread for a rule that keeps no forward map (a bound on a row dual, a fixing at infinity). The structure check's proof is written for the rules that exist. Until its extension exists, a new rule is unchecked by these two checks; this happened with the row addition on brazil3 and piperout-d27. Measured with milestone M1: the singleton-column rules brought their part: the record `col_from_rows` with its three kinds is read by the structure check (the composition of the records, the replay and the postsolve part), by clause 5 of the crush check and by the hash of the postsolve stack. A new record kind has to be added in these five places, or a check fails or goes blind.
- **Set by:** Design (stated in the check headers; the lesson of HISTORY Entry 25).
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:21-49; gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:93-119. At commit `fa8430e24`: gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:400 (`compose_records`), :1736 (`replay`) and :1831 (`postsolve_at`); gpu_presolve/check/crush_check.hpp:421-446; gpu_presolve/src/hash.cpp:218-219.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: each new rule brings its check extension; which checks run is set by the flags of gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py.
- **Alternatives:** None; each new rule brings its check extension and wrong engines.
- **May change:** A wrong reduction of a rule whose extension is missing passes the checks.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: wrong engines for each new rule that the extended checks must catch.
- **State:** designed (per milestone); built for the singleton-column rules (commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-282 The adapter's conversions cost several times the engine; tracked, not optimised
- **Assumption:** Inside cuOpt the presolve call took 94 s on the 240 (128 s in an earlier run on a busier node) for work the standalone engine does in 18 s (the engine before the groundwork, one run); the adapter's conversions are a median of 650 ns per nonzero, and bohle, square47 and kasavu carry the most. - **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_design.txt:8, :35, :63, :273, :277; v2_reviews.txt:11, :79; lowworth_v2_design.txt:8, :235; lowworth_v2_answers.txt:74.
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: a gap of reading; the PaPILO methods cuOpt runs are listed in cpp/src/presolve/third_party_presolve.cpp:725-759 (AS-138).
- **Alternatives:** Read PaPILO's forms of the same rules (it is the presolve to be replaced in cuOpt).
- **May change:** Comparisons with PaPILO inside cuOpt may rest on a wrong picture of what PaPILO does; the designs may miss a form that PaPILO has and HiGHS has not.
- **Test at the end:** The main comparison (mode 0 against mode 3) would show a PaPILO-only form as a difference; it does not isolate it.
- **State:** measurement only.

### AS-329 Parallel rows keep the row with the smaller index whatever the scales: a removed row of larger scale is violated by 1.56e-3
- **Assumption:** Of two parallel rows without a singleton column the one with the smaller index is kept, whatever the scales of the two. Whatever a later rule writes into the kept row at double precision (a side, or a value in the undo) reaches the removed row times the ratio of the two. A model of the validity reviewer: r0: s + y + z in [0.1, 5.3]; r1 = 262144 * r0; s continuous in [0, 9e7] without cost; y in [-1e8, 10], z in [0, 1]. The parallel rows remove r1 into r0, the zero-cost singleton rule [R3] then removes s and sets the lower side of r0 to the double -89999999.900000006 (the exact value is -89999999.9); a reduced point with violation 0 postsolves to a point that violates r1 by 1.563e-3. The base commit `74350acb3` gives the same 1.563e-3 without any column rule, through a doubleton substitution that shifts the sides of r0 by 9e7. With the larger row first, and so kept, nothing is wrong. A guard inside the zero-cost singleton rule cannot close it, because the undo arithmetic at values of 9e7 has the same rounding. The validity reviewer names it a decision of the owner and asks that the parallel rows be repaired before the Stage 1c gate. Measured with milestone M1: the two failing models and the one that passes; 300 million campaign models did not reach the class, because the generator draws no side beyond 1e8 next to a ratio of 2^18 (AS-330); no count was made on the 240 of merged pairs with a large ratio next to large sides.
- **Set by:** Code (the base engine: the Stage 1 parallel rows, "the one with the smaller index is kept" in the contract of the rule; found by the M1 validity reviewer). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner that this wrong result is not repaired and is not the milestone's, and that a repair (keep the row of larger scale) with its own attack was started beside the next milestone; the owner has not decided anything about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:299-303; gpu_presolve/src/rules_parallel_rows.cpp:795-797 and 324-357 (`merge`); the models are stage1c_reports/m1/probes/parallel_scale.cpp, parallel_scale_base.cpp and parallel_scale_swapped.cpp (not in the test suite, because the first two fail); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (fields "wrong_results" and "open"); HISTORY Entry 48.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (which row is kept); `options_t::enabled[rule::parallel_rows]` (tool `--off parallel_rows`) turns the whole family.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the row of the larger scale (the repair in work); refuse a pair whose ratio times the size of its terms is beyond the tolerance; carry a reach factor per row that later rules guard with.
- **May change:** A postsolved solution that violates a row of the original model by up to about 1e-3 on models with parallel rows of very different scale and sides or values near 1e8; with the repair, which of two parallel rows stays in the reduced model, and with it the scale of that row.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality pair: the two models as tests after the repair, a generator knob for the class, and a count on the 240 of merged pairs with a ratio above 1000. If the repair changes reduced models on the 240, a size run with the hashes and then the standard cluster comparison on the instances that differ; in every arm the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`, as at the base commit); a repair is in work (HISTORY Entry 48).

### AS-330 The campaign generator cannot draw four structures; nine wrong engines fail unit tests only
- **Assumption:** No generated model has a side beyond 1e8, a fixed term that swallows a side beside a column of the singleton-column rules, a ratio that is off an integer by less than 1e-9, or a chain of pivot ratios above 1000; and none has a side beyond 1e8 next to parallel rows with a ratio of 2^18 (AS-329). The guards and limits of the milestone that act only on such models are therefore tested by unit tests and fixed models alone (what the oracle itself can decide is AS-278). Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns of 1,440,000 models per wrong engine, on the Mac): 9 of the 99 wrong engines are caught by no campaign check: fs_guard, zs_guard and fc_guard (the guards on sides next to fixed terms, AS-076), fs_drift, r_alias_drift and fs_shared (ratios and sides that are integers only within the tolerance, AS-093, AS-322), fs_limited (a column that only the growth factor of earlier substitutions kept does not report limited, AS-318), ev_sides (the mark of AS-325) and ev_scale (AS-269). The validity reviewer looked at a generator knob for a side beyond 1e8 that becomes readable (a column fixed at about 1e9 with the sides of its rows shifted) and did not build it: the side draw clamps to `max_side` in several places. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (campaigns on the Mac): the generator also has no row with two singleton columns that both pass, no interval record with two columns, no side near 1e8 next to terms of that size and no bound within a few bands of what its row gives, so two of the 17 other option values and the three removed ones are not reached (AS-355); of the 20 wrong engines of the switches 9 fail unit tests only, which makes 18 of 119.
- **Set by:** Code (the generator as the M1 author extended it; the blind spots were named by the M1 validity reviewer). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner the nine wrong engines and what the generator lacks, and states as a lesson that every class a review finds by hand must become a generator knob, with a wrong engine that only that knob catches.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp:121-147 (the knobs that exist); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:238-248; gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:484-490 and 902-967 (the nine recipes, with the unit tests that catch them); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (fields "other_defects" and "open"); HISTORY Entry 48.
- **Switch:** fields of `generator_options_t` (gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp), raised by the campaign option `--columns`; no knob for the four structures: needs one each.
- **Alternatives:** Generator knobs for sides beyond 1e8 that become readable, for fixed terms that swallow a side, for ratios within 1e-9 of an integer, for chains of pivot ratios above 1000 and for parallel rows of very different scale; planted models.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; a wrong change of one of these guards in a later milestone passes every campaign and is seen only if a unit test covers it.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: with each knob built, the campaign must catch the wrong engine that only that knob reaches.
- **State:** measurement only (a blind spot; no knob is built).

### AS-331 The structure check sees a wrong undo of an interval or forcing record only at three or four test points
- **Assumption:** The postsolve part of the structure check compares its own undo with the engine's postsolve at a few points of the reduced model, with an extra point of its own for at most 8 interval records, and it asks the saved row of an interval record to hold only where the rest of the point lies inside the sides of the snapshot (AS-228). An error in the undo of an interval or a forcing record that shows only where a row other than the first, or a column other than the first, decides the value passes it on some results. Measured with milestone M1 (campaigns of 1,440,000 models, on the Mac): 21 results of the wrong engine zs_room and 35 of fc_first_row failed the oracle with an infeasible postsolved point and passed the structure check. On the 240 as local dumps no stored points were used and no solver ran (AS-336), so there the structure check is the only test of these undos (17,901 interval and 117 forcing records).
- **Set by:** Design for the form of the test (v2_amendments.txt D, validity review item 1: one extra point per interval record that needs a move; a wrong undo of the zero-cost rule is assigned to the crush check and the oracle); code (the M1 author) for the constants. The weakness was measured by the M1 validity reviewer. HISTORY Entry 48 does not name it.
- **Where:** stage1c_design/v2_amendments.txt:48-52; gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:139-143, :245 (`max_replay_points`), 1831-1841, :1876 and 1916-1981; gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:818 and :853 (`zs_room`, `fc_first_row`); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (fields "other_defects" and "open").
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one (named constant `max_replay_points` = 8; the test points are fixed in the check).
- **Alternatives:** Test points per forcing and per interval record at which a row other than the first, or a column other than the first, decides the value (the reviewer's proposal); stored points for the 56 instances without one (AS-276).
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; a wrong undo of these two record kinds can pass the checks on a real instance without a usable point.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the two wrong engines must fail the structure check on every result on which they fail the oracle; in every cluster arm the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check.
- **State:** measurement only (a blind spot of the check at commit `fa8430e24`).

### AS-332 Without a solver the check tool passes 7 of 22 wrong engines of the singleton rules on the 17 local dumps
- **Assumption:** On real instances the milestone was checked by the check tool without a solver (LP and MIP time limits of 0), so the verdicts that compare optima are skipped (176 on the 17 local dumps, 4,320 on the 240); the other verdicts pass with the right engine, 490 on the 17 and 5,040 on the 240, and none fails. In that form the tool does not see a restriction that cuts no stored or pushed point (AS-275), nor an error on a path that none of the instances takes (AS-226). Measured with milestone M1 (the 17 local dumps on the Mac, default and primal-only mode, 2 cost variants, 4 pushed points): of the 14 wrong engines of the author 12 fail a verdict (three of them only through the cost variants) and two, fs_side and zs_side, pass all; of 8 wrong engines of the validity reviewer 3 are caught (fs_not_dual on brazil3, zs_one_side on unitcal_7, fc_first_row through the cost variants on 4 instances) and 5 pass every verdict (fs_fixed, fs_ahead, fs_any_side, zs_infinite, zs_room). In the campaign the oracle catches all seven. Whether the verdicts with a solver catch them on real instances was not tested.
- **Set by:** Design (a blind spot found with the wrong engines of the M1 author and of the M1 validity reviewer, and accepted for now; that the real instances of the milestone were checked without a solver follows from where the milestone ran, on the Mac: the check with its solver runs on the node, AS-178, AS-223, AS-336). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner that nothing ran with a solver, not these counts.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py (flags `--lp-time-limit`, `--mip-time-limit`); gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:596, :622, :685, :750, :789, :802 and :818 (the seven recipes, each with the check that its comment names); stage1c_reports/m1/impl_m1.json (fields "evidence" and "unsure") and attack_validity.json (field "other_defects").
- **Switch:** `--lp-time-limit` and `--mip-time-limit` of gpu_presolve/check/run_check.py (0 switches the solver verdicts off); `--cost-variants`, `--push-points`.
- **Alternatives:** The check with the solver on the node (AS-223); more pushed points; a support test per tightened bound (AS-275).
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; a restriction by a singleton-column rule on a real instance is seen only by the solver verdicts, on the instances where HiGHS proves the reduced optimum, or by the end test.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the seven wrong engines under the check with a solver on the node; the end test as AS-275 describes it.
- **State:** measurement only (a blind spot).

### AS-333 A campaign model of profile open_ends (seed 403349198) fails the oracle at the base commit and is untriaged
- **Assumption:** One of 302 million additional release models of the author's campaign fails the oracle: profile open_ends, seed 403349198, option variant 8, no profile option: a feasible point with two columns at -3e19 has objective -1, its crushed point 9. The base commit `74350acb3`, the first version of the milestone and the final commit fail it with the same message, the engine with the singleton family off as well, and the family has no finding on it. It is not of the known 1.1e-9 class (AS-259): the difference is 10. The author's reading, from the arithmetic of the printed model and not from a trace of the engine: the objective offset as one double cannot hold -10 next to -9e19. Measured with milestone M1: that one model, confirmed by the validity reviewer on the three engines. Measured with the node campaign of 5.4 billion models (engine commit `daa4f5d68`; HISTORY Entry 52): 11 of the 18 oracle failures are of this kind: a feasible point of the model with entries of 3e19 to 1e23 whose crushed point has a higher objective. Release build, no profile option: huge_parallel 1000074524938. Release build, `--singletons`: equations 1000118664898, huge_equations 1000149468558, open_ends 1000110929538, and huge_parallel 1000130321033 (option variant 3, primal-only mode; the two objectives differ by 2.5e-9 only). Release build, `--columns`: huge_parallel 1000209869848 and 1000242124018. Assertion build, no profile option: huge_parallel 1000342975398. Assertion build, `--singletons`: open_ends 1000407955258, and seed 1000409012258 in the two profiles huge_parallel and huge_equations. That is one in about 490 million models; the 302 million models of the Mac had found one. Ten of the eleven have option variant 8 (tiny budgets), as seed 403349198 has. The structure check failed on none and missed all of them (it compares no optimal values and tests no points with huge entries; by the campaign's own count none of them is a relaxation, AS-259); no assertion fired. These are failures at points that a solver would not visit; the one wrong answer of the campaign at an ordinary point is counted apart (AS-358). The seeds are with the triage; the reading of the failure lines is the assistant's, not yet the verdict of the triage. With the engine of the switches of milestone M1 at the defaults seed 403349198 fails as before (objective -1 against 9).
- **Set by:** Code (the base engine; found by the campaign of the M1 author and not repaired, because it is not the milestone's code). HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner that a triage of the known failing campaign seeds, this one among them, was started beside the next milestone; it has no result yet. HISTORY Entry 52 records the counts of the node campaign by class as the assistant's reading; it has no message of the owner.
- **Where:** stage1c_reports/m1/impl_m1.json (fields "evidence" and "unsure") and attack_validity.json (fields "other_defects" and "open"); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:290-294 (option variant 8: tiny budgets); HISTORY Entry 48. gpu_presolve/results/stage1c/campaign/s1c_m1_big1 (log.txt, the totals `<run>.txt`, the lines `<run>.failures.txt` and the part files with the printed models); HISTORY Entry 52.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: the cause is not known; the model is the first one of gprs_fuzz `403349198 1 --profile open_ends`.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the objective offset as a compensated sum; refuse a fixing or a substitution whose offset term is beyond a size.
- **May change:** An objective value of the reduced model that is off by an absolute amount on models with columns fixed or moved at values near 1e19; none of the 240 is known to have such values.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the triage, a fixed model for it, then the campaign; in every cluster arm the objective of each postsolved solution recomputed in the original model.
- **State:** in the engine (base commit `74350acb3`, commit `fa8430e24` and commit `f05d48e31`); 12 models of the kind are known, one from the Mac's campaigns and 11 from the node campaign; with the triage (HISTORY Entry 52), no verdict yet.

### AS-334 A row can be scanned twice in one call of the singleton family
- **Assumption:** A column that an early reduction of a pass lists while it is still waiting in the list of that pass is served in that pass and again in the next one, so its row is scanned a second time in the same call although nothing has changed in between (the stamp of a row holds for one pass over the lists only). Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac; a scratch counter of the worth reviewer): 9,120 of 452,152 row scans and 194,277 of 10,497,776 scanned entries (1.9%) are such repeats, 0.15% of the work units of the family; 179,588 of the entries are on mushroom-best (8,124 rows, 30% of the family's work there). A certain repair exists (skip the scan when the call has made no reduction since the last scan of the row in this call); it changes work units and the per-visit statistic `free_singleton_rejected_explicit`, and no result. It was not made, because the gain is too small to touch a reviewed commit for. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the repeats are now counters of the engine (`rows_rescanned`, `rescanned_slots`) and give the same 9,120 rows and 194,277 slots, 179,588 of them on mushroom-best.
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author; found and left by the M1 worth reviewer). HISTORY Entry 48 does not name it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:39-41 and 403-404 (the stamp), 556-566 (the passes); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_worth.json (field "other_defects"). At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:67-70 and 584-588; gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:81-86.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if the repair is to be compared.
- **Alternatives:** Skip the repeated scan.
- **May change:** Presolve time by a part of the family's share of 1.4%; nothing in the reduced model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: work units of the family before and after the repair, with identical hashes on the 240.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`); not repaired.

### AS-335 The singleton family costs time without a finding on thor50dday and neos-873061
- **Assumption:** On two instances every row holds an integer singleton column that fails the implied-bound test or the integrality test, and every row is listed again for the second call; the family scans them all again and finds nothing. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, timing run of the worth reviewer): the family takes 26% of the presolve time of thor50dday (6.9 ms of 26.4 ms, 106,260 row scans in two calls) and 16% of neos-873061 (4.8 ms of 29.7 ms, 87,800 scans). In the sum over the 240 it takes 1.4% of the engine time; its largest shares besides these are on savsched1 (8.2%, 8.0 ms) and supportcase12 (5.5%, 7.6 ms), where it has findings. Not timed on the node or on the 40. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (on the Mac, release build, 16 repetitions): with the counters of the engine (AS-347) the family takes 8.7 ms on thor50dday against 7.1 ms with the engine before; two copies of the engine before differ by 0.4% there.
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author; measured by the M1 worth reviewer). HISTORY Entry 48 gives the owner the cost in the sum (+2.3% engine time, the family itself 1.4%), not these two instances.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:271-282 (the two tests) and 400-417; stage1c_reports/m1/attack_worth.json (fields "other_defects" and "timing").
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one only if a memory of refused rows is built; `options_t::enabled[rule::singleton_cols]` (tool `--off singleton_cols`) turns the family, and `options_t::budget[rule::singleton_cols]` bounds a call.
- **Alternatives:** Remember per row that its singleton columns were refused by a test that only an event of the row can turn, and skip the scan until then; a type test before the scan.
- **May change:** Presolve time on instances whose rows hold integer slack columns; nothing in the reduced model.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: timing on the node at the next milestone; the primal integral is the quality measure that sees presolve time, and 7 ms cannot move it.
- **State:** in the engine (commit `fa8430e24`); not repaired.

### AS-336 The singleton-column milestone was merged on results of the Mac only: no node, no solver, no testbed
- **Assumption:** The milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] was built, reviewed, merged into `feat/gpu-presolve` and taken as the base of the next milestone on what ran on the Mac: 473 unit tests in both builds, the campaigns, the wrong engines, the identity with the engine before, sizes, work and time on the 17 local dumps and on the 240 as local dumps, and the check tool without a solver. The node reservation ended at its time limit while the milestone ran. Measured with milestone M1: every figure of the milestone in this register is from the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, from the 17 local dumps or from generated models.
- **Set by:** Taken as recommended (the assistant merged and pushed the milestone and started the next one on it; HISTORY Entry 48 tells the owner what was not done because the node was gone; the owner has not answered). It repeats the step of AS-269, where the first rule milestone was started before the node had confirmed the groundwork.
- **Where:** HISTORY Entry 48 ("What I did"); gpu_presolve/tools/stage1c_engine_runs.sh (the chain that was not run); gpu_presolve/results/bench240/s1c_m1_mac (the stored run on the Mac); stage1c_reports/m1/attack_validity.json (field "open") and attack_worth.json (field "open").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the chain is gpu_presolve/tools/stage1c_engine_runs.sh with the tag `s1c_m1`, on the node.
- **Alternatives:** Hold the next milestone until the node has confirmed this one.
- **May change:** A defect or a slowdown that shows only on the testbed instances outside the 240 (neos-4332810-sesia and dlr2 carry the testbed distance, AS-239; the 240 hold two instances above 1e7 nonzeros, the 40 nine), with the stored points or under the check with a solver stays unseen while later milestones are built on this engine.
