Hash, sizes and fixed-point flag equal the Mac's on 240 of 240; the 40 testbed instances are at 1.0028 / 1.0009 / 1.0032 of `tb_m1_a` with nothing below 0.95; the assertion build, the structure and crush check and the check with a solver are clean (hawaiiv10-130 has no usable stored point); time against the engine before is +1.78% on the 240 and +7.28% on the 9 instances above 1e7 nonzeros. The campaign of 5.4 billion models on the node (`gpu_presolve/results/stage1c/campaign/s1c_m1_big1`, commit `255e91ff2`) has 18 oracle failures, none of them in the rules of this milestone as far as traced; one is a lost optimum that depends on a bound of -3e19 (open, with the triage).

### AS-338 The singleton-column milestone built choices without a switch; its measurement switches and counters exist in scratch builds only
- **Assumption:** The rule of this register is that a choice which cannot be switched gets a switch when it is built ("How an entry is added"). The milestone of the singleton-column rules and the forcing column [M1] did not follow it. Built without a switch: the order inside a visit and the smallest index (AS-116, AS-127); the zero-cost singleton rule only for continuous columns, only on rows with two finite sides and only when another free entry stays (AS-028, AS-029); what the growth ratio reads and how the factor is carried (AS-318); explicit bounds only in the implied-bound test (AS-022); the test of the bound behind the move (AS-317); integer pivot columns of the inequality pivot (AS-339); the start value and the order of the interval undo (AS-057); the rounding band of an integer forcing column (AS-319); and the membership of the zero-cost singleton rule and the forcing column in primal-only mode, which is fixed in `sub_rule_active` (AS-004). The M1 author gives the reason for those it lists: a switch for these "needs a second code path that the checks would have to cover". The worth reviewer measured or counted most of them in an instrumented copy of the sources: switches read from the environment (the growth ratio over continuous other columns only, integer pivot columns exempt from the limit, the implied tolerance scaled, the side guards scaled or off, the family as the first phase after the cascade, the two rules on in primal-only mode), counters of every refusal of the family by its cause, a second presolve that classifies the singleton columns that are left, and a counter of repeated scans. None of it is in the repository, and neither are the other tools of the reviews: the tool that reads the quality of a reduced model (cost range, coefficient ranges, sides, equations), the local build of the instrumented HiGHS that counts pivot kinds, the tool in exact rational arithmetic of the validity reviewer, the identity tool and the timing script with the turning order. So the figures that rest on them (in AS-004, AS-022, AS-023, AS-025, AS-027, AS-028, AS-029, AS-076, AS-080, AS-088, AS-096, AS-114, AS-116, AS-204, AS-205, AS-266, AS-278, AS-317, AS-318, AS-323, AS-324, AS-328, AS-334 and AS-335) cannot be repeated from the repository, on the node, on the 40 or on a later engine version, and no check has covered the second code paths. Measured with milestone M1 (the 240 as local dumps on the Mac): the instrumented build gives the hashes of the plain build on all 240, and 12 runs over the 240 were made with it. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (commit `f05d48e31`, HISTORY Entry 54; the 240 as local dumps on the Mac, presolve only, not run on the node): the rule is now followed for every choice named above whose other value is right. Closed, each with an engine option whose default is the rule as it was: the order inside a visit, the smallest index and the chains (`singleton_order`; AS-116, AS-127, AS-345); the zero-cost singleton rule on rows with one finite side and on rows made only of such columns (`zero_cost_one_sided`, `zero_cost_whole_row`; AS-028); what the growth ratio reads (`growth_ratio`; AS-318, AS-344); integer pivot columns of the inequality pivot (`dual_integer_pivots`; AS-339); the start value and the order of the interval undo (`interval_start`, `interval_order`; AS-057); the rounding band of an integer forcing column (`forcing_rounding_units`, 0 to 8; AS-319, AS-340); the two rules in primal-only mode (`primal_projections`; AS-004, AS-346); and two that the worth reviewer had measured in the scratch build: the side guards scaled (`side_guard_scale`; AS-076, AS-343) and the family as the first phase after the cascade (`singleton_cols_early`; AS-114). That is 11 options with 17 other values that the campaign draws (AS-348). Also closed: the refusal counters are statistics of the engine (47 counters; AS-347), the second presolve that classifies what is left is the tool flag `--left`, and the quality tool (gprs_quality with analysis/quality_compare.py), the identity tool (gprs_ident with run_ident.sh; AS-349), the timing script with the turning order (time_multi.py) and the scripts of the table (switch_table.py, compare_runs.py, reduce_summary.py; AS-350) are in the repository. Every figure of the worth report that the two agents compared reproduces from them but two: 10 reduced models, not 12, differ with the family as the first phase (AS-114), and the lead on roi5alpha10n8 does not come from integer pivot columns (AS-339). Remaining without a switch, each with its reason: integer columns in the zero-cost singleton rule (not valid as the rule stands, the valid form is a new mechanism; AS-029, AS-352); implied bounds read from other rows (a new mechanism; AS-022, AS-023); the bound ahead only (not valid; AS-317); the band of the implied-bound test (a wider band is not valid, a narrower one breaks the fixed point; AS-088, AS-341); the side guards off (not valid; AS-076); and how the growth factor is carried (a sum, a reset per call, a limit for columns without a bound: not built; AS-318). Still not in the repository: the frames in exact rational arithmetic of the M1 validity reviewer and of the reviewer of the switches (AS-355) and the scripts of the HiGHS runs that count pivot kinds; they are with the notes (stage1c_reports/m1/review_tools). The adapter passes none of the 11 options (AS-152). At the defaults the engine with the switches is the engine before on 240 of 240, in default mode, with the family off and in primal-only mode (hash, sizes, fixed-point flag and every statistic).
- **Set by:** Code (the M1 author, who built the choices without a switch and lists them as not done; the M1 worth reviewer, who measured them with scratch switches of which "none is built into the commit"). The merge in this state is taken as recommended (the assistant, HISTORY Entry 48). Entry 48 tells the owner that the figure of AS-004 comes from a scratch switch of the worth reviewer; it does not say that these choices have no switch. The switches were built on 2026-11-28 by an implementer and an adversarial reviewer (code; stage1c_reports/m1_switches); taking the reviewed commit into the project branch on results of the Mac alone is taken as recommended (AS-357). HISTORY Entry 54 records the switches and gives a table for the owner's decisions; that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has decided none of it.
- **Where:** stage1c_reports/m1/impl_m1.json (field "not_done") and attack_worth.json (fields "other_defects", "evidence" and "open"); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:175-179 (`sub_rule_active`); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_presolve.cpp:171-207 (the options that the tool has). The scratch directories are named in the field "evidence" of the three reports and are not part of the worktree. At commit `f05d48e31`: gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:72-126 (the four enums), 225-318 (the 11 options), 321-331 (`choices_in_range`) and 333-344 (`sub_rule_active`); gpu_presolve/include/gprs/rules.hpp:65-163 (the counters); gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp:10-23 (the flags); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_presolve.cpp:8-28; stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json and attack_switches.json.
- **Switch:** Since commit `f05d48e31` 11 options of `options_t`, each with its default first: `dual_integer_pivots` (true, false; `--no-dual-integer-pivots`), `primal_projections` (false, true; `--primal-projections`, with `--primal-only`), `growth_ratio` (`all_columns`, `continuous_columns`, `integer_pivot_exempt`; `--growth-ratio <all|continuous|integer-exempt>`), `zero_cost_one_sided` (false, true; `--zero-cost-one-sided`), `zero_cost_whole_row` (false, true; `--zero-cost-whole-row`), `singleton_order` (`smallest_index`, `largest_pivot`, `zero_cost_first`, `single_pass`; `--singleton-order <index|largest-pivot|zero-cost-first|single-pass>`), `interval_start` (`lower`, `upper`, `nearest_zero`, `middle`; `--interval-start <lower|upper|nearest-zero|middle>`), `interval_order` (`ascending`, `descending`; `--interval-order <ascending|descending>`), `forcing_rounding_units` (8; 0 to 8; `--forcing-rounding-units <x>`), `side_guard_scale` (1; 1e-3 to 11 with the default tolerances; `--side-guard-scale <x>`) and `singleton_cols_early` (false, true; `--singleton-cols-early`). The flags are those of gprs_presolve, gprs_quality and gprs_check (gpu_presolve/tools/option_flags.hpp). No switch, not built: the six choices named as remaining. What the family had before: `options_t::sub_enabled[sub_rule::free_singleton, zero_cost_singleton, forcing_column, free_singleton_dual]` (all on by default; tool `--off <name>`), `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` (default 1000; tool `--pivot-growth-limit <x>`), `options_t::saved_row_factor` (default 4; tool `--saved-row-factor <x>`) and `options_t::tol.integral_drift_share` (default 0.1; tool `--integral-drift-share <x>`).
- **Alternatives:** Build each measurement switch as an option of the engine, covered by unit tests, wrong engines and the option draw of the campaign; make the refusal counters statistics of the engine (`column_stats_t`); keep the measurement tools in the repository.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model. Without the switches the end test has no arm for these choices (an arm is a benchmark commit that changes one default, AS-152), and the figures of the milestone cannot be checked again.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: for each choice a switch in the engine, then the size run on the 240 with the hash of the reduced model at both values that its own entry asks for; the counters as statistics of the engine, repeated on the node on the 240 and the 40.
- **State:** measurement only; closed in part on 2026-11-28 (commit `f05d48e31`, HISTORY Entry 54): 11 options, the counters and the tools are in the repository, verified on the Mac only (AS-357); open for the six choices that have no switch.

### AS-353 The implied-bound band times the growth limit: at the defaults a restored value passes its bound by 1.5e-4 at an exactly feasible reduced point
- **Assumption:** A wrong result of the engine at the default options, shown by the reviewer of the switches and not repaired. A free singleton column leaves when its row implies its bounds within the band of the implied-bound test (AS-088, about 2e-7), so its restored value can pass its bound by the band. A column that is restored after it, from a row that holds it, passes its own bound by the band times the ratio of the two coefficients, and the growth limit (AS-094) allows ratios up to 1000. The band is no deviation of the reduced point, so this happens at a reduced point that is exactly feasible. The model: x + 1000 y + w = 500 and y + 0.5 z + 0.5 v = 1; x continuous in [-501, 499.99985], y continuous in [1.5e-7, 1], w continuous in [0, 1] with cost 1, z and v binary with cost -1. The lower bound of y is within the band of what the second row gives, x and y both leave as free singleton columns, the reduced model is empty, and its only point, which is exactly feasible, postsolves to x = 500, which is 1.5e-4 above the upper bound of x. The engine before the switches (commit `fa8430e24`) and the engine with them give the same, and none of the three forms of the growth ratio changes it (AS-318, AS-344). It is the stacking that the M1 validity review lists as remaining by design (AS-088, AS-094), stated there for a reduced point that uses the full tolerance; it is none of the known classes of campaign failures. No check of the project sees it: the structure check is silent on the model, and the campaign generator has no bound within a few bands of what its row gives (AS-355). The feasibility tolerance of the engine is 1e-6; cuOpt tests a postsolved solution against the original model and gives a warning only (AS-168). The contract of the engine now names the chain and its bound (at most 2e-4 with the band of the rule). Measured with the switches of milestone M1: that one model, with the binary of commit `fa8430e24` and with the final binary. How often a bound lies within the band next to a later ratio above 5 on the 240 was not counted, and no postsolved point of the 240 was tested for it.
- **Set by:** Code (the base engine at its defaults: the band of AS-088 and the growth limit of AS-094, each set by the design; that the two multiply at an exactly feasible reduced point was found by the reviewer of the switches, who left it as it is because a repair changes results at the defaults). HISTORY Entry 54 records the defect with the model, that no check of the project sees it and that it is not repaired; that entry has no message of the owner, and the owner has not decided.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/include/gprs/presolve.hpp:283-293 (the contract names the chain and its bound) and 90-92; gpu_presolve/src/rules_singleton_cols.cpp:207-232 (`implied_free`) and 365-387 (the growth test); gpu_presolve/src/implied.hpp:32-40 (`implied_tolerance`); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results", item 4, "switches", row 3, and "open"). The model is in the report only: it is not a test of the repository, because it fails.
- **Switch:** no switch yet: needs one for a repair (the reviewer names the band divided by the growth factor of the column). What exists: `options_t::pivot_growth_limit` (default 1000; tool `--pivot-growth-limit <x>`) bounds the ratio and with it the excess, which is the band of about 2e-7 times the limit; the band itself has no option (AS-088, AS-341).
- **Alternatives:** The band divided by the growth factor of the column; a lower growth limit (the excess is about 1e-6 at a limit of 5; AS-094 has the sizes at the limits 1, 100 and 500); rounding only in the implied-bound test, with fresh sums; a clamp of the restored value in the undo; leave it and count the postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check.
- **May change:** A postsolved solution that violates a bound of the original model by up to about 2e-4 at the default limit, on models where a bound of a free singleton column lies within 2e-7 of what its row implies and a later substitution has a ratio of up to 1000. A repair changes which singleton columns are substituted at the defaults, and so the reduced models.
- **Test at the end:** An owner's decision first (repair or leave). Before that not a quality pair: a count on the 240 of substitutions whose bound is implied only within the band and whose column is read by a later substitution with a ratio above 5; a generator knob for a bound within a few bands of what its row gives next to a ratio near 1000, with a wrong engine that only it catches; the model as a fixed test after a repair. If a repair changes reduced models on the 240, a size run with the hashes and then the standard cluster comparison on the instances that differ; in every arm the count of postsolved solutions that fail the feasibility check.
- **State:** in the engine (commits `fa8430e24` and `f05d48e31`, at the defaults); not repaired; repair or leave is for the owner, who has not decided (HISTORY Entry 54 has no message of the owner).

### AS-354 The structure check follows the forcing band of the stack and cannot see a band that is too wide
- **Assumption:** The structure check replays the undo of an integer forcing column with the band that the stack carries (`postsolve_t::forcing_rounding_units`). With a band other than 8 units the engine's undo and the check's own, which sum in another order, can give neighbouring integers; the value of postsolve() then stands if the sum lies within the band of the integer between them, and the rows and the bounds are tested with it. At the default band the two must agree, as before. So the check accepts what the band of the stack allows and cannot say that a band is too wide: on the model of AS-340 it is silent at 64 and at 90 units, where a point check (`check_point`) rejects the postsolved point. Only a point check can see it: the oracle of the campaign, the check of a postsolved point, the crush check with points. The implementer's statement that the structure check and the crush check pass for all 31 option settings was true and covered two settings that give wrong results. The wrong engine with a band in thousandths (sw_band_units) was documented as caught by the oracle of the campaign; with the value 64 gone from the draw the oracle no longer sees it in 1,440,000 models, while the structure check does (5 and 7 results of 720,000 models without and with `--switches`) and two unit tests do. Measured with the switches of milestone M1: the model at 64 and at 90 units; in the campaign with 0 units pinned on every seed (5,040,000 models, 68 results changed) no structure failure.
- **Set by:** Code (the implementer of the switches wrote the check's rule for a band other than 8; the weakness was shown by the reviewer). HISTORY Entry 54 records it in one line (silent at 64 and 90 units). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/check/structure_check.hpp:138-154 (the contract), :260 (`forcing_rounding`) and 1833-1867 (the replay of a forcing record); gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:1097-1103 (sw_band_units); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "other_defects", items 4 and 7, and "assumptions") and impl_switches.json (field "assumptions").
- **Switch:** no switch of its own: the check follows `postsolve_t::forcing_rounding_units`, which presolve() writes from `options_t::forcing_rounding_units` (0 to 8).
- **Alternatives:** The check tests the saved row at the engine's value with the feasibility tolerance (the reviewer's proposal); the same summation order in the engine and in the check.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model. While the range of the option is 0 to 8 the blind spot covers no option value; a later change that widens the band, or writes another band into a stack, passes the structure check and is seen only by a point check.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: a wrong engine that writes a band of 64 units into the stack must fail the structure check, once the check tests the saved row at the engine's value; until then the fixed test of AS-340.
- **State:** measurement only (a blind spot of the check at commit `f05d48e31`).

### AS-355 Option values that the campaign generator never reaches rest on unit tests, the dump checks and an exact enumeration outside the repository
- **Assumption:** Two of the 17 other values of the options never change a result in the campaign: `singleton_order` = `largest_pivot` and `interval_order` = `descending` (0 results changed in 5,040,000 models each with the value pinned on every seed). Generated models have no row with two singleton columns that both pass and no interval record with two columns. The three removed values were not reached either: in the reviewer's campaign on the implementer's commit 2,728,464 release models ran with `forcing_rounding_units` 64 alone (1 result changed) and 5,461,728 with a band factor of 2 or 5 alone (0 changed), without a failure, although each value gives a wrong result on a hand-made model (AS-340, AS-341); the generator has no side near 1e8 next to terms of that size and no bound within a few bands of what its row gives. So the campaign did not find the wrong option values; a reviewer with hand-made models did. The two values that stay rest on unit tests, on the checks on the dumps and on the reviewer's enumeration in exact rational arithmetic (tiny models with up to three continuous singleton columns per row, rows made of singleton columns, one-sided rows and ratios of 2048, in 40 to 44 option sets): there `largest_pivot` changes the reduced model on 31 of 1,984 models, and `descending` and `middle` change the undo on 87 and 92; 41,555 models show no failure of the engine. That frame and its generator are in the reviewer's scratch directory and not in the repository. The exact solver that it reuses gives an integer column without bounds a window of 14 values; one model needs 15, which printed 25 false failures, and such models are now skipped as ambiguous. A generator knob for two continuous singleton columns in one row would need the exact solver of the campaign to take two line columns per row; the implementer did not build it. Of the 20 wrong engines of the switches 9 are caught by unit tests only (sw_dual_integer, sw_early, sw_growth_continuous, sw_integer_exempt, sw_largest_pivot, sw_descending_coefficient, sw_one_sided_off, sw_single_any_pass, sw_stack_choices); with the 9 of AS-330 that is 18 of 119. Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (campaigns and enumeration on the Mac): the figures above.
- **Set by:** Code (the generator as it is, AS-330; the blind spots were named by the implementer of the switches and by the reviewer). HISTORY Entry 54 states it as a lesson: the two wrong values were drawn 8 million times without a failure, because the generator cannot reach the models that break them. The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:321-354 (the table of the drawn values) and 448-459 (`--pin`); gpu_presolve/tests/oracle.hpp (the knobs of the generator); gpu_presolve/check/make_mutants.py:983-1180 (the 20 wrong engines of the switches); stage1c_reports/m1_switches/attack_switches.json (fields "evidence", "other_defects", item 5, "wrong_results", item 6, and "open") and impl_switches.json (field "not_done").
- **Switch:** no knob yet: needs one each for a row with two continuous singleton columns that both pass, for an interval record with two columns, for a side near 1e8 next to terms of that size, and for a bound within a few bands of what its row gives. The campaign flags that exist are `--switches` and `--pin <value>` (AS-348).
- **Alternatives:** The four knobs (the first needs the exact solver of the campaign to take two line columns per row); the reviewer's exact frame in the repository as a second campaign; planted models.
- **May change:** Nothing in the model; a wrong change of one of these paths in a later milestone passes every campaign and is seen only if a unit test covers it.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: with each knob built, the campaign with the value pinned must change results, and a wrong engine that only that knob reaches must be caught.
- **State:** measurement only (a blind spot; no knob is built).

### AS-356 One oracle failure of the switches' campaign could not be reproduced (seed 5100094942)
- **Assumption:** The implementer's campaign with the option drawing reported one oracle failure: release build, `--columns --switches`, profile scaled, seed 5100094942, option variant 2, with `zero_cost_whole_row` drawn alone: a postsolved point with a bound violation of 2. It did not happen again. The implementer ran the same part of 5,040,000 models four more times with the same binary (0 failures, four byte-identical outputs that equal the failing run in every count but that one), the whole run of 10,080,000 models once more, and the seed alone in both builds; on that model the option changes nothing, and the point that the message prints is feasible for the model that it prints. The reviewer ran the part four more times with the implementer's source: the release build as it is, the same with freshly allocated and freed heap memory scribbled, and two builds with every uninitialised local variable set to zero and to a pattern. All four outputs are byte-identical and clean, so an uninitialised read of heap or stack does not explain it on that part. The implementer thinks that it was a fault of the machine during that check (a value of 2 where the true value is 0 is one flipped bit of a double) and cannot prove it; the reviewer cannot say what did it. It is none of the known classes of campaign failures (AS-259, AS-333). Measured with the switches of milestone M1 (campaigns on the Mac): 1 failure in the 45,360,000 models of the implementer's campaign with the drawing; 0 in the 90,720,000 models of the reviewer's campaign on the same commit, and 0 in 108,360,000 models on the final engine (22,680,000 with the drawing, 85,680,000 with a value pinned); the undefined-behaviour sanitizer reports nothing on 3,672,000 and 900,000 campaign models with `--switches`. If it is a defect of the engine, it is rarer than one in 45 million models.
- **Set by:** Code (found by the campaign of the implementer of the switches; explained neither by the implementer nor by the reviewer). HISTORY Entry 54 records it in one line; it is in both reports (field "unsure" of the implementer, fields "wrong_results" and "open" of the reviewer). The owner has decided nothing about it.
- **Where:** stage1c_reports/m1_switches/impl_switches.json (fields "evidence" and "unsure") and attack_switches.json (fields "wrong_results", item 5, and "open"); gpu_presolve/tools/gprs_fuzz.cpp:262-319 (the option variants; variant 2 keeps the defaults) and 321-354 (the drawn values). The outputs of the failing run and of the reruns are in the scratch directories of the two agents and not in the repository.
- **Switch:** no switch: the cause is not known. The model is that of gprs_fuzz with the profile scaled, the seed 5100094942 and `--columns`. With `--switches` the seed draws other values at commit `f05d48e31` than at the implementer's commit `55b639503` (AS-348); the options of the failing run are reached there with `--pin zero_cost_whole_row`.
- **Alternatives:** A campaign that runs a failing seed again at once in the same process and reports whether the failure repeats; the part under the address sanitizer on the node (it hangs at start on the Mac).
- **May change:** Nothing known. If it is a defect of the engine, a postsolved point with a bound violation on a model of the profile scaled with `zero_cost_whole_row`, rarer than one in 45 million models; if it was a fault of the machine, nothing.
- **Test at the end:** Not a quality test: the campaign of the switches on the node, with `--switches` and with `--pin zero_cost_whole_row` over the seed range of the failing part; a second occurrence makes it a defect with a triage of its own.
- **State:** open; not reproduced (on the Mac, commits `55b639503` and `f05d48e31`).

### AS-357 The switches of milestone M1 are verified on the Mac only: no node, no solver, no testbed
- **Assumption:** The options, counters and tools of the switches (commit `f05d48e31`, cherry-picked onto `feat/gpu-presolve` as `5611b07df` with the same engine sources) were built, reviewed and taken into the project branch on what ran on the Mac: 502 unit tests in both builds; the identity with the engine before at the defaults (the 240 as local dumps in three stored modes, the 17 local dumps, 8,064,000 random models per build with the record stack hashed field by field, 18,000,000 traced campaign models, the four known seeds); the campaigns with the option drawing and with each value pinned; 41,555 tiny models in exact rational arithmetic; the wrong engines; the check tool without a solver on the 17 local dumps and on the 240 (time limits of 0: 352 verdicts skipped per run on the 17, 4,800 on the 240); the sizes of every option value on the 240 as local dumps; the time on the 240. Not run: anything on the node or on the 40 testbed instances (both reports also name the two instances above 1e7 nonzeros); the check with a solver; any measurement of solution quality or of postsolved feasibility with solutions; the address sanitizer (it hangs at start on the Mac; the undefined-behaviour sanitizer ran clean on the tests and on campaign samples); and, by inference only (neither report names it: cuOpt and the device code are built on the node, where nothing ran), the build of the cuOpt adapter and of the device code against this engine. The assistant rebuilt the commit from a clean export on the Mac (HISTORY Entry 54): 502 tests pass in both builds, the defaults equal the engine before on 240 of 240 in default and in primal-only mode, and three option values reproduce the stored runs on 240 of 240; a campaign slice of the assistant with the option drawing (21.6 million release and 9.0 million assertion models over the three kinds) has no failure (HISTORY Entry 54).
